BTNYC — Project Charter

The Constitution of the product: the outcome we serve, the laws that protect it, and the engineering discipline that keeps those laws honest.

Document scope: this Project Charter is the durable Constitution of the BTNYC codebase. “The Charter” is its legacy title in historical material; the binding direction and Rules are stated here, while timelines and implementation artifacts retain their separate evidentiary roles.

ChaptersI The Client ExperienceII The DomainIII ComplexityIV Confidence & UncertaintyV Friction & Uncertainty RetirementVI IntakeVII No White FlagVIII Runtime & ArchitectureIX Pricing & Booking OutcomeX Verification & MeasurementXI Rule, Principle & Evidence Index
How to read Rules that describe the target architecture. A target contract states the intended steady state; it does not claim the migration is already complete. Open “Rule health & evolvability” beneath any Rule or Principle for its protected outcome, harm, implementation freedom, reconsideration trigger, and evidence mechanism. Historical Battles are linked for context but are recorded only in Part XI.

How to Read This Charter

Read the Charter as one argument, not as a checklist of disconnected prohibitions. Begin with the Vision and the client outcome; follow the domain model into complexity, uncertainty, evidence, and pricing; then read the architectural boundaries that make that outcome trustworthy. Rules state binding boundaries and required behavior. Principles state the direction those boundaries must serve. Governance explains how to detect drift, learn from failure, and change a system shape when the shape—not merely a local implementation—is the cause.

  1. Understand the promise. What a client should be able to accomplish, and what makes a quote honest.
  2. Understand the model. How the job is represented, what evidence is needed, and how precision is earned.
  3. Understand the boundaries. Which component owns each decision, how data and state flow, and why presentation must not re-decide business truth.
  4. Understand how the system evolves. Rules, verification, defect classes, and historical Battles explain how the architecture stays aligned without freezing its implementation.

When a rule seems to obstruct the Vision: do not quietly bypass it, and do not preserve a broken design merely because a rule describes it. Identify the conflicting invariant or bad system shape, trace the effect to its owning layer, and use the Charter's change-governance path to repair the root cause. The intended endpoint is a better shape that removes the workaround—not a permanent exception around it.

Search rules, principles, governance processes, glossary terms, footnotes, and historical Battles; open a quick view or jump to the full passage.

Open the Rule, Principle & Evidence Index

What BTNYC Is

BTNYC is a production home-service quoting platform. Its central question is not "how do we list every service?" — it's:

How do we turn an arbitrary client description into the smallest amount of reliable scope necessary to produce a transparent, accurate, bookable price?

Most platforms solve this one of two ways: catalogue the universe (thousands of SKUs, each requiring its own pricing, intake chain, and maintenance burden), or give up and route everything to "Call us." BTNYC does neither. It models the business — the small, finite set of concepts, behaviors, and conditions that actually determine what work costs — not the SKU. A customer-named thing is the entity: a washer, fridge door, toilet seat, laptop, or wall-mounted object. Its behavioral class and family describe it; they do not replace it as the primary domain identity. Actions and reusable modifiers combine with that entity to describe the work. The catalog stays small; coverage comes from composing those declared parts.

The Story Behind a Quote

A customer does not arrive thinking in categories, schemas, routing modes, or pricing formulas. They arrive with a job: five subway tiles need replacing, a television needs mounting, a door will not close, or a laptop has stopped behaving. They may know exactly what they want, know only the symptom, or know that something is wrong without knowing what to call it. The platform's first responsibility is to meet them there—not make them learn the shape of the catalog before they can ask for help.

The deeper lesson is that these are not seven unrelated missing fields or four unrelated module bugs. They are symptoms of a domain model that leaves important relationships implicit. If a service cannot declare which uncertainty matters, confidence has no honest denominator. If a question cannot declare which entity it applies to and what it depends on, the composer has to infer or reconstruct that relationship. If an option cannot state its actual effects, pricing and confidence must guess what its answer means. If recovery is not a distinct responsibility, “Answer More Questions” can reopen the same path without retiring anything new. The remedy is to author the relationships once, give each concern a proper home, and let each engine consume the same declared domain model.

BTNYC turns that ordinary description into a shared understanding of the work. Navigation, free text, guided choices, photographs, and answers contribute evidence to the same BookingContext. The system reflects that understanding back as a sentence the customer can correct. It asks another question only when the answer can change a meaningful part of the job or its outcome. When the evidence supports an exact price, it gives one. When it supports only a range or a conditional price, it says so. When a remote quote would pretend to know what only an on-site inspection can establish, it offers a real, priced diagnostic path instead. The customer should never have to choose between an invented price and a dead end.

For the engineer, that simple experience is a strict contract. If the preview interprets the request one way, the pricing engine interprets it another, and the booking flow quietly chooses between them, the interface can look polished while the customer receives a different job or price from the one they agreed to. So the platform must have one canonical understanding, one authoritative resolution for each decision, and a traceable route from authored business knowledge to the price shown and booked. The compiler may transform knowledge for runtime use, but it may not become the only place that knowledge exists; derivation and validation must remain separate; and a test must be capable of failing when the defect it claims to detect is present.

This is why the recurring engineering failures are not treated as a random list of bugs. Again and again, an artifact was expected to do two jobs whose needs conflict: authored knowledge and generated index, code organization and deployment packaging, derivation and validation, behavioral verification and source-shape preservation, durable law and live project ledger. Without a clear boundary, one role quietly became the authority for the other. The correction is not to add another fallback, another registry, or another status label. It is to decide which artifact owns each responsibility and make the handoff explicit.

The Charter tells both sides of that story. To the customer, it promises that the system will preserve their intent and justify the price. To the engineer, it defines the boundaries that make that promise reproducible. The rules that follow are not architecture for its own sake: each protects a step in the journey from a human request to a transparent, bookable outcome.

The Vision

How to interpret this Constitution. This document defines enduring outcomes, boundaries, and governance for present and future implementations. It is not a live inventory of files, modules, policies, data values, or migration status. Names and examples are explanatory unless explicitly declared as durable concepts; implementation, thresholds, and artifact layouts may evolve when the protected invariant remains intact. Historical evidence belongs in the Battles reference. When a current implementation conflicts with this Charter, treat the conflict as a design/governance issue to resolve—not as proof that the Charter should describe the implementation as law.

Flawless Friction Fulcrum

Every client gets a fully qualified quote on the site, without a pre-visit. A fully qualified quote is a concrete, bookable pricing outcome whose precision is justified by the resolved evidence; it may be exact, a bounded range, explicitly conditional, or a priced diagnostic when the evidence cannot honestly support a narrower result. The system asks the fewest questions that can retire the uncertainty that actually changes the price — never so many that the client walks away, and never so few that the price is invented. And when the exact price genuinely cannot be known from a distance, the system still offers a real, priced next step rather than a dead-end handoff.

Zero "call us" white flags. Zero system surrender.

One understanding, everywhere — the sentence a customer watches compose is the sentence the price is built from.

Foundational Commitments

These are the pillars of the project. Every rule, every chapter, every fix in this document traces back to one of them.

  1. 1Model concepts, not every item in the universe. A finite catalog of behaviors covers an unlimited surface of specific products.
  2. 2One engine, convergent entry modes. Every entry path resolves to the same underlying scope, the same confidence, the same price.
  3. 3Never make the client repeat themselves. What the client has already told us — through their tap, their text, or their answers — is not asked again.
  4. 4Ask only what we need, when we need it. A question exists only if its answer changes price, time, branch, or preparation.
  5. 5Price from reproducible rules. The same resolved scope and inputs always produce the same result.
  6. 6Confidence and complexity are dynamic. The job's shape evolves as evidence arrives; so does the intake, the routing, and the price.
  7. 7Transparency is part of the product. The Ad-Lib sentence summarizes every request in plain English.
  8. 8No white flag. Where an exact price isn't justified, there's still a range, a conditional quote, or a priced diagnostic. Never a dead end. Never a "call us."
  9. 9Converge, verify, preserve. All supported entry modes converge; every mechanism is verified against the complete relevant set; nothing curated is lost to a compiler.
  10. 10Surface the foundation. Never build silently around a broken shape. When a foundational artifact — the SSOT's graph, a schema's core premise, an authored contract — appears wrong, or is being worked around rather than fixed, that is itself a finding. It is named and raised to the party who can fix it. Working around a broken foundation is a decision, not a default, and it is surfaced as one.

How This Document Works

The Charter begins with the customer outcome and works downward to the constraints that protect it. A client describes a job; BTNYC must understand it, ask only for evidence that matters, resolve it into a defensible scope, and return a transparent price or a genuinely useful priced next step. The engineering model exists to make that outcome reproducible. It is not the customer's job to understand our internal architecture, and it is not the engineer's job to preserve an architecture that obstructs the outcome.

Statement key.Vision names the destination; Principle gives binding direction; Rule requires or prohibits an observable behavior or structure; Governance Process governs authority, conflict, amendment, and records. Invariant describes a property of the domain or system; when required as policy, it is expressed as a Rule. Labels identify statement kind—not priority, maturity, or enforcement status. Directory links are navigation, not dependency bookkeeping.

This document states durable direction and binding Rules. It is not the live task ledger, technical schema, implementation inventory, or evidence archive. Those artifacts have separate roles because the project repeatedly paid for asking one artifact to do two jobs. The timeline records history; pending decisions record open choices; the SSOT records authored business knowledge; the schema defines valid shape; derived output serves the runtime; verification supplies evidence. The Charter explains the law governing their relationships.

Structural Layers — Vision, Principles, Governance, Rules, and Implementation

The Charter distinguishes different kinds of statements so that direction, authority, operational law, and implementation are not confused with one another. These layers are complementary, not mechanically paired. A Principle does not require a Rule, and a Rule does not require a named Principle. The purpose of the hierarchy is to distinguish kinds of statements, not to create dependency bookkeeping between them.

LayerDefinitionWhat it doesWhat accomplishment looks like
VisionThe ultimate North Star destination.Defines the outcome the project exists to achieve.Decisions can be explained as protecting or advancing the stated outcome.
PrincipleThe strategic philosophy and core beliefs that translate the Vision into enduring direction.Guides judgment where an explicit Rule does not prescribe the answer.A difficult choice can be explained consistently without inventing a new Rule merely to settle that one choice.
GovernanceThe process by which authority, disagreement, amendment, and structural change are handled.Prevents both bureaucracy and silent drift.Conflicts are surfaced, decisions have an identifiable authority, and durable changes are recorded in the appropriate source.
RuleA binary operational guardrail: an explicit required or prohibited behavior, structure, or boundary.Protects the Vision through observable day-to-day constraints.Two independent reviewers can determine from the resulting behavior or state whether the Rule was satisfied.
ImplementationThe code, data, configuration, tests, and other mechanisms that realize the Rules and Principles.Turns the durable model into a working system.The live system exhibits the required behavior without requiring implementation details to become hidden doctrine.

G-GOVERN-VISIONLINKGovernance ProcessRule-to-Vision Traceability

Every Rule and Principle must identify the specific Vision outcome it protects or advances and the concrete harm it prevents. Traceability is explanatory, not a requirement to pair each Rule with a specific Principle. A link that could be copied unchanged onto an unrelated statement is not specific enough.

Rule Health and Evolvability Contract. Each Rule and Principle must make five things discoverable in its local passage or its immediately linked canonical specification: (1) the specific Vision outcome, (2) the harm prevented, (3) the implementation freedom that remains, (4) the evidence or condition that should trigger reconsideration, and (5) the evidence mechanism appropriate to the claim. These are substantive obligations, not mandatory repeated headings. Use progressive disclosure where detail would interrupt the narrative. Behavioral claims require behavioral evidence; source-shape claims require source inspection; cross-layer claims require representative end-to-end evidence. A test is evidence, not authority.

Accomplishment: A reader can explain the distinct Vision connection of each Rule and Principle without inventing a ceremonial link. If that explanation cannot be made honestly, clarify, amend, or retire the statement through governance; do not manufacture a link merely to satisfy a checklist.

Governance of Normative Statements

Normative text is classified by what it does, not by how easy it is to test. A statement that directs judgment is a Principle. A statement that requires or prohibits observable behavior is a Rule. A statement that governs how people or records make and change decisions is Governance. Explanations, examples, evidence, history, specifications, and implementation details remain those things; they do not become doctrine merely because they appear in the Charter.

When a Rule and Principle appear to conflict, do not create a pairing rule to decide the matter. First determine whether the statement has been classified correctly. If both are correctly classified and genuinely conflict, Governance resolves the conflict and the durable decision is recorded as an amendment. Convenience, test coverage, or historical implementation does not resolve the conflict by itself.
A Rule does not become optional because it lacks automation. Enforcement coverage describes implementation maturity. It never changes the normative status of the Rule.
A passing test is never itself an accomplishment. The accomplishment is the behavior or resulting state the Rule requires. A failing test is evidence of a possible Rule violation; it is not permission to distort the implementation merely to restore the test.

G-GOVERN-ANTISTALEGovernance ProcessThe Anti-Staleness Rule

Vision link: It protects the Vision by keeping the Charter trustworthy as durable law, while ensuring historical evidence and mutable implementation facts cannot masquerade as current requirements.

Accomplishment: The Charter contains enduring commitments and explicit architectural intent, not task IDs, timeline numbers, mutable counts, live priorities, current module inventories, or data-specific values that belong in an owning specification. The Battles Won reference in Part XI is the sole home for historical Battle narratives and their dated evidence trails. Rule bodies may link to a Battle in one line, but do not reproduce its preview, T-numbers, or contemporaneous implementation description.

The Charter records enduring outcomes, boundaries, and decision principles. The owning specification records current policy and authored data; implementation documentation records chosen mechanisms; the timeline records chronology; verification records observed evidence. A current filename, module inventory, threshold, task identifier, or migration state must not become constitutional merely because it exists today. Examples are illustrative unless explicitly declared as durable concepts. Historical material belongs in Part XI; elsewhere, a Battle may be referenced by a concise link only, never replayed as a preview or presented as current architecture.

The Charter is deliberately durable. It may state a principle, an architectural boundary, a business invariant, or a lesson that should survive implementation changes. It should not become a second status ledger. The classification is checked by a source scan: every sentence in this document that names a task, a T-number, a specific current-state count, or a live priority is flagged for relocation to the owning record.

The Battles are history, and history is allowed to date. The Battles Won reference and its evidence trails in Part XI are the explicitly exempted historical record. They preserve the reasoning and lessons of past sessions rather than describe the current system. T-numbers, dated decisions, and descriptions of the system at the time of a Battle are historical content only within those battle records. Everywhere else in the Charter — every Rule, Principle, Governance Process, and architectural statement — must be forward-looking and must not carry mutable state.

G-GOVERN-PROJECTGovernance ProcessEvidence Projection Is Separate from Law

A principle does not have a status. The project's current embodiment of the principle has a status.
  1. No authored status on a principle. Live state is projected from the aggregator, not authored here.
  2. Battle cards cite evidence, not identity. A Battle is named for its lesson, not its T-number.
  3. Volatile facts live in ledgers. Current violations, priorities, test counts, and field-level contracts belong to the ledgers, not the charter.
  4. Any sentence that would need editing when a task closes is not a charter sentence.

G-GOVERN-PRECEDENCEGovernance ProcessSource Disagreement Resolution

Vision link: It protects the Vision by keeping decisions, records, and verification trustworthy rather than allowing stale or self-reinforcing artifacts to distort the customer outcome.

Executable reality establishes what the system currently does; it does not by itself establish what the system ought to do. The owning specification establishes the intended contract, this Charter establishes durable architectural law, and evidence establishes what was actually observed. When these disagree, state the difference rather than letting whichever artifact is easiest to read silently win. Governance decides which source is wrong, and the owning source is amended so that observed behavior, intended contract, and durable law converge.

Serves:P-GOVERN-GOODHART

  1. Executable reality: current code and executable tests establish what is actually live.
  2. Owning specification: the schema, component map, SSOT, or other named engineering document establishes the intended contract.
  3. Evidence: the historical record and related ledgers establish what happened and why.
  4. Project Charter: this document states the durable rule and architectural intent; it does not override the owning specification or executable reality by assertion.

When those layers disagree, treat the disagreement as a tracked engineering issue. If the intended contract has changed, deliberately amend the owning specification and, when the change is durable, this Charter; if the code is behind the adopted contract, the divergence is a defect to resolve.

Accomplishment: When sources disagree, the record identifies observed behavior, intended contract, evidence, and the governance decision; the owning source is then amended so the disagreement is not silently carried forward.

G-GOVERN-SURFACEGovernance ProcessConflict Surfacing

The Charter governs durable direction and decision principles. It is the shared reference, not subordinate to momentary reasoning. When any decision — the agent's own, or one the operator has directed — appears to contradict the Charter, the agent surfaces the conflict, cites the specific rule or principle code, and neither executes nor refuses silently. If the operator confirms the instruction after the conflict is surfaced, the instruction becomes law; the agent executes, then amends the Charter by preserving the original text in a collapsed <details class="amendment-log"> block and recording the new text, the date, and an "Operator Override — Authorized" tag.

G-GOVERN-OVERRIDEGovernance ProcessAmendment and Override

If an operator confirms an instruction that conflicts with an existing Rule, record the conflict and the proposed exception explicitly. A confirmation is authority to make a governance decision, not a silent rewrite of the Vision or an invisible exception. If the decision is intended to change durable law, amend the Rule through the governance process before treating the changed behavior as the new steady state; preserve the prior decision in the timeline, not as a competing law inside this document.

G-GOVERN-TRANSITIONGovernance ProcessStructural Change Is Authorized

Vision link: It protects the Vision by keeping decisions, records, and verification trustworthy rather than allowing stale or self-reinforcing artifacts to distort the customer outcome.

Accomplishment: Every structural change records the defect in the current shape, the intended target shape, the migration/rollback condition, and whether a second live source exists; any dual-source period also satisfies R-SYSTEM-DUALSOURCE.

Serves:P-GOVERN-DEFECTCLASS

The Charter authorizes reshaping. It does not only protect what exists.

A structural change to the system — the shape of the SSOT, the placement of derived data, the naming of a namespace, the boundary between a service and its parent, the layout of a chain — is an authorized engineering act when the current shape forces a consumer to work around it. Such a change requires three things and no others:

  1. A rationale — the specific workaround the current shape forces. "Consumers must copy this fact into a second location to resolve it correctly" is a rationale. "It feels wrong" is not.
  2. An endpoint — the new shape, stated plainly.
  3. A ledger entry — recorded so the change is discoverable to future sessions.

The change does not require operator sign-off when the rationale is concrete. It does not require a new rule code. It does not require a test in the same change, because it does not change the system's behavior — it changes the shape the behavior is expressed in. The enforcement check asserts that every ledger entry describing a structural change carries both a rationale and an endpoint.

Local and foundational change are governed differently. A local shape change is autonomous when the rationale is concrete. A foundational shape change carries an additional obligation: the operator is informed before or as the change lands, because the change reshapes what every other part of the system assumes. Notification is not permission — the change is still authorized by this rule — but it is required.

ClassExampleSign-off
Local shapeRename a namespace; relocate a derived field; redraw a module boundaryAutonomous. Ledger entry with rationale and endpoint.
Foundational shapeThe graph model itself is wrong; the schema's central premise is broken; a contract the architecture is built onOperator notified before or as the change lands, in addition to the ledger entry. Notification, not permission.
Why this rule exists. Prior to this rule, every norm in the Charter was a preservation norm. Preserve the SSOT's content. Preserve canonical implementations. Preserve the on-return-value provenance. Preserve the fix. There was no affirmative verb for reshaping. When the SSOT's shape was itself the problem, the Charter had nothing to say — and the agent, needing to make progress, either worked around the shape or invented a convention that turned into a rule the Charter never authorized. This rule names the missing act.

G-GOVERN-RAISEGovernance ProcessDuty to Raise a Broken Foundation

No participant — agent or human — builds silently around a foundation they believe is broken.

Every participant carries an affirmative duty to surface a foundational artifact that appears wrong: the SSOT's shape, a schema's core premise, an authored contract, or any artifact other work is being built on top of. The duty is not discharged by working around it, by compensating for it locally, or by assuming someone else has noticed.

The duty has three parts:

  1. Recognize. A workaround in progress is evidence the shape is wrong (R-DOMAIN-SHAPE). Recognizing a workaround as a workaround is part of the duty, not separate from it.
  2. Name. State the artifact, the defect, the workaround if one is in use, and whether the workaround is transitional. "This feels wrong" is not a report. "The graph is non-flat and every consumer compensates" is.
  3. Raise. Tell the party who can fix the foundation. When the foundation is foundational to the whole project — the SSOT's graph, the schema's central premise — that party is the operator, and the operator being informed is part of the obligation, not a consequence of it.

The duty applies even when the participant has the authority to fix the shape autonomously (G-GOVERN-TRANSITION). Autonomy to reshape a local structure does not extend to silently accepting a foundational problem.

Why this rule exists. The Charter previously had prohibitions ("do not execute silently"), authorizations ("you may reshape"), and diagnostics ("a workaround is evidence the shape is wrong") — but no affirmative duty to report. The failure mode that motivated this rule: a foundational artifact was wrong; every session worked around it locally and reasonably; no session was obligated to say so; the workaround accumulated into the architecture; the operator learned of it only after the fact. The missing act was not a prohibition or an authorization. It was a duty. A rule cannot make a participant notice what they do not notice — so the duty pairs with a periodic foundation review, a standing obligation to step back and ask whether the shape everything is built on is still the right shape.

G-GOVERN-AUTONOMYGovernance ProcessDecision Authority and Agent Autonomy

Within the Charter, the agent has both the authority and the directive to decide: business numbers, pricing, time estimates, taxonomy, naming, structural placement, and every other choice the Charter does not reserve. Deferral is not the default. Reasoning in place of deciding is a failure mode.

When a real business number is needed, the agent applies the following hierarchy and decides — not defer:

  1. NYC market average. Where a comparable real-world rate exists, use it. Cite the source inline.
  2. Existing catalog data. Where a directly comparable service, modifier, rate, or fee exists in the authored source, anchor to it. This includes percentages of an existing rate, values scaled from a comparable modifier, and values anchored to an existing band.
  3. Midpoint of the high/low range. Where neither of the above applies but evidence supports a range, use its midpoint and record the range inline.
  4. Best-considered estimate. Where none of the above applies, make the best-considered estimate and document the reasoning inline.

Every decision made under this authority is documented where it lives — the data field's own _note, a code comment beside the change, or the owning ledger entry — with the reasoning. The agent is not excused from accountability by being empowered to decide.

Standing test."Do I have concrete reasoning grounded in the charter and available evidence, and no operator instruction forbidding it? If yes — decide. If no — the conflict itself is the thing to surface, not the decision."

When an agent acts autonomously, use this decision hierarchy in order:

Decision Hierarchy — Quick Reference

When a business number, fee, threshold, or policy value is needed:

StepSourceWhen to useDocumentation
1NYC market averageA comparable real-world rate existsCite the source inline in the _note
2Existing catalog dataA comparable service, modifier, rate, or fee existsAnchor value + reference in the _note
3Midpoint of high/low rangeEvidence supports a range but no direct anchorRecord the range in the _note
4Best-considered estimateNone of the above produces an answerDocument the reasoning in the _note
Defer only if: the decision would contradict an explicit or clearly-implicit operator instruction. Surface the conflict; do not silently resolve it.
Do not defer because the agent lacks perfect information. That is the normal condition under which the hierarchy exists.

G-GOVERN-INDEXGovernance ProcessRule and Principle Index

Vision link: It protects the Vision by keeping decisions, records, and verification trustworthy rather than allowing stale or self-reinforcing artifacts to distort the customer outcome.

Accomplishment: Every normative statement has a stable code and a working anchor; the index links to each current Rule, Principle, and Governance process without implying enforcement status or mandatory pairing.

Serves:P-GOVERN-GOODHART

Every citable statement carries a stable code. Rules use R-<DOMAIN>-<NAME>; Governance Processes use G-<DOMAIN>-<NAME>; Principles use P-<DOMAIN>-<NAME>. The prefix identifies the category. Governance processes that carry the G-GOVERN-* prefix are governance processes by kind, not operational product Rules, and the directory lists them under their current codes.

Document Relationship Map

The Charter is the durable architectural and product-direction layer. The other documents own different kinds of truth; none is a substitute for the others.

Reader aids and evidence projections are optional presentation mechanisms, not normative sources. They may be replaced or removed without changing the Charter’s authority or the architectural responsibilities it defines.

Important: the diagram describes governance and ownership boundaries, not an undocumented runtime dependency graph. It does not claim that the evidence/status projection directly reads every source or that every source is generated from another.

Part I
The Client Experience
What the client sees. Multiple ways in. One canonical destination.
R-CLIENT-PRESENTATION-ONLYRuleEvery supported customer entry mode is a presentation and evidence-acquisition layer only. Entry modes may differ in how a customer expresses a job or supplies evidence; they must not own separate resolvers, confidence engines, pricing calculators, or booking logic. Every supported mode hands the same canonical convergence contract to the same resolution and pricing pipeline and reaches the same canonical convergence outcome. Equivalent facts must produce equivalent resolved scope, quote, and booking outcome regardless of entry path.

Vision outcome protected: All customer entry paths feed one canonical interpretation and pricing pipeline, so the same job cannot receive different outcomes based on how it was described.

Accomplishment: A cross-mode equivalence check supplies the same facts through every registered supported entry mode and verifies equivalent canonical scope, applicable pricing behavior, and customer-facing result. The set of modes is discovered from the governing design, not frozen by this Rule.

In plain English: A cross-mode equivalence check supplies the same facts through every registered supported entry mode and verifies equivalent canonical scope, applicable pricing behavior, and customer-facing result. The set of modes is discovered from the governing design, not frozen by this Rule.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — All customer entry paths feed one canonical interpretation and pricing pipeline, so the same job cannot receive different outcomes based on how it was described.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: all customer entry paths feed one canonical interpretation and pricing pipeline, so the same job cannot receive different outcomes based on how it was described.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that all customer entry paths feed one canonical interpretation and pricing pipeline, so the same job cannot receive different outcomes based on how it was described fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

P-CLIENT-THREEPrincipleDistinct entry modes are supported ways for a customer to express intent, and all converge on one canonical engine.One engine, convergent entry modesAdding, removing, or redefining an entry mode is a deliberate architectural change governed by its effect on convergence, not by a fixed gateway count.

Vision outcome protected: giving customers a small, coherent set of entry paths that converge on one canonical understanding rather than fragmenting into separate engines.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Category Navigation and the “Other” path illustrate two complementary ways to descend through the catalog: a behavioral-class-driven picker for shapes the catalog already expresses, and a group-scoped structured builder for work that needs more detail. These paths may evolve; their shared obligation is to converge on the same canonical interpretation. The two paths offer complementary levels of structure: a behavioral-class-driven picker for the shapes the catalog already expresses, and a group-scoped structured builder for everything else the group can still legitimately receive. SmartQuote is the third gateway, and it is the free-text surface that leads naturally into the Ad-Lib Confirmation chapter that follows.

The Booking Funnel

Six stages, end to end. One BookingContext in, one qualified quote out. This is the pipeline the code runs — the contract that connects the client's entry to a bookable price.

SSOTThe authored source — the single source of every rule the six stages above consult.

The platform can also be described as four conceptual stages — Recognition → Scoping → Pricing → Expression — and as the seven-step client-facing flow in Part I — Describe → Understand → Infer → Scope → Price → Explain → Book. These are not competing pipelines; they are three lenses on the same flow. The six-stage funnel above is the execution contract: Entry → Collect → Orchestrate → Price → Express → Book.

Gateway A — Category Navigation

The first thing a client sees on the landing page is the category grid. It is the most prominent surface on the screen, and for a client who already knows roughly what kind of work they need, it is the fastest way in. They tap a tile — Minor Home Repairs, Plumbing Help, Tech Trouble — and drill down through groups and services until they land on a curated card.

The navigation itself carries information. Reaching a service through a specific path — Wall Mounting → Brick → Frames — already declares the wall type, the billing model, and the object being mounted. The system uses those facts. It never asks the client to restate what their own tap already told us.

P-CLIENT-NAVFACTPrincipleWhat the navigation path declares is a fact. A path that reaches a service through a "Brick or Concrete" sub-group has already answered the wall-type question. A path that reaches a repair service through a specific appliance group has already answered the appliance-type question. The intake chain consults the navigation path the same way it consults the customer's own free text — and refuses to ask again what the path already supplied. See The Wire-or-Remove Test.

Vision outcome protected: treating the customer’s navigation choices as evidence already supplied, avoiding redundant questions and contradictory paths.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — treating the customer’s navigation choices as evidence already supplied, avoiding redundant questions and contradictory paths.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: treating the customer’s navigation choices as evidence already supplied, avoiding redundant questions and contradictory paths.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that treating the customer’s navigation choices as evidence already supplied, avoiding redundant questions and contradictory paths fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Beneath Every Group, a Picker

Reaching a group does not produce an undifferentiated grid of services. It produces a picker whose shape reflects what the customer is most likely to already know for this family of work — the parts of door hardware, the symptoms of an appliance, the surfaces of a repair. For a family of work where the customer arrives with a part in mind, the picker leads with parts; for a family where the customer arrives with a problem, it leads with symptoms. The picker honours the shape the customer is actually holding, not the shape the catalog finds convenient.

At the same level as the picker sits its companion "Other" tile — the second door into the same family of work, for the case where the picker's own vocabulary does not cover the customer's job. See Gateway B — The "Other" Tile for what "Other" is and why it is a current part of the product.

Whichever door the customer takes — a specific part, a specific symptom, or "Other" — their choice hands off to the same canonical resolution every other entry path uses. See Convergence — One Destination. How the picker's shape is classified, and how the "Other" tile pairs with it, is stated once in The Navigation Architecture; that chapter is the rule's home, and this chapter references it rather than restating it.

Gateway B — The "Other" Tile

At every group, alongside the behavioral-class-driven picker, sits one more way in. If the picker's own options do not describe the client's actual job — the component is one the catalog has not authored, the symptom is outside the group's known set, or the job is genuinely a different shape from what this group usually handles — the "Other" tile opens a guided builder scoped to that group.

It is not a blank form. It is a structured, step-by-step flow that already knows which group the client came from and asks only what that group still needs answered. It is the same composer underneath — the same sentence machinery, the same passive chips, the same live preview — seeded with the context the client already provided by drilling into the group.

P-CLIENT-OTHERPrincipleThe “Other” tile is a permanent, intended entry path — not a transitional fallback, and not a legacy escape hatch awaiting retirement. It is the structured complement to behavioral-class-driven navigation, and it is the mechanism by which the system keeps its promise that the catalog stays finite while coverage does not. Where the picker covers the shapes the catalog already knows how to express for this family of work, "Other" covers everything else the group can still legitimately receive — through the same canonical resolution every other path uses. Its role is permanent in the same way the free-text composer is permanent.

Vision outcome protected: keeping unfamiliar or unlisted requests on a structured path to a useful result rather than abandoning the customer.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — keeping unfamiliar or unlisted requests on a structured path to a useful result rather than abandoning the customer.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: keeping unfamiliar or unlisted requests on a structured path to a useful result rather than abandoning the customer.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that keeping unfamiliar or unlisted requests on a structured path to a useful result rather than abandoning the customer fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Why “Other” Is Permanent, Not Transitional

The instinct to treat "Other" as a temporary mechanism comes from a reasonable-sounding assumption: that a mature catalog eventually covers every case, so the escape hatch is only needed until the catalog is complete. That assumption is wrong at the level of the business model.

  • The catalog is finite by design. It models concepts, not every product. A customer naming a specific product the catalog has never seen is not a gap in the catalog; it is the catalog working as intended.
  • Behavioral-class-driven navigation is a structured first answer, not an exhaustive one. It covers the shapes the family of work most commonly takes. Anything outside those shapes is not an edge case to be eliminated; it is the reason the "Other" tile exists at the same level.
  • The structured flow is not a degraded experience. A customer reaching the group's builder receives the same quality of intake, the same live preview, the same ad-lib confirmation, the same priced outcome — just reached by a different door.
  • The set of things a customer might describe is unbounded. No picker, however well-designed, can enumerate it. The correct answer to that structural reality is not a bigger picker; it is a structured escape hatch that stays at the same level as the picker itself.

Remove "Other" and the picker becomes a dead end for exactly the cases the system was built to handle. That is the opposite of the product promise.

Group-Scoped, Not a Blank Form

An "Other" tile that opened a blank form would be a much weaker mechanism — it would ask the customer to describe their job from scratch, discarding everything their own navigation already told us. The tile that exists opens a builder scoped to the group the customer came from. It knows the category, it knows the group, and it asks only what the group still needs answered. This is the same inference discipline that governs intake everywhere else: what the customer has already told us through their tap is not asked again.

It Reaches the Same Canonical Outcome

Whatever the group-scoped builder collects, it resolves through the same canonical pipeline every other entry path uses. There is no second resolution pipeline for "Other" and no separate pricing path. See Convergence — One Destination for the invariant, and Dynamic Services & Bespoke Concepts for how the group-scoped dynamic resolution is composed.

What the Client Sees

The engine-side view of the "Other" tile, and the classification that drives the whole picker/pair, is in The Navigation Architecture. The chapter you are reading states the customer experience and the durable rule; the engineering chapter states how the pairing is built and enforced.

Gateway C — SmartQuote

Directly beneath the category grid sits the SmartQuote bar. It is the second thing the client sees, and it is the surface that does the most work. A client can type freely — "I need five subway tiles replaced in my kitchen" — or use the guided builder's choices to construct the request slot by slot. Both modes feed the same composer, and the composer reflects what the system has understood back to the client as a live sentence.

The two modes are not two surfaces. They are one surface. Typing a phrase fills slots the builder's choices would have filled; tapping a chip narrows the free text the way a clarifying word would. The composer does not care which hand wrote the request. It only cares that the request is complete, and it uses the same machinery to build the sentence whether the client arrives by keyboard or by tap.

SmartQuote is the third gateway, and it is the free-text one. Where the picker and its "Other" companion descend through structure the customer has already been given, SmartQuote lets the customer lead with language and lets the system reconstruct the structure from what they said. It is the natural lead-in to the Ad-Lib Confirmation chapter that follows: the sentence the customer watches compose here is the same sentence the price will be built from there.

R-CLIENT-PREVIEWThe sentence you watch compose is the sentence that gets priced. There is one parse of the request and one composer of the sentence; every render path reads from the same canonical understanding. See The NLP Layer for the structural rule and its enforcement.

Vision outcome protected: On every supported entry mode, the preview and pricing input carry the same canonical intent; a planted disagreement causes verification to fail.

Accomplishment: On every supported entry mode, the preview and pricing input carry the same canonical intent; a planted disagreement causes verification to fail.

In plain English: On every supported entry mode, the preview and pricing input carry the same canonical intent; a planted disagreement causes verification to fail.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. Read the interface in the order the customer experiences it: free-text entry → live composed preview → inline slot edits → passive applicable chips → Edit Drawer → confirmation. The preview sits above the text input, updates as the customer types, renders recognized values as editable pills, and leaves unresolved values visible as ghost slots. These are presentation mechanisms over one shared understanding—not separate business engines.

The Live Preview — Live Slot Composition

The live preview is the intended customer-facing presentation of what SmartQuote has understood so far. It updates as the customer types, showing a readable sentence assembled from recognized words and structured slots. Resolved values appear as inline pills that can be changed in place; unresolved values remain visible as ghost slots rather than being silently guessed. This is a real-time view of the shared interpretation—not a prototype, a second parser, or an independent quote calculator.

Passive Applicable Chips — Optional Refinements

As typed text or slot state warrants, related chips appear beneath the input. The user can tap a chip to apply it, and it is appended to the end of their input in the text box. Tapping again — or tapping a selected chip — removes it. Selection and removal are passive: no modal, no navigation, no lost cursor.

Only applicable chips may appear. Chips are drawn from the real smart-tag set in the SSOT, scoped by the applicability model, and surfaced only when the current typed content or slot state actually warrants them. Arbitrary, exhaustive, or "just in case" chip display is prohibited.
P-CLIENT-CHIPSPrincipleChips do not compose into the preview sentence. A chip's role is to carry a fact into the booking context — it is rendered in the chip row, and its effect appears in the preview only through the answer it produces. The sentence is not a canvas for chip clauses.

Vision outcome protected: making optional refinements visible without letting presentation chips create a second interpretation or hidden price input.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — making optional refinements visible without letting presentation chips create a second interpretation or hidden price input.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: making optional refinements visible without letting presentation chips create a second interpretation or hidden price input.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The Edit Drawer — Deliberate Corrections

Once the input has formed a logically complete "We Understood" preview — all required slots (quantity, item, action, location) filled — an EDIT button appears. The user may always continue editing by typing; EDIT is an additional, optional way to modify their input parameters.

When the user needs to refine the system's interpretation or manually adjust specific parameters, they interact with the edit section. This view provides explicit dropdowns for interpreted slots, ensuring the user maintains control over the final job statement before calculating the estimate.

Tapping EDIT opens an inline, interactive editor over the preview itself. Each editable slot is changed via a dedicated drop-down. Selecting a new value re-renders the sentence in place and dispatches to the logic layer; the renderer stays pure.

This is not a port of the existing Intake Screen editor. That editor exists today and is judged poor. The requirement is a rebuild of interaction quality — more fluid, more responsive, genuinely usable. The same editor must live in the main Smart Quote view and the Guided Builder input, not only the Intake Screen.

The Ad-Lib Confirmation

Illustrative values. Illustrative example only — all amounts and fee values in this example demonstrate sentence composition, not current pricing policy.

Slot rendering rules. Template slots render as inline pills. Filled slots render as solid, tinted pills; empty slots render as dashed "ghost" pills bearing the slot's prompt text (how many?, what item?, doing what?, which room?). The next-empty slot in composition order pulses to guide the user. When a slot fills, it flashes briefly — confirming the system identified the user's input.

The following examples belong together: first the customer sees an incomplete request being assembled from slots; then the same canonical sentence appears fully resolved for confirmation. These are two states of one Ad-Lib experience, not separate quoting systems.

In-Progress Slot Composition

A dynamic, interactive sentence in flawless natural English — not a bullet list — that names every answer the booking context carries and shows how each fee influences the price.

After the pricing engine processes input, it generates a dynamic, interactive ad-lib confirmation sentence. It is the output-side mirror of the "We Understood" preview the client watched compose itself in Gateway C. Same grammar. Same template machinery. Different moment.

Chips are not the sentence's vocabulary. The Ad-Lib sentence names answers — the facts the booking context carries. It does not name chips. A chip may be the mechanism by which an answer was produced (a tag firing, synthesizing an answer); the chip itself is never composed into the sentence. The chip row is the chip's home; the sentence is the answer's home.
  • Linguistic requirement. Flawless, readable, natural English — not a robotic list. It must read like a fluent human summary.
  • Functional requirement. The sentence explicitly validates the user's input by naming the specific answers the booking context carries — whether client-given or system-inferred — and clearly showing how their associated fees influence the final price.
"You need 5 picture frames mounted in your living room. Because we're working with drywall and you'd like same-week scheduling, your quote is $247 — including a $35 drywall-anchor fee (per visit) and $20 rush fee (per visit)."
R-CLIENT-STUTTERThe sentence must not stutter. A service's display name can end in the same action word the sentence is about to append ("Cabinet Door or Drawer Adjustment adjusted"). The composer strips a trailing, nominalized action suffix from the label before appending the verb, so the customer reads a clean sentence rather than a doubled word. When the strip cannot apply, the composer does not silently produce the redundancy; the label is either corrected or the sentence structure is chosen to avoid it.

Vision outcome protected: For every catalog service name and supported action, composing the customer-facing sentence produces no repeated or duplicated action word.

Accomplishment: For every catalog service name and supported action, composing the customer-facing sentence produces no repeated or duplicated action word.

In plain English: For every catalog service name and supported action, composing the customer-facing sentence produces no repeated or duplicated action word.

Rule health & evolvability

Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The “We Understood” Preview

The completed sentence is the output-side mirror of the live slot composition: both use the same canonical template machinery, and neither creates a separate interpretation or pricing path.

Trust Through Transparency

This sentence is a trust-building mechanism, not a UI flourish. It must drastically reduce uncertainty and make the user feel understood, in control, and respected — not processed.

Design implication. Because the sentence names specific answers and their fees, the underlying pricing must be genuinely explainable in natural language. A pricing formula that cannot produce such a sentence is a signal that the formula is too opaque or the intake is too shallow.

Placement and Stacking Order

The Gateway C SmartQuote stack—the free-text input, live preview, and applicable-chip area—belongs to the SmartQuote panel. Shared components such as the edit drawer may be reused by the Guided Builder without relocating the Gateway C stack itself.

Within that panel, three elements stack vertically in this exact top-to-bottom order. No future change may reorder, merge, or relocate them without amending this section:

  1. The constructed sentence preview text (top)
  2. The text input box (directly beneath the preview)
  3. Passive smart-tag chip suggestions (directly beneath the text input box, rendering dynamically as the user types and NLP infers or identifies them as applicable)

This is the inverse of the usual "input, then output" arrangement and is deliberate: the preview sits where the eye lands first, so the "We Understood" reconstruction is always the first thing read, with the input and chips as the controls that shape it.

Component-Layer Obligations

The layered architecture rules apply without exception:

  • The composer UI is a Renderer. Its state is resolved state, not local mutable business state.
  • Chip applicability is business logic, not rendering logic. The SSOT defines the facts and predicates; a logic/selector layer evaluates them and supplies the renderer with the currently applicable chips. Indexes, memoization, caching, or precomputed lookup structures are implementation details permitted to make that evaluation fast.
  • The preview sentence comes from the canonical ad-lib machinery, not a second sentence generator.
  • No new resolution pipeline. The composer produces or updates canonical context; the orchestrator remains the authority for resolution and pricing.
Prototype is evidence, not architecture. A working interaction prototype can validate the shape of an experience without proving that the production resolution, SSOT, applicability, confidence, or pricing path is complete. The production contract lives in the architecture and its tests. Prototype gaps are implementation evidence to be tracked in the engineering record, not silently promoted into product doctrine.

Convergence — One Destination

Three roads, one destination. The destination is not a page. It is a single canonical BookingContext — the contract between resolution and rendering — regardless of which gateway the client came through.

Gateway A:    Minor Home Repairs > Doors > Prehung Door > Install
↓ tile navigation (behavioral-class-driven picker)
↓ same BookingContext → same resolved service

Gateway B:    Group-scoped "Other" → guided builder
↓ step-by-step flow
↓ same BookingContext → same resolved service

Gateway C:    "I need a Prehung Door Installed in my Bedroom"
↓ composer slots
↓ same BookingContext → same resolved service

All three must converge on the same resolved scope, applicable routing and pricing logic, and customer-facing outcome. They may legitimately collect information differently — one asks a picker question, another a builder question, a third parses it from free text — but for the same facts they must resolve to the same canonical result. Live preview, estimate, and final booking must also converge on that same result. If two paths produce different outcomes for the same facts, the difference is a system defect, not a product variation.

R-CLIENT-CONVERGEOne understanding, everywhere. Convergence is not only about the final price. The live preview the customer watches compose must be the same understanding the price is built from. When two parsers exist, they eventually disagree, and the customer is the one who finds out — they watch one understanding compose and receive a different one priced. The single-parse rule in The NLP Layer is the structural answer; this section names the product consequence.

Accomplishment: Equivalent facts entered through Category Navigation, Other, and Smart Quote resolve to equivalent BookingContext, scope, and price; a path-specific divergence fails verification.

Vision outcome protected: Equivalent facts entered through Category Navigation, Other, and Smart Quote resolve to equivalent BookingContext, scope, and price; a path-specific divergence fails verification.

In plain English: Equivalent facts entered through Category Navigation, Other, and Smart Quote resolve to equivalent BookingContext, scope, and price; a path-specific divergence fails verification.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Equivalent facts entered through Category Navigation, Other, and Smart Quote resolve to equivalent BookingContext, scope, and price; a path-specific divergence fails verification.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: equivalent facts entered through category navigation, other, and smart quote resolve to equivalent bookingcontext, scope, and price; a path-specific divergence fails verification.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that equivalent facts entered through category navigation, other, and smart quote resolve to equivalent bookingcontext, scope, and price; a path-specific divergence fails verification fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The rule exists because the codebase did not start this way. Three entry paths were added in three different sessions, each solving a different problem, and each quietly re-implementing resolution logic the others already had. Two of the three eventually produced different prices for the same real job. Worse, the free-text path once carried its own, second parser — so the sentence a customer watched compose could be structurally different from the sentence that got priced.

The field-level contract for BookingContext is maintained in the maintained component-responsibility map. The charter commits to the concept — one canonical convergence point between resolution and rendering — not to the specific field names or shapes, which will evolve.

The Final Expression

The system's only job: ask just enough questions so that the resolved scope is sufficient for the applicable pricing path, and if exact scope cannot yet be established, offer a priced path forward — never a dead end.

The finished platform will allow any user to:

  1. Describe their job — via free text, the guided builder, or catalog browsing.
  2. See a transparent, fully editable breakdown — with the ad-lib confirmation as the centerpiece.
  3. Book with confidence — knowing exactly what they're paying for and why.

It scales because it does not catalogue the universe — it models concepts, attributes, and behaviours. It will never say "Call us" without a price.

Describe → Understand → Infer → Scope → Price → Explain → Book
Part II
The Domain
What the system models. How it names what it resolves. What happens when the catalog runs out.

The Domain Model

Names versus contracts. BookingContext names the canonical resolved-request contract in this Charter. The contract is the enduring requirement; its concrete type, field names, serialization, and module location may change through governed evolution.

BTNYC does not begin with a catalog of services. It begins with the concepts the business can reason about. The domain model is the hierarchy that turns an arbitrary customer phrase into reusable behavior without requiring a bespoke service record for every possible item or wording.

Naming From Scope

Illustrative values. Illustrative examples only — the service names and amounts demonstrate scope-derived naming and presentation; the values are not current pricing policy.
Naming guidance. A client-facing service name should render its scope clearly, often as [Object] + [Qualifier] + [Action]. This is a presentation convention, not a key or separate Rule; machine identity and references are governed by R-SYSTEM-IDENTITY.
📺

Flatscreen Mortar Mounting

About$60

Mount TV under 60" on wall (customer provides bracket).

🚰

Sink Drip Leak Repair

Around$70

Fix leaky faucets, replace worn cartridges, and seal drips.

🔒

Door Deadbolt Install

$65

Precision deadbolt bore, strike plate alignment & mounting.

Example: scope [Sink] + [Drip Leak] + [Repair] renders to Sink Drip-Leak Repair. The same scope produces the same name regardless of whether the client arrived via Gateway A, B, or C.

Because the name is rendered from the scope, the name must not stutter. The composer's strip rule and its one real exception are in The Ad-Lib Confirmation. When a service's authored display name already ends in the action word the ad-lib appends, the trailing nominalized action is stripped before the sentence is composed; when the strip cannot cover the case (as it cannot for "Faucet Repair (Drip)"), the label itself is corrected rather than the rule being widened beyond what it was verified against.
Customer-named Entity (noun)
        + Action (verb)
        + Applicable Modifiers (conditions)
        = Service expression (curated or dynamically composed)

Domain semantics: the entity is the thing the customer names or the thing a symptom points to: a washer, fridge door, toilet seat, door lever, wall, or wall-mounted object. It is not a behavioral class such as "fixture" or "electronic device." Those classes remain useful metadata about an entity, but they are not the primary organizing axis. The action says what is to be done; modifiers describe conditions that materially affect the work.

Example:(ent.toilet_seat, act.replace, []) describes toilet-seat replacement. (ent.wall_mounted_object, act.mount, [mod.brick, mod.oversized, mod.ladder]) describes a broader mounting request without requiring a bespoke catalog tile for every object. A curated service is a named, priced, intake-aware point in this space; a dynamic service is the same composition before it has been curated.

Behavioral classes remain metadata. They can supply defaults or explain handling, but they must not replace the customer-named entity, become a competing identity system, or require every unfamiliar noun to receive a dedicated service record.

Coverage is evidence, not status. A Rule is binding whether or not automation exists. Verification records identify the mechanism and the exact claim it covers; they do not label the Rule itself as enforced, partial, or optional.
PartWhat it meansExample
EntityThe customer-named thing or component; stable identity is independent of label.ent.washer, ent.fridge_door, ent.toilet_seat
ActionThe operation requested or resolved for that entity.act.repair, act.install, act.mount
ModifierA reusable condition that changes scope, price, time, preparation, routing, or risk.mod.brick, mod.oversized, mod.ladder
ServiceA curated entity/action/modifier combination with its price, intake, and booking behavior.svc.washer_repair = ent.washer + act.repair + no modifiers
P-DOMAIN-FINITEPrincipleThe curated catalog stays finite; the service space remains compositional. Entities, actions, and modifiers form reusable domain vocabulary. Behavioral classes describe handling. A curated service is a named point in that space, while an unfamiliar request can still be resolved through the same entity/action/modifier model.

Vision outcome protected: keeping the curated catalog understandable while allowing valid combinations of reusable domain concepts to cover bespoke work.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

R-DOMAIN-SHAPERuleA Workaround Is Evidence the Shape Is Wrong

In plain English: When a consumer requires copied facts, parallel representations, or caller-side reconstruction because of source shape, the change record treats the shape as a defect and either corrects it or records an explicit, bounded exception.

Vision outcome protected: Repeated workarounds trigger examination of the underlying domain shape instead of becoming permanent caller-side exceptions.

Accomplishment: When a consumer requires copied facts, parallel representations, or caller-side reconstruction because of source shape, the change record treats the shape as a defect and either corrects it or records an explicit, bounded exception.

Serves:P-GOVERN-DEFECTCLASS · P-INVARIANT-PROPAGATE

A consumer that must work around the SSOT's shape — by copying facts into a second location, by maintaining a parallel representation, by re-deriving what the source could have provided directly — is evidence that the shape is wrong. The response is to change the shape, not to add the workaround.

Workarounds are permitted only as transitions. A transitional workaround is filed as one: its rationale states the shape change that would eliminate it, and the shape change is scheduled. A workaround without a scheduled shape change is a permanent second implementation, and is a defect under R-INVARIANT-CANONICAL.

The enforcement check scans the codebase for the specific shapes workarounds take — a caller-side || or ?? that composes a resolver return with a second source; a second copy of a value that already lives authoritatively elsewhere; an inline re-derivation of a fact that a prior step already resolved — and fails when one appears without a companion ledger entry naming the shape change that will retire it.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Repeated workarounds trigger examination of the underlying domain shape instead of becoming permanent caller-side exceptions.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: repeated workarounds trigger examination of the underlying domain shape instead of becoming permanent caller-side exceptions.

Implementation freedom — Files, modules, data structures, and graph shape may change freely when the new design preserves the domain meaning and reduces forced duplication, reconstruction, or special-case handling. A workaround is a signal to examine the shape, not a permanent exception.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that repeated workarounds trigger examination of the underlying domain shape instead of becoming permanent caller-side exceptions fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Three Related Concepts, Three Different Jobs

The derived runtime artifact exposes three implementation namespaces, and they are deliberately distinct:

NamespaceAnswersConsumed by
routing_archetypesHow is a group's intake structured — component-first, symptom-first, parent, or undetermined?The orchestrator's chain composition, the behavioral-class-driven picker, and the semantic RouteValidator
archetypesWhat is the behavioural-class default shape for a group — default routing strategy, default confidence strategy, default checkout state?Informational only today; the eventual home of inherited defaults
pricing_archetypesHow does a service's price decompose — flat, hourly, formula, diagnostic, project?Documentation for human readers; the live pricing engine reads per-service financial_engine.type and financial_engine.pricing_archetype
Only routing_archetypes and archetypes are read at runtime. pricing_archetypes is a derived, human-readable catalogue — the pricing engine reads per-service fields, not the aggregate. This distinction matters: it means a change to the aggregate catalogue cannot change a price, and a change to a per-service field can.

The Entity Is the Noun

A customer can name a specific product while the business reasons about the kind of thing it is. A "Kwikset Halifax Satin Nickel Privacy Lever" is still a Door Lever; "Andersen Window" is a Window. The product-specific details remain attributes that can matter to scope or pricing, but they do not require a new conceptual service.

Behavioral Classes Provide Behavior

Behavioral Classes allow unfamiliar phrases to inherit useful behavior from a broader class. A phrase such as "Smart Mirror" does not need a bespoke service entry merely because the exact phrase was not previously catalogued; it can inherit behavior from the appropriate broader behavioral class and enter the same resolution machinery.

Services Are Expressions, Not the Foundation

A service exists so the client can understand and book a resolved piece of work. It should not become the place where the business's entire knowledge is duplicated. The same entity/action/modifier vocabulary can produce different curated service expressions when pricing, intake, or booking behavior genuinely differs.

P-DOMAIN-SCOPEPrincipleFact Scoping Is Explicit at Three Levels

Vision outcome protected: ensuring authored facts apply only to the service shapes for which they are true, preventing broad rules from contaminating unrelated work.

A recognized fact — a smart tag, in the SSOT's own vocabulary — is not a universal statement. It is a fact about a shape of work, and its applicability is declared at three narrowing levels:

LevelWhat it saysWhen to use it
CategoryThis fact applies to every service in this category.The fact is genuinely universal to a family of work — an electrical fact, a plumbing fact.
GroupThis fact applies to this group of services, narrowing the category further.The fact is specific to a shape of work — a wall-mounting fact that does not apply to wall repairs.
Service exclusionThis fact does not apply to these specific services, even though it applies to their group.The fact applies to almost all of a group, but one real exception exists — a group whose shape contains both small and large jobs needs to say "not this specific service" rather than "not this whole group".

The three levels are deliberate, and they exist because scoping is a genuine tradeoff. A fact scoped too broadly touches work it has nothing to say about; a fact scoped too narrowly has to be re-declared for every sibling service. The exclusion layer exists because a real group can genuinely contain both the fact-applying and the fact-not-applying case — and the honest answer is neither to remove the fact from the whole group nor to author a second, less specific version of it. It is to say, at the group level, "this applies, except here."

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — ensuring authored facts apply only to the service shapes for which they are true, preventing broad rules from contaminating unrelated work.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring authored facts apply only to the service shapes for which they are true, preventing broad rules from contaminating unrelated work.

A derived field cannot silently override a field the catalog authored. Validation is a separate act governed by R-INVARIANT-DERIVATION.
