Dynamic Services & Bespoke Concepts

The catalog is finite. The set of things a client might ask for is not. BTNYC resolves this tension by modeling the business — the small, finite set of concepts, behaviors, and conditions that determine what work costs — rather than cataloguing every specific product. When a client names something the catalog has never seen, the system does not fail; it finds the closest behavioral class and prices the work from there.

A dynamic service is a real, priceable resolution that was not authored as its own named service entry. It has the same shape as a named service — an entity, a routing mode, a capability, a strategy, an action, a price — but it is composed at resolution time from the parts the catalog already has. A "smart mirror" is a mirror. A motorized-curtain request is a curtain installation with an electromechanical nuance the intake can capture.

Some supported entry modes may reach dynamic services directly. The “Other” path is an explicit route for a customer who does not find the job in the picker: a builder scoped to the selected group helps resolve the request as a dynamic service within that group. Free text reaches the same mechanism when the client's phrase maps to a behavior without mapping to a named service. The Ad-Lib Confirmation then names what was resolved in the same way it would name any other service: as a scope, not as a catalog record.

The Finite Catalog Thesis, Expanded

The platform does not attempt to enumerate the universe. It does not seek to catalog:

  • Every product
  • Every brand
  • Every model
  • Every entity
  • Every phrase
  • Every customer description

That set is unbounded. Instead the platform catalogs:

  • Behaviors
  • Capabilities
  • Conditions
  • Environments
  • Cost drivers
  • Uncertainty classes

This distinction is what allows a finite system to provide effectively unlimited coverage.

P-DOMAIN-UNCERTAINTYPrincipleCoverage scales through uncertainty modeling rather than exhaustive enumeration. The platform does not attempt to catalog every possible entity. It attempts to catalog the finite set of conditions that materially influence how work is priced, routed, scheduled, scoped, or prepared. There are infinitely many entities. There are not infinitely many reasons work becomes more expensive, takes longer, requires different preparation, or follows a different pricing path. Coverage scales through uncertainty modeling rather than entity enumeration.

Vision outcome protected: scaling coverage through honest uncertainty handling instead of pretending every possible job can be pre-enumerated.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — scaling coverage through honest uncertainty handling instead of pretending every possible job can be pre-enumerated.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: scaling coverage through honest uncertainty handling instead of pretending every possible job can be pre-enumerated.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that scaling coverage through honest uncertainty handling instead of pretending every possible job can be pre-enumerated fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Bespoke Work Is Not a Failure Condition

A bespoke request is evidence that the domain model is functioning correctly. The platform does not need perfect recognition of every object. The platform must identify the uncertainty classes that determine how the work should be scoped and priced. Novel entities therefore enter the same uncertainty-resolution machinery as familiar entities.

A previously unseen object may still be:

  • Mounted
  • Repaired
  • Installed
  • Removed
  • Diagnosed

The object's uniqueness does not require a unique pricing architecture. Only genuinely new uncertainty classes require expansion of the model.

The Shape of a Dynamic Service

A dynamic service is keyed by its resolved scope: the entity, the action, and the applicable modifiers, constrained by the group the request arrived through. The client-facing name is derived from that scope. The same scope renders the same name regardless of entry path.

A dynamic service carries a full pricing shape, a checkout state, an intake composition, and a confidence policy — the same fields a named service carries. Each of these projects from the entity/action/modifier composition, the applicable uncertainty profile, and any service-specific overrides the authored source owns. Intake is composed from entity-scoped questions and their first-class dependencies, not from a per-service chain declaration.

A dynamic service's relationship to its owning group — whether the composed service extends, replaces, or is overridden by the group's defaults for a given concern — is a resolution-time rule. It is not a second source of domain identity, and it does not exist to make the composed service into a second authored record.

What Makes a Dynamic Service Honest

R-DOMAIN-DYNCHAINA dynamic service's intake chain must match its routing mode. If a group declares itself symptom_first and its fallback asks only for a count, the customer's actual problem never gets scoped. The pairing is the design contract; a mismatch is a defect, not a variation.

Vision outcome protected: For every dynamic service, the configured intake chain agrees with its declared routing mode. Any diagnostic-first exception is explicit and still resolves all facts required for the selected component and pricing method before an exact quote is shown; a.

Accomplishment: For every dynamic service, the configured intake chain agrees with its declared routing mode. Any diagnostic-first exception is explicit and still resolves all facts required for the selected component and pricing method before an exact quote is shown; a symptom-only chain that leaves component identity unresolved fails publication validation.

In plain English: For every dynamic service, the configured intake chain agrees with its declared routing mode. Any diagnostic-first exception is explicit and still resolves all facts required for the selected component and pricing method before an exact quote is shown; a.

The Fallback Chain

When a request reaches the dynamic resolution machinery, it descends a short, auditable ladder. Each rung is a real, explicit rule — not a silent attempt:

Free-text or Other-tile request
↓
Resolve a named service?  —— yes ——→ the named service
↓ no
Resolve a group + reasonable confidence?  —— yes ——→ open the group-scoped builder
↓ no
Resolve a category at all?  —— yes ——→ generic wizard for that category
↓ no
Default fallback category, service_type, base_price

Each tier keeps whatever the customer already told us. If the group is known but no specific service is, the system opens the group's guided builder — not a blank form. If only the category is known, the generic wizard asks which group. If nothing is known, the fallback still carries a category, a service type, and a base price — the customer never reaches an unpriced dead end.

P-DOMAIN-FALLBACKPrincipleThe fallback never invents certainty it does not have. Each rung of the ladder corresponds to a real level of confidence about what the job is. The system steps down only as far as it must, and every question it asks at each level is a question that level still needs answered.

Vision outcome protected: ensuring each fallback reflects the evidence actually available and never manufactures certainty merely to complete a flow.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — ensuring each fallback reflects the evidence actually available and never manufactures certainty merely to complete a flow.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring each fallback reflects the evidence actually available and never manufactures certainty merely to complete a flow.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that ensuring each fallback reflects the evidence actually available and never manufactures certainty merely to complete a flow fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

When a Phrase Deserves a Named Service

The boundary between named services and dynamic resolution is a design decision, not a technical one. Some behaviors deserve to be named — because they carry their own specific intake requirements, their own pricing shape, or their own customer-facing vocabulary. Others do not; they are covered generically, and the system's coverage scales with the breadth of its behavioral model rather than the depth of its catalog.

A phrase deserves its own named service when at least one of the following is true:

  • It has a distinct, authored pricing shape that a generic formula cannot express (the PAX wardrobe formula; the tile repair formula).
  • It carries a distinct, real intake requirement that no sibling service can share (a fixture-specific question; a material-specific one).
  • It has its own, named, client-facing vocabulary — a customer says "the dishwasher repair," not "a sink repair that involves a dishwasher."
  • It carries a regulatory, safety, or warranty distinction that must be honoured separately.
The judgment is not "can this be a dynamic service?" — almost anything can. The judgment is "does the client experience improve, or does the pricing become more honest, by authoring a real service here?" When the answer is no, a dynamic service with a well-authored chain is the better answer, because it stays in sync with the concepts the rest of the catalog already expresses.

What Dynamic Services Buy

The mechanism keeps the catalog honest at three levels simultaneously:

  • Coverage without sprawl. A customer naming a "smart mirror" or a "dining nook" does not require a product-team decision to author a new service. The behavioural model covers it, and the dynamic service expresses the resolved work.
  • Consistency under ambiguity. Two customers describing the same job in different words reach the same dynamic service, because both were routed by the same group-and-routing-mode logic. The "Other" tile is not a blank form; it is a constrained composer.
  • Honest scope across entry paths. The dynamic service's own pricing shape is the same one a named service would use. A dynamic service is not a degraded second class — it is the same class, composed differently.

The Two Failure Modes Worth Naming

Two failure modes recur, and both have a real, checkable answer.
  • The naked quantity trap. A dynamic service whose only question is "how many?" under a symptom-first or component-first group. The fix is to ask the shape question first, then quantity if the shape still warrants it. A dynamic service with a bare count is a defect, not a simplification.
  • The cross-category bleed. A dynamic service authored under one group whose chain carries questions from another category's vocabulary. The fix is to check the chain against the group's own real services, not against the category's general fallback.

A dynamic service is real when it is reachable, its chain matches its routing mode, and its pricing shape is honest. Those three tests are the whole mechanism.

Dynamic Service Chains Must Match Their Routing Mode

A dynamic service is only as honest as the questions it asks. If a group declares itself symptom_first, but the chain it runs asks only a quantity, the customer's actual problem never gets scoped. The table below is the enforcement rule: any dynamic service's chain must be consistent with its declared routing mode, or the routing mode is wrong or the chain is.

Routing mode and chain shape are separate fields. The authored SSOT mapping selects the permitted chain shape for a routing mode and service family; it may be conditional or one-to-many, so the Charter does not invent a universal one-to-one mapping. Validation rejects a mode/shape pair not declared by that mapping.

Chain shapeChain must includeChain must NOT be
symptom_first (major appliances)A real symptom questionA bare quantity or generic surface-type question
component_first (door hardware, plumbing fixtures, wall-mounted objects, electrical fixtures, furniture)A "what specifically" component/fixture question before quote precision is claimed. A symptom-shaped diagnostic prelude is allowed only when explicitly declared and the chain still resolves the component/fixture before pricing.A symptom-only chain that never resolves the component/fixture, or a bare quantity question alone
surface_repair (walls, floors, drywall)A surface-type questionA symptom-shaped question
quantity_driven (pure count services)Only quantity / item-countAny question that does not affect count

This rule is what keeps the dynamic mechanism trustworthy. A dynamic service is a promise that the system can price a job it has never seen; that promise is kept only if the chain the customer walks reflects the shape the routing mode claims. Enforcing the pairing is what makes "the catalog stays finite, coverage does not" a design fact rather than a wish.

Part III
Complexity
Complexity exists because it exposes uncertainty. What actually varies in a job — and why a single scalar cannot capture it.

The Multi-Dimensional Complexity Model

Complexity is not a score.

Complexity is not confidence.

Complexity is not uncertainty.

Complexity is the set of characteristics that determine how much uncertainty can legitimately exist about a job.

The platform's purpose is not to determine whether a job is complex. The platform's purpose is to determine what must be known in order to price the job honestly.

Complexity therefore exists because it exposes uncertainty. The relationship is:

Complexity ↓ Exposes Uncertainty ↓ Requires Evidence ↓ Creates Confidence ↓ Unlocks Pricing Precision

This sequence is the canonical relationship between Complexity, Confidence, and Friction.

Complexity never directly lowers confidence. Complexity increases the amount of uncertainty that may legitimately exist. The confidence system itself remains unchanged. Only the uncertainty landscape changes.

P-CONF-COMPLEXITYPrincipleComplexity does not directly reduce confidence. Complexity determines the job shape and therefore which uncertainty classes may be relevant. More complex work may require retirement of more or different uncertainty before the same pricing precision is justified. The confidence calculation remains constant; the active uncertainty landscape and, where explicitly specified, the policy threshold may change.

Vision outcome protected: preventing complexity from being misused as a proxy for uncertainty or confidence.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — preventing complexity from being misused as a proxy for uncertainty or confidence.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: preventing complexity from being misused as a proxy for uncertainty or confidence.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that preventing complexity from being misused as a proxy for uncertainty or confidence fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

Complexity Is Not One Number

Complexity is a description of the job's shape, not a universal score. A job can differ in quantity, access, environment, material handling, scope, trip count, structural conditions, installation conditions, or safety conditions without becoming a different kind of work in every respect.

These are job-shape dimensions. They are evidence about what the job actually is and what uncertainty may be relevant. They are not themselves the three classification axes.

Job-shape dimensionExampleWhat it may affect
VolumeOne cabinet pull vs. twenty pullsDuration, batching, preparation, and explicitly authored batch-risk conditions.
AccessGround-floor hallway vs. narrow stairwellLabor, duration, preparation, pricing, or safety.
EnvironmentOpen floor vs. finished white carpetProtection, preparation, labor, or price.
Material handlingCustomer-supplied stock vs. special-order materialTrips, procurement, duration, and price.
ScopeSwap vs. new installation vs. repairCapabilities, labor, pricing, and routing.
Trip countOne visit vs. measure/order/installScheduling and per-visit pricing.
Structural / installation conditionsSound substrate vs. damaged framingSkill, risk, labor, materials, and scope.
Safety / regulatory conditionsNormal access vs. active electrical or structural workRisk, routing, preparation, and permissible service path.
Job-shape dimensions must not be collapsed into an arbitrary scalar complexity score. The system may use authored classifications or derived policy states where the owning specification defines them, but the Charter does not authorize a hidden universal complexity number.

The same nominal service can therefore occupy very different positions in job-shape space. The correct response is to resolve the relevant facts and uncertainties, not to manufacture a single "complexity score" that then drives every downstream decision.

Complexity Classification — Independent Dimensions

Every resolved service explicitly declares each complexity dimension required by the governing model. The present core dimensions are skill, risk, and batch-risk; they remain independent, and no dimension is inferred from another. New dimensions may be added and existing dimensions retired through a deliberate, compatibility-aware domain and schema evolution that defines meaning, defaults, evidence effects, and treatment of existing profiles.

Complexity is multi-dimensional — the previous section establishes that. This section names the dimensions, and names the rule that keeps them independent: skill, risk, and volume are different questions, and treating any one of them as a proxy for another is the defect this section exists to prevent.

When independent dimensions are not explicit and enforced, a single tier is pressured to answer several different questions. The result is a misleading classification: the number of items does not by itself determine skill, a larger batch is not automatically more hazardous, and longer duration does not by itself prove greater complexity. The model exists to keep these questions distinct while allowing its vocabulary and dimensions to evolve deliberately.

The present core dimensions are orthogonal, explicitly declared, and independently enforced; the model may evolve through governed extension or retirement of dimensions.

AxisQuestion it answersWhat it drivesWhat it never drives
Skill tierHow hard is one of these to do correctly?Hourly rate, skill framing, which pricing formulas may applyDuration, scheduling, checkout state
Risk tierHow much can go wrong, or be regulated, on a single instance?Safety gating, diagnostic eligibility, which checkout states are permissibleSkill tier, duration, batch behavior
Batch riskDoes doing this N times compound the cost of one mistake?Whether volume raises the risk tierSkill tier, per-instance risk

Duration is derived from the resolved work shape and quantity; it is not itself a complexity dimension. The independent complexity dimensions describe the job; duration describes the time consequence under the applicable policy.

R-COMPLEX-AXESRuleComplexity Dimensions Remain Orthogonal and Explicit

In plain English: Skill, risk, and batch risk are separate decisions; changing one must not silently change the others.

Vision outcome protected: Skill, risk, and batch risk are separate decisions; changing one must not silently change the others.

At each resolution checkpoint, every declared dimension has exactly one effective, valid value. The current core dimensions are skill, risk, and batch-risk. Their values may evolve with explicit evidence or a declared change in the resolved job shape, but changing one dimension never silently changes another. Adding or retiring a dimension requires an explicit compatibility-aware model change; it must not invalidate existing profiles by accident.

Serves:P-COMPLEX-DYNAMIC

Every resolved service explicitly declares each complexity dimension required by the governing model. The current core dimensions are skill, risk, and batch-risk; they remain independent, and no dimension is inferred from another. A future dimension may be added or an existing one retired only through an explicit domain/schema/resolution change that defines its meaning, evidence effects, defaults, compatibility, and treatment of existing profiles.

The present core dimensions are:

complexity declaration:
  tier:                  routine | skilled | specialized | project
  risk classification:             R0 | R1 | R2 | R3
  batch-risk flag:  true | false

Examples illustrate why dimensions must remain independent: a reversible routine swap may have low risk and no compounding batch exposure; an installation requiring new holes may involve greater skill and a different batch-risk decision; a hazardous repair may require a higher risk classification regardless of duration. These are conceptual examples, not fixed service records or a closed set of combinations.

Risk classification describes the consequence and exposure of the work, independently of skill or duration. Its vocabulary may distinguish no material hazard, limited hazard, active or regulated hazard, and life-safety exposure. The owning policy and schema define the exact labels, cutoffs, and any additional categories.

Batch risk describes whether a per-instance mistake becomes materially more costly or irreversible across repeated instances. The representation may evolve, but the meaning must remain distinct from quantity itself: quantity alone does not make the flag true.

The enforcement. A structural test reads every service's complexity declaration, confirms all three keys are present, and confirms each is a legal value. A resolver test confirms no code path derives one axis from another — no tier = f(units), no risk classification = f(minutes).

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Skill, risk, and batch risk are separate decisions; changing one must not silently change the others.

Harm prevented — A single classification can conceal that skill, risk, and batch exposure are different questions, leading to unsupported preparation, pricing, or safety decisions.

Implementation freedom — The present dimensions are independent concepts, not a forever-closed list. A future dimension may be added or one retired through an explicit domain/schema/resolver change that defines its meaning, evidence effects, defaults, compatibility, and treatment of existing profiles. No existing profile should become invalid merely because the model evolves.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a newly observed job shape or proposed complexity dimension cannot be represented independently, or evidence shows one dimension is being used as a proxy for another. Amend the mechanism through governance; do not bypass the commitment silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

R-COMPLEX-VOLUMERuleVolume Does Not Promote Skill or Risk by Itself

In plain English: Increasing only quantity or duration leaves skill and risk classifications unchanged; any classification change requires a separately stated physical, regulatory, or authored batch-risk condition.

Vision outcome protected: Increasing only quantity or duration leaves skill and risk classifications unchanged; any classification change requires a separately stated physical, regulatory, or authored batch-risk condition.

Accomplishment: Increasing only quantity or duration leaves skill and risk classifications unchanged; any classification change requires a separately stated physical, regulatory, or authored batch-risk condition.

Purpose: Prevent quantity and duration from becoming hidden proxies for classification.

Quantity never promotes skill or risk merely because there are more units. Quantity may change duration, preparation, or a separately authored batch-risk condition. A change in risk classification must be justified by a change in the physical or regulatory consequences of the resolved job, not by arithmetic alone.

Duration is a derived scheduling consequence. It may change the checkout state or the number of visits without changing what one instance requires to be done correctly or what one instance can cause if done incorrectly.

quantity changes → recompute job shape
job shape → apply authored classification rules
classification → skill / risk / batch-risk
quantity + classification → duration / scheduling

Never:
  skill_tier := f(quantity)
  risk classification  := f(quantity)
  skill_tier := f(minutes)
  risk classification  := f(minutes)

Example. Twenty ordinary knob swaps can remain routine / R0 / false while duration increases. Ten knob installations that involve new drilling can remain skilled / R1 / true while duration increases. If a larger batch genuinely changes risk because the physical consequences of an error compound, the owning specification may declare that condition explicitly; the quantity itself is not the reason.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Increasing only quantity or duration leaves skill and risk classifications unchanged; any classification change requires a separately stated physical, regulatory, or authored batch-risk condition.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: increasing only quantity or duration leaves skill and risk classifications unchanged; any classification change requires a separately stated physical, regulatory, or authored batch-risk condition.

Implementation freedom — Quantity and duration may affect price, preparation, or a separately evidenced batch-risk decision; they must not silently stand in for skill or hazard. New quantity-sensitive behavior is allowed when its own causal rule is explicit.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that increasing only quantity or duration leaves skill and risk classifications unchanged; any classification change requires a separately stated physical, regulatory, or authored batch-risk condition fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

R-COMPLEX-COMPOUNDSRuleBatch Risk Compounds Only When Errors Are Expensive

In plain English: A batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance error becomes materially more costly or irreversible across the batch; routine reversible swaps remain false regardless of quantity.

Vision outcome protected: A batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance error becomes materially more costly or irreversible across the batch; routine reversible swaps remain false regardless of quantity.

Accomplishment: A batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance error becomes materially more costly or irreversible across the batch; routine reversible swaps remain false regardless of quantity.

Serves:P-COMPLEX-FURNITURE · P-COMPLEX-DYNAMIC

batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance mistake is irreversible or expensive. A swap, a bulb, a plug-in, a reseated connection — no compounding, regardless of quantity. A drilled hole, a cut, an adhered surface, a sealed joint, a mis-sized order — compounds with each unit.

The distinction is not about the item's value. It is about the reversibility of the mistake. A customer with twenty loose knobs after a bad swap has twenty items to tighten. A customer with one bad hole out of twenty-five installs has a door that needs replacing. The knob does not compound because the fix is a screwdriver. The hole compounds because the fix is a new door.

The mechanical proxies — the signals the enforcement reads from the SSOT to derive the flag rather than asking authors to hand-author it — are:

Signal in SSOTImplication
formula_override: "item_count_overflow_formula" with an overflow coefficient defined by the owning pricing specification. The coefficient is authored data, not a Charter constant.Compounds — each unit is nearly a full job, not a batch increment
A pricing formula declares per-unit penalty minutes — not merely per-unit work minutesCompounds — the formula's own author believed the N-th unit carries non-linear risk
required_materials includes a single-use consumable: wax ring, gasket, adhesive, mastic, membraneCompounds — one mistake wastes a consumable that cannot be reused
An intake chain contains a scope-changing branch whose answer reveals a materially different job shapeCompounds — the batch has heterogeneous instances, and one of them can promote the whole job

None of these signals present: the service does not compound. The flag is derived, not authored. An author who believes a service compounds must produce the signal — a per-unit penalty, a single-use consumable, an overflow rate — that justifies it. Belief alone is not a signal.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — A batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance error becomes materially more costly or irreversible across the batch; routine reversible swaps remain false regardless of quantity.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: a batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance error becomes materially more costly or irreversible across the batch; routine reversible swaps remain false regardless of quantity.

Implementation freedom — The model may evolve its risk vocabulary and evidence, but the batch flag must continue to mean that errors compound materially across instances—not merely that the quantity is large.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a batch-risk flag is true only when a per-instance error becomes materially more costly or irreversible across the batch; routine reversible swaps remain false regardless of quantity fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

R-PRICE-DURATIONSTATERuleTier and Checkout State Are Independent Decisions

In plain English: The price presentation must reflect the resolved duration; long work cannot be disguised as a bounded flat-rate visit.

Vision outcome protected: The price presentation must reflect the resolved duration; long work cannot be disguised as a bounded flat-rate visit.

Accomplishment: The same resolved scope can retain its skill tier while checkout presentation changes based on authored duration policy; duration alone never changes skill or risk tier.

Serves:P-COMPLEX-DYNAMIC

A resolved job whose authoritative minimum-duration estimate exceeds the applicable policy threshold may not be presented as a bounded flat-rate visit, even if its skill tier is routine. The job must use a registered checkout presentation that honestly represents its duration and pricing arrangement. Exact field paths, thresholds, and permitted state vocabulary belong to the owning specification and schema; this Charter does not freeze their names or values.

The durable lesson is to prevent a client from being quoted a bounded flat rate for work whose resolved duration exceeds the applicable policy threshold. The threshold is a policy value owned by the authoritative specification, not a permanent number in the Charter. The mechanism is the checkout state, not the skill tier.

The decision uses the authoritative minimum-duration estimate for the resolved quote. Operational expected-duration metadata is not a substitute for the quote’s resolved estimate. If that estimate is absent or invalid, the system must not assume the job is below the threshold; it must use a conservative, policy-supported path or surface an authoring/validation error.

A resolved job whose authoritative minimum-duration estimate exceeds the owning policy’s threshold may not resolve to a checkout presentation that implies a small, bounded visit. It must use a suitable presentation registered in the governing schema. If no registered presentation honestly expresses the job’s duration and pricing arrangement, the schema and policy must be deliberately extended before the service is published; the runtime must not invent an unregistered state.

The tier does not change. Twenty knob swaps stay routine. The checkout state changes because the client is being asked to book an hour of work, and the price should be presented as an hour of work, not as a flat fee that happens to be for many small things. The tier answers what kind of work is this; the checkout state answers how do we tell the client about the time and the price. Different questions, different answers, correctly decoupled.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — The price presentation must reflect the resolved duration; long work cannot be disguised as a bounded flat-rate visit.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: the price presentation must reflect the resolved duration; long work cannot be disguised as a bounded flat-rate visit.

Implementation freedom — Thresholds, pricing methods, and supported checkout states are policy/schema concerns and may change. The enduring requirement is that presentation honestly reflects the resolved duration and pricing arrangement; no unregistered state or invented threshold is permitted.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that the price presentation must reflect the resolved duration; long work cannot be disguised as a bounded flat-rate visit fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

P-COMPLEX-DYNAMICPrincipleThe Axes Evolve as the Job Reveals Itself

Vision outcome protected: allowing the system’s understanding of a job to become more precise as evidence arrives without conflating skill, risk, volume, or duration.

Complexity is not a label assigned at resolution and never revisited. A job's skill tier, risk tier, and batch-risk flag may each be promoted — and, in rare cases, demoted — as the client's answers, the navigation path, or the intake chain reveal more about the actual shape of the work.

This principle is not new doctrine. It is the complexity chapter's existing belief — "The selected service does not determine the complete intake by itself. As the client answers questions, the system may learn that the actual job is simpler, more complex, or materially different from the initial baseline" — applied to the independent complexity dimensions defined by the model. The belief was already in the Charter; the axes it applies to were not. This principle names them.

The evolution is not free-form. Each axis has a defined set of signals that can move it:

AxisSignals that promote itSignals that demote it
Skill tierAn intake answer carries complexity_override: skilled or specialized. A formula override fires. An authored branch reaches a sub-job with its own higher baseline. A detected tag carries escalate_complexity. A structural condition is revealed — a commercial fixture, a hidden material, a non-standard size.An answer confirms the standard shape — a residential fixture, a standard size, an accessible location. A scope-changing branch reaches a simpler-than-baseline sub-job. Rare and deliberate; the base tier is the floor for most services.
Risk tierA hazard tag activates — #emergency, #water_hazard, #board_level, #data_recovery, #flushometer. A symptom answer reveals active water, active electrical, or gas. A scope-changing branch reveals a regulated condition.A hazard-precluding answer arrives — no water present, no gas odor, a standard fixture confirmed. Rare; most risk signals are one-way — once revealed, they stay revealed.
Batch riskA scope-changing branch reveals a materially different job shape nested inside the batch — one of the twenty pieces needs a real repair, not a swap.An answer confirms the batch is homogeneous and each instance is the same shape as the baseline.

The composition follows the current resolved job shape. Earlier classification is not authority over later evidence. A new fact may promote, demote, or otherwise change an axis when it changes the physical, regulatory, or scope conditions on which that axis rests. Confidence may legitimately decline when new uncertainty is revealed; classification may likewise change when the resolved job changes. The system must not preserve an incorrect classification merely because it was previously selected.

Dynamic services are governed by the same independently declared complexity dimensions. A dynamically resolved service — a "smart mirror" that resolves to a mirror with a light — inherits the axes of the group it resolved into, then adjusts them per the signals above. A dynamic service whose group is component_first / R0 is routine / R0 / false unless and until a signal promotes it. Dynamic services do not get a special complexity regime; they get the same regime, applied at resolution time from the parts the catalog already carries.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — allowing the system’s understanding of a job to become more precise as evidence arrives without conflating skill, risk, volume, or duration.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: allowing the system’s understanding of a job to become more precise as evidence arrives without conflating skill, risk, volume, or duration.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that allowing the system’s understanding of a job to become more precise as evidence arrives without conflating skill, risk, volume, or duration fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

P-COMPLEX-FURNITUREPrincipleAssembly Has Its Own Metric

Vision outcome protected: classifying assembly work according to the actual work involved rather than forcing it into an unrelated complexity measure.

Furniture assembly is a distinct complexity family. A customer assembling ten identical side tables is not doing ten different jobs; they are doing one job ten times. The standard volume rule, which is conservative because field-service jobs genuinely change with quantity, is too conservative for assembly, where quantity often changes only time.

Assembly gets its own matrix. The axes remain — skill, risk, batch risk — but the thresholds for routine are relaxed in a way the other families do not allow:

Assembly classCriteriaResulting tier
A0 — SimpleIdentical SKU, within the authored per-item, total-duration, and quantity limits; no wall anchoring, electrical work, cutting, two-person lift, or site modification.routine permitted even above the standard volume and duration ceilings
A1 — Standard flat-packMixed SKUs, a total duration or quantity beyond the authored A0 limits, or one mild complication.skilled minimum
A2 — ComplexConfigurable or large assembly, wall anchoring, a required two-person lift, or work beyond the authored standard limits.specialized minimum
A3 — Custom / built-inSite measurement, cutting, electrical integration, structural involvement, permit, multi-visitproject minimum

The exception is conditional, not absolute. If any A0 condition fails — a single mixed SKU, a single wall anchor, a single electrical connection, a single cut — the standard rules apply and the tier is computed from the axes as they are, not from the assembly ceiling. The exception exists because assembly is often repetitive in a way the rest of the catalog is not; it does not exist to exempt assembly from the axes, only to give it a metric that reflects the shape it actually has.

The existing furniture_assembly_flat_pack, furniture_disassembly_for_moving, and pax_wardrobe_assembly services declare their assembly class explicitly. A dynamic service that resolves into the assembly family declares it at resolution, from the parts the group already carries.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — classifying assembly work according to the actual work involved rather than forcing it into an unrelated complexity measure.

Harm prevented — Work can be classified at the wrong difficulty, risk, or volume, so the resulting scope, preparation, or price no longer reflects the physical job. The specific commitment at risk is: classifying assembly work according to the actual work involved rather than forcing it into an unrelated complexity measure.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that classifying assembly work according to the actual work involved rather than forcing it into an unrelated complexity measure fails under a newly observed job shape or proposed complexity dimension, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

A Worked Example, Start to Finish

The cabinet knob or pull install is the service that produced this amendment. Read it through the axes and the doctrine becomes concrete.

It is two jobs. The intake chain's first step is install_type, whose two answers — "Swapping existing hardware (same holes)" and "New holes needed (measure, drill, mark)" — are not two versions of one job. They are two different jobs that share a service record. The axes declare the difference.

AxisSwapNew holes
Skill tierroutineskilled
Risk tierR0R1
Batch risk compoundsfalsetrue
Per-instance minutes, from hardware_install_formula36
Twenty of them takeIllustrative duration only; the owning specification supplies the estimate.Illustrative duration only; the owning specification supplies the estimate.
Tier after twentyroutine, unchangedskilled, unchanged
Risk tier after twentyR0, unchanged — nothing compoundsR2, stepped — one bad hole among twenty is a door
Checkout state after the duration thresholdA suitable non-flat checkout state registered in the SSOTA suitable non-flat checkout state registered in the SSOT

Target-behavior illustration, not a claim about the current SSOT: twenty swaps and twenty installs may both cross the duration threshold, but duration alone must not promote skill or risk. If authored evidence declares a routine, low-risk swap and a skilled, higher-risk install, the resolved outcomes can be routine / R0 for the swaps and skilled / R2 for the installs. That divergence exists only when the SSOT actually declares the relevant baseline and answer-level overrides. Without those declarations, the runtime must preserve the authored baseline; the Charter example cannot manufacture behavior that the data does not implement. The tier answers the skill question, the risk tier answers the safety question, and the checkout state answers the duration/presentation question.

Part IV
Confidence & Uncertainty
Confidence measures the proportion of relevant uncertainty retired. The mathematical heart of the platform.

Confidence & Uncertainty

Price cannot be known until complexity is known. Complexity cannot be known until enough information is available. But asking for information has a cost.

The system asks only enough questions to close the confidence gap required for the price — and no more. This is the mathematical heart of the entire platform.

Confidence Is Not Probability

BTNYC does not model confidence as a probability.

The platform does not attempt to predict:

"There is an 82% chance this quote is correct."

Instead it models:

"0.82 of the pricing-relevant uncertainty (82% is a display-only equivalent) required for this pricing path has been retired."

This distinction is deliberate.

Probability attempts to predict correctness. Confidence measures evidence completeness.

The system does not claim it knows whether unseen conditions exist. It claims it knows which meaningful uncertainties remain unresolved.

P-CONF-UNCERTAINTYPrincipleConfidence is not a prediction. Confidence is the proportion of relevant uncertainty retired. The system never expresses confidence as a statistical claim regarding quote correctness. Confidence is an evidence-completeness measure.

Vision outcome protected: grounding confidence in relevant uncertainty retired rather than presenting it as statistical probability.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — grounding confidence in relevant uncertainty retired rather than presenting it as statistical probability.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: grounding confidence in relevant uncertainty retired rather than presenting it as statistical probability.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that grounding confidence in relevant uncertainty retired rather than presenting it as statistical probability fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

Uncertainty Is the Primitive

Customers do not pay for questions. Customers pay for work.

Questions exist only because they retire uncertainty about work. The platform therefore tracks uncertainty directly. Confidence is derived from uncertainty retirement. Questions are merely one mechanism that may retire uncertainty. Photographs, documents, model numbers, previous answers, navigation context, and inferred facts may do the same.

Intent Confidence and Scope Confidence

The platform maintains two separate confidence measures.

  • Intent confidence (Cᵢ) answers: Do we understand what work the customer wants?
  • Scope confidence (Cₓ) answers: Do we understand the conditions under which that work will occur?

These concerns are intentionally separate.

A request may have high Intent Confidence and low Scope Confidence. The platform may know exactly what the customer wants while knowing very little about the conditions that determine cost.

Notation.Cᵢ is intent confidence on a 0–1 scale; Cₓ is scope confidence on a 0–1 scale; τₓ is the required scope-confidence threshold for the active resolution/pricing policy. These are normalized confidence measures, not probabilities of an outcome.

Confidence Is Not Question Count

Confidence must never be calculated from questions answered divided by total questions. This calculation is prohibited.

Questions are not inherently valuable. Only uncertainty retirement is valuable. Five low-impact answers may retire less uncertainty than one high-impact answer. The system therefore evaluates uncertainty classes rather than questionnaire completion.

P-CONF-NOQUESTIONMATHPrincipleConfidence is not derived from question counts. Confidence derives from retired uncertainty. A service whose client answered more questions is not necessarily better understood. Question quantity and confidence are not equivalent.

Vision outcome protected: preventing question count from masquerading as evidence that a request is understood.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — preventing question count from masquerading as evidence that a request is understood.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: preventing question count from masquerading as evidence that a request is understood.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that preventing question count from masquerading as evidence that a request is understood fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

Uncertainty Classes

The platform reasons about uncertainty classes: material unknowns that can affect price, labor, routing, scheduling, preparation, safety, or the quote route. Their definitions belong to the entity because the underlying unknown can be shared across actions. Each service must explicitly declare which entity-owned classes apply to its pricing path, how they are weighted or otherwise made relevant, and which confidence policy governs the route. The service references the definitions; it does not silently inherit an empty set.

Every declared class identifies explicit retirement paths. A path names the question/evidence and, where not every answer closes the uncertainty, the qualifying option or predicate. A generic question ID is sufficient only when every valid substantive answer actually retires that class. “Not sure,” diagnostic, and non-retiring fallback options must not be treated as retirement merely because the question was answered. A class can be retired only when the resulting resolved state demonstrates that the material uncertainty is closed. Questions may retire one or more classes, reveal additional classes, or retire nothing. An answer that changes no relevant outcome contributes no confidence.

Missing declaration is invalid, not confidence. A service with a confidence bar but no applicable uncertainty profile or confidence policy is not 100% confident by default. The schema/compiler must reject it or mark the service explicitly not quote-ready under a declared diagnostic policy. A service whose declared classes cannot reach its exact/range threshold through its declared retirement paths is also invalid. This is enforced by R-CONF-DECLARED.

Confidence Accounting — The Live Uncertainty Ledger

The canonical relationship is:

Complexity → defines the relevant uncertainty landscape → Evidence → retires or reveals uncertainty → Confidence → permits pricing precision

This is an accounting model, not a scalar complexity formula. Complexity remains multi-dimensional. The system does not collapse skill, risk, batch risk, duration, access, environment, material handling, structural conditions, or other job characteristics into one universal number.

At resolution step t, define:

U(t) = the set of uncertainty classes currently relevant to the resolved job and pricing path
R(t) = the subset of U(t) whose uncertainty has actually been retired
O(t) = U(t) − R(t), the currently open uncertainty
w(u) = the positive relevance weight assigned to uncertainty class u
τₓ(t) = the active scope-confidence threshold from explicit policy

The important distinction is that U(t) contains both retired and open classes that remain relevant to the current pricing path. When a class becomes irrelevant because a scope-changing branch closes it out, it leaves U(t). When new evidence reveals a previously unrecognized material condition, the newly relevant class enters U(t). A retired class does not disappear merely because it was retired; otherwise the denominator would shrink simply because the system learned something and the accounting would manufacture confidence.

The canonical scope-confidence calculation is:

Cₓ(t) = Σ w(u) for u ∈ R(t)
       ―――――――――――――――――――――――――――――
         Σ w(u) for u ∈ U(t)

If the active uncertainty set is empty, Cₓ(t) is complete by definition. The result is normalized to 0–1. The numerator contains only uncertainty whose retirement is supported by the resulting downstream state. An answer that records a fact but leaves every material outcome unchanged does not move R(t) and contributes no confidence.

What Counts as Retirement

Retirement is a property of the resolved outcome, not of the question that produced it. An evidence event may retire one class, several classes, reveal new classes, retire and reveal classes at the same time, or retire nothing. The event is evaluated by comparing the resolved state before and after the evidence is applied.

A retirement is mechanically defensible when the evidence closes a material uncertainty that the active resolution can no longer vary: it changes a pricing-relevant modifier, a labor/duration consequence, a scope-changing branch, a routing or safety condition, a scheduling condition, or required preparation. The exact downstream fields belong to the owning resolution specification; the Charter does not prescribe a universal tuple beyond the requirement that the claimed retirement be observable in the resolved result.

An answer whose options all resolve to the same downstream state has not retired that uncertainty. Recording the answer is still valid if the business wants the fact for another reason, but the answer receives no scope-confidence credit merely for existing.

Weights Are Authored Data, Not Hidden Code

w(u) is the relevance weight of an uncertainty class for the active pricing path. The weight is an authored or explicitly derived SSOT/specification value. It is not a secret coefficient embedded in JavaScript, and it is not a universal constant of the Charter.

Weights may be informed by the magnitude and reach of the uncertainty — for example, an uncertainty capable of changing a scope branch and a material fee may deserve more weight than one that only changes a preparation note — but the derivation method is not itself a Charter constant. The owning specification must say how weights are assigned or derived for a given service family.

How Complexity Dimensions Enter the Accounting Model

Complexity dimensions do not become a second confidence formula. They are part of the resolved job shape that determines which uncertainty classes are relevant and which policy applies.

  • Skill tier describes how difficult one instance is. It may activate uncertainty about labor, technique, materials, or scope, but it does not directly subtract confidence.
  • Risk tier describes what can go wrong or what is regulated on one instance. It may activate safety, routing, scheduling, or preparation uncertainty. A risk tier may also affect τₓ if and only if the owning policy explicitly says so.
  • Batch risk describes whether quantity can compound the cost of a per-instance mistake. When the flag and quantity make heterogeneity material, the active uncertainty set may gain a batch-uniformity class. Quantity still does not promote skill by itself.
  • Duration remains a derived consequence. It may affect labor, scheduling, and checkout state, but it is not a fourth complexity axis and must not be used as a proxy for skill.

The resulting relationship is:

resolved job shape
      ↓
skill / risk / batch-risk + other relevant characteristics
      ↓
determine which uncertainty classes are applicable
      ↓
evidence retires or reveals those classes
      ↓
Cₓ is recomputed
      ↓
τₓ and precision policy are applied from explicit policy

This means Complexity can legitimately influence the confidence result indirectly. If new evidence changes the job shape, the applicable uncertainty set may change and the denominator may change. That is not direct complexity-to-confidence arithmetic; it is the intended state transition through uncertainty.

Evidence Events, Not Questions, Are the Unit of Progress

Evidence can arrive through navigation, NLP, direct catalog selection, chips, a guided builder, a structured intake answer, a photograph, a document, a model number, or another supported mechanism. These are input mechanisms, not separate confidence models.

Every mechanism produces or updates canonical BookingContext facts/evidence. The orchestrator then resolves the resulting state. No gateway, parser, picker, chip handler, or intake module may independently add scope-confidence points.

R-CONF-ACCOUNTINGScope confidence is the weighted proportion of relevant uncertainty retired. The canonical confidence implementation must derive Cₓ from the active uncertainty ledger and evidence state, using SSOT/specification weights, rather than from question count, module count, answer order, or a fixed legacy confidence-gain metadata increment. A test must construct equivalent evidence through multiple supported entry mechanisms and assert identical U, retired classes, Cₓ, and permitted precision. A second test must include an answer that changes no downstream outcome and assert that it contributes no scope-confidence retirement. Any existing legacy confidence-gain metadata field is non-authoritative legacy metadata unless the owning specification explicitly defines it as a weight for an uncertainty class; it must never be consumed as a direct Cₓ increment.

Vision outcome protected: Confidence is the weighted share of relevant uncertainty retired by evidence, not a count of questions.

Accomplishment: Recomputing scope confidence from the active uncertainty ledger yields the same result at every call site; adding or retiring a relevant uncertainty changes the result according to its declared weight.

Historical evidence: Battle 10 — Fix a collision at the data level when you can

In plain English: Confidence is the weighted share of relevant uncertainty retired by evidence, not a count of questions.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Confidence is the weighted share of relevant uncertainty retired by evidence, not a count of questions.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: confidence is the weighted share of relevant uncertainty retired by evidence, not a count of questions.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that confidence is the weighted share of relevant uncertainty retired by evidence, not a count of questions fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

R-CONF-COMPLEXITYComplexity may influence confidence only through the active uncertainty landscape and explicit threshold policy. Complexity must not directly add or subtract Cₓ. If two resolved states have the same active uncertainty classes, the same weights, and the same evidence retirement state, their Cₓ must be identical even if their complexity profile differs in a way that does not change those inputs. If complexity changes the relevant uncertainty set, the resulting denominator may change. If complexity changes the applicable policy threshold, τₓ may change. The mapping from complexity axes to threshold is an explicit SSOT/specification decision, not a hidden Charter formula.

Vision outcome protected: Holding the uncertainty ledger and threshold policy fixed while changing only a complexity label leaves scope confidence unchanged; a direct complexity-to-confidence adjustment fails verification.

Accomplishment: Holding the uncertainty ledger and threshold policy fixed while changing only a complexity label leaves scope confidence unchanged; a direct complexity-to-confidence adjustment fails verification.

In plain English: Holding the uncertainty ledger and threshold policy fixed while changing only a complexity label leaves scope confidence unchanged; a direct complexity-to-confidence adjustment fails verification.

Vision outcome — Holding the uncertainty ledger and threshold policy fixed while changing only a complexity label leaves scope confidence unchanged; a direct complexity-to-confidence adjustment fails verification.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: holding the uncertainty ledger and threshold policy fixed while changing only a complexity label leaves scope confidence unchanged; a direct complexity-to-confidence adjustment fails verification.

Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

Dynamic Uncertainty

Uncertainty is not fixed. The customer's answers may reveal additional uncertainty. The platform therefore permits confidence to decrease.

Example:

  1. Initial request: Mount a television. Potential uncertainties: Wall type; Television size; Mount availability.
  2. Customer later answers: Above a fireplace. Additional uncertainties emerge: Masonry drilling; Heat exposure; Elevated mounting conditions; Cable-routing constraints.

Total uncertainty increases. Confidence may decline.

This behavior is correct. The system understands the job more accurately than it did previously.

P-CONF-DYNAMICPrincipleConfidence may decrease as evidence arrives. Evidence may reveal new uncertainty classes. When total relevant uncertainty increases, confidence may legitimately decline. The objective is not monotonic confidence growth. The objective is accurate representation of remaining uncertainty.

Vision outcome protected: allowing newly discovered uncertainty to lower confidence honestly rather than forcing confidence to rise monotonically.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — allowing newly discovered uncertainty to lower confidence honestly rather than forcing confidence to rise monotonically.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: allowing newly discovered uncertainty to lower confidence honestly rather than forcing confidence to rise monotonically.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that allowing newly discovered uncertainty to lower confidence honestly rather than forcing confidence to rise monotonically fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

The Job Shape Is Dynamic

The selected service does not determine the complete intake by itself. As the client answers questions, the system may learn that the actual job is simpler, more complex, or materially different from the initial baseline. The required questions therefore follow the evolving shape of the actual job, not merely the name of the service that started the flow.

The Seesaw

Cᵢ high → service identified
↓
Cₓ low  → material uncertainty remains (acquire evidence)
↓
evidence arrives → job shape / U(t) / Cₓ recomputed
↓
Cₓ reaches the active policy threshold → the permitted pricing precision is unlocked
Scope-confidence gap = max(0, τₓ − Cₓ). This is a diagnostic measure of remaining evidence needed to reach the active policy threshold. It is not customer friction and must not be presented as a unit of customer effort. Customer effort is evaluated comparatively when choosing among evidence paths. Intent confidence remains a separate routing/safety gate.

Scope Confidence

Conceptually:

Cₓ = Σ retired uncertainty weights / Σ relevant uncertainty weights

The denominator is dynamic. It changes when the actual job shape changes the set of relevant uncertainty classes. The numerator changes only when evidence actually retires a relevant class. The platform measures progress toward understanding the actual job, not progress toward completing a fixed questionnaire.

R-CONF-ONEFORMULARuleOne Formula, Everywhere

In plain English: All gateways return the same confidence result for equivalent BookingContext and evidence state; a gateway-specific duplicate formula is absent.

Vision outcome protected: All gateways return the same confidence result for equivalent BookingContext and evidence state; a gateway-specific duplicate formula is absent.

Accomplishment: All gateways return the same confidence result for equivalent BookingContext and evidence state; a gateway-specific duplicate formula is absent.

Serves:P-CONF-NOQUESTIONMATH · P-CONF-UNCERTAINTY

R-CONF-DECLAREDRuleA Confidence Gate Requires Authored Uncertainty

In plain English: every confidence-gated service has a non-empty, valid uncertainty profile and explicit policy; every referenced class, question, qualifying option, and retirement path resolves; non-retiring fallback options do not earn retirement; the compiler rejects.

Vision outcome protected: Confidence-based pricing is permitted only when the relevant uncertainty and its retirement path are explicitly defined.

A service that uses a confidence gate must explicitly declare its applicable uncertainty classes and confidence policy. Uncertainty-class definitions belong to the entity; the service declares the subset relevant to its work, any service-specific weights or relevance, and the evidence/questions that can retire each class. A service may not obtain confidence from a missing profile, an empty default, a question count, or a nominal confidence gain.

The schema and compiler must reject a service whose declared exact/range/diagnostic policy cannot be reached from its declared uncertainty classes and retirement paths. Confidence thresholds use the same normalized 0–1 scale as Cₓ. A deliberate diagnostic-only service may use an explicit policy for that route; it may not masquerade as a fully scoped exact quote.

Accomplishment: every confidence-gated service has a non-empty, valid uncertainty profile and explicit policy; every referenced class, question, qualifying option, and retirement path resolves; non-retiring fallback options do not earn retirement; the compiler rejects unreachable thresholds and missing declarations; and removing a required declaration causes validation to fail rather than returning 100% confidence.

There is one canonical confidence implementation, not one formula per gateway. The orchestrator's confidence function consumes the canonical evidence state. Entry paths may contribute different facts and therefore produce different inputs, but they do not get separate confidence arithmetic. Any path-specific confidence calculation is a second implementation and a defect.

The same accounting model applies regardless of whether evidence came from direct catalog selection, navigation, free text, a guided builder, chips, or structured intake. A direct catalog selection may make intent confidence high because the customer selected the named service; it does not erase scope uncertainty. Conversely, a free-text or group-scoped path may build intent and scope progressively. The path changes the evidence supplied to the canonical model, not the model itself.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Confidence-based pricing is permitted only when the relevant uncertainty and its retirement path are explicitly defined.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: confidence-based pricing is permitted only when the relevant uncertainty and its retirement path are explicitly defined.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that confidence-based pricing is permitted only when the relevant uncertainty and its retirement path are explicitly defined fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

The Thresholds

Confidence thresholds are policy inputs used by the resolution engine; they determine when a particular pricing path has enough scope to proceed at the level of precision it promises. Their exact values, tier boundaries, and tuning belong in the owning specification and decision record, not in this Charter.

How τₓ is set today: τₓ is not derived from a universal scalar complexity score. The active threshold is an explicit policy input associated with the applicable resolution/pricing tier in the SSOT and owning specification. The multi-dimensional complexity model describes the job shape that informs that policy; it does not currently collapse every job into one universal scalar. If the project adopts a different mapping, that mapping is an explicit specification decision and this charter must be updated with it.

P-CONF-PRECISIONPrincipleConfidence gates precision, not conversion. A confidence gate may prevent an exact quote from being presented prematurely. It must not be used as a generic excuse to stop the customer from reaching a priced outcome.

Vision outcome protected: gating quote precision without turning uncertainty into a generic conversion blocker or dead end.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — gating quote precision without turning uncertainty into a generic conversion blocker or dead end.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that gating quote precision without turning uncertainty into a generic conversion blocker or dead end fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

Pricing Precision

Confidence gates precision. Confidence does not gate conversion.

Different pricing outputs require different levels of uncertainty retirement:

  • Exact Quote
  • Bounded Range
  • Conditional Quote
  • Diagnostic Path

The system's responsibility is selecting the most precise outcome justified by available evidence. The customer always receives a priced path forward.

P-CONF-SAFETYPrincipleThe Safety Net

Vision outcome protected: refusing to treat missing evidence as certainty and by preserving a useful, honest next step when a precise quote is not yet justified.

Intent confidence alone is not sufficient to auto-select a specific named service. The orchestrator needs sufficient deterministic evidence to commit to that scope. When it does not have that evidence, it should stay at the appropriate dynamic, category, or diagnostic level rather than overstate certainty.

Free text may contribute evidence, but no single weak signal should be allowed to masquerade as complete scope. The exact evidence rules and confidence coefficients belong to the resolution specification.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — refusing to treat missing evidence as certainty and by preserving a useful, honest next step when a precise quote is not yet justified.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: refusing to treat missing evidence as certainty and by preserving a useful, honest next step when a precise quote is not yet justified.

UI Paths Map Perfectly

Confidence values use the canonical internal 0–1 scale. Percentages may be shown in customer-facing UI only as display conversions.
PathCᵢCₓUI behaviour
