Principle 6 — Confidence and complexity are dynamic. Confidence changes as evidence accumulates or conflicts; complexity changes as the actual job shape is revealed. Therefore intake, routing, and pricing cannot be determined solely from the initial service label.
Part V
Friction & Uncertainty Retirement
Friction exists to retire uncertainty. How much certainty is worth how much client effort.

Friction & Uncertainty Retirement

The confidence model answers how much relevant uncertainty has been retired. The economic model answers which available evidence path retires that uncertainty at an acceptable customer effort. These are related but distinct questions. The Charter deliberately does not collapse them into one scalar.

Evidence Acquisition Must Justify Customer Effort

Friction is not caused by questions. Friction is caused by customer effort. Questions are one form of customer effort. Photographs are another. File uploads are another. Measurements are another.

The platform therefore evaluates friction by asking:

How much uncertainty does this action retire?

not

How much information does this action collect?

Information is not the objective. Retiring meaningful uncertainty is.

The Friction-Efficiency Principle

The optimal interaction is not the one that collects the most information. The optimal interaction retires the most meaningful uncertainty with the least customer effort. The platform therefore prefers high-density evidence whenever business rules permit it.

R-FRICTION-RETIRERuleEvidence acquisition must be justified by uncertainty retirement and customer effort. An evidence request is justified only when it can retire or resolve material uncertainty, or enable a branch that eventually changes the resolved outcome. When two supported evidence methods retire the same required uncertainty without materially reducing the resulting resolution quality, the system must prefer the method requiring less customer effort. The Charter does not invent a universal effort unit merely to make this comparison look numeric.

Vision outcome protected: Every presented evidence request names a material uncertainty or decision it can change; requests that cannot change scope, price, time, routing, preparation, or safety are removed or justified by a recorded material purpose.

Accomplishment: Every presented evidence request names a material uncertainty or decision it can change; requests that cannot change scope, price, time, routing, preparation, or safety are removed or justified by a recorded material purpose.

Historical evidence: Battle 11 — A correct conclusion can be quietly invalidated by later work

In plain English: Every presented evidence request names a material uncertainty or decision it can change; requests that cannot change scope, price, time, routing, preparation, or safety are removed or justified by a recorded material purpose.

The specific commitment at risk is: every presented evidence request names a material uncertainty or decision it can change; requests that cannot change scope, price, time, routing, preparation, or safety are removed or justified by a recorded material purpose.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

P-FRICTION-NOTBLINDPrincipleDo not minimize friction blindly. Minimize unnecessary friction while protecting the business from uncertainty that materially changes price, labor, schedule, or outcome. The right amount of friction is the least client effort that retires the risks that actually matter.

Vision outcome protected: minimizing unnecessary customer effort without ignoring uncertainty that materially changes price, safety, labor, or preparation.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that minimizing unnecessary customer effort without ignoring uncertainty that materially changes price, safety, labor, or preparation fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

High-Density Evidence

Certain forms of evidence may retire multiple uncertainty classes simultaneously. Examples:

  • Photographs
  • Videos
  • Manufacturer labels
  • Model numbers
  • Existing installation photos

A photograph may retire more uncertainty than multiple text questions. When supported by service rules, the platform should prefer high-density evidence over equivalent low-density questionnaires.

The Economic Balance

The platform does not seek:

Minimum Friction

Nor does it seek:

Maximum Confidence

It seeks:

Minimum Friction necessary to achieve the confidence required for the intended pricing precision.

Anything beyond that is unnecessary friction. Anything below that risks invented precision.

P-FRICTION-SKIPPEDPrincipleDurable Rules

Vision outcome protected: requiring every customer effort to earn its place through meaningful uncertainty reduction or necessary preparation.

  • An intake action is justified only when its evidence can retire or materially resolve an uncertainty that affects price, labor/time, routing, scheduling, preparation, safety, or a scope-changing branch; the owning resolution specification defines the exact downstream fields.
  • A skipped answer must remain unknown; the system must never silently invent certainty.
  • If uncertainty remains material, the system may move to a range, conditional quote, or priced diagnostic rather than blocking the client.
  • High-density evidence such as a photograph may replace several lower-value questions when the service's rules support that inference.
  • Any risk markup, scheduling buffer, confidence coefficient, or numeric threshold is an SSOT coefficient or an explicit business decision, not a charter constant.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — requiring every customer effort to earn its place through meaningful uncertainty reduction or necessary preparation.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: requiring every customer effort to earn its place through meaningful uncertainty reduction or necessary preparation.

Part VI
Intake
What we ask. What we refuse to ask. How the chain evolves as answers arrive.

Intake Question Design Discipline

Illustrative values. Illustrative examples only — the fee and minute values demonstrate that evidence requests can carry real effort or cost; they are not current policy thresholds.

The following rules govern any authored intake chain. They are the canonical statement — a future conversation that conflicts with them should be checked against them, not assumed to supersede them.

P-INTAKE-QTESTPrincipleThe Question Test

Vision outcome protected: requiring each question to justify its friction with a necessary, targeted contribution to understanding or preparing the work.

Vision link: It protects the Vision by requiring each question to justify its friction with a necessary, targeted contribution to understanding or preparing the work.

Before asking any intake question:

Is this absolutely necessary, highly targeted, and not already answerable from information the system already has? Is the added friction worth the uncertainty it retires?

A question must not be asked if:

  1. the client already answered it in free text;
  2. the navigation path already supplied the fact;
  3. another answer already makes it redundant;
  4. the service itself makes the answer effectively obvious.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — requiring each question to justify its friction with a necessary, targeted contribution to understanding or preparing the work.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: requiring each question to justify its friction with a necessary, targeted contribution to understanding or preparing the work.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that requiring each question to justify its friction with a necessary, targeted contribution to understanding or preparing the work fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

R-INTAKE-WIREREMOVEThe Wire-or-Remove Test

In plain English: Every question must change price, time, a meaningful next branch, or necessary preparation; otherwise remove it.

Vision outcome protected: Every question must change price, time, a meaningful next branch, or necessary preparation; otherwise remove it.

Every question in any intake chain must pass at least one of the following. A question that passes none must be removed or made display-only.

  1. Price — its answer changes the final price through a genuinely-consulted pricing mechanism.
  2. Time — its answer changes the estimated labor time.
  3. Branch — its answer determines which subsequent question(s) appear, and at least one of those passes this same test.
  4. Prep — its answer captures information a technician genuinely needs before arriving, not already inferable from navigation context.

Specific violation classes:

  • Location class. Asking location on services where the location is structurally constant (a dishwasher is always in a kitchen) or already implied by navigation path. Pure friction.
  • Setup-module class. A computer/TV setup question must not appear in the intake chain of any service outside its own category.
  • Force-injection class. A module force-injected into every service in a tier, regardless of relevance to the specific service, is almost always a violation. The bar for a universal question is much higher than the bar for a scoped one.

Wire-or-Remove scope: a question is wired only when its answer cannot already be inferred from navigation, free text, or structural knowledge and the answer can change the resolved outcome.

Accomplishment: Every published intake question demonstrably changes price, labor time, a meaningful downstream branch, or technician preparation that cannot already be inferred; a deliberately planted redundant or outcome-neutral question is detected and fails verification.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Every question must change price, time, a meaningful next branch, or necessary preparation; otherwise remove it.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: every question must change price, time, a meaningful next branch, or necessary preparation; otherwise remove it.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every question must change price, time, a meaningful next branch, or necessary preparation; otherwise remove it fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

R-INTAKE-NONRETIRINGThe Non-Retiring Answer — A Fallback That Resolves to Nothing

In plain English: A fallback answer must change the resolved path or offer an honest next step; it cannot pretend uncertainty was resolved.

Vision outcome protected: A fallback answer must change the resolved path or offer an honest next step; it cannot pretend uncertainty was resolved.

Wire-or-Remove says a question must change price, time, branch, or prep. That rule was always correct. What this section adds is the specific failure mode its four-word form did not name: a question whose confident answers change one of those things, and whose fallback answer — "not sure", "the tech will measure on site", "I'll describe it in the notes" — changes nothing at all.

The fallback is not an answer. It is the customer's honest admission that the question asked them to know something they do not know. That admission is real, and it is worth recording. What is not real, and not worth trading confidence for, is treating the admission as if it retired an uncertainty. It did not. It stated that the uncertainty will be resolved on-site by a tape measure, a diagnostic, or a technician's eye — and the on-site visit is a mechanism that runs regardless of what the customer answered here.

Accomplishment: each fallback is either wired to a real downstream consequence or explicitly routes to a conditional/diagnostic path; a test using the fallback fails if it retires no uncertainty while claiming confidence; and the customer is not left with an unpriced dead end.

The two honest forms

An intake question with a fallback earns its place in exactly one of two ways.

Form (a) — the fallback is wired. The "not sure" option carries a real modifier_ref reflecting the honest cost of the technician's on-site determination. The customer's uncertainty genuinely costs the business time, and the fee reflects it. Three existing modules are correct in this form:

Illustrative values only. The fee and minute amounts in this example explain how an answer can change real effort or cost; they are not current prices, universal defaults, or policy constants. Current values belong in the authoritative SSOT/policy record.

ModuleFallback answerModifierWhy it is honest
existing_type"Not sure – the tech can check (may add time)"existing_type_not_sure_the_tech_can_check_ma — $10 / 15 minThe technician genuinely spends longer identifying the connection type before starting work.
lath_check"Not sure"lath_check_not_sure — $15 / 15 minVerifying whether solid backing exists takes real probing, and the answer determines which anchors are used.
faucet_part_available"Not sure what part I need"faucet_part_available_no — $20 / 20 minThe job becomes a two-trip job — diagnosis now, part ordered, return visit — and the fee reflects the second trip.

Each of these fallbacks resolves to a different downstream outcome than the confident answers. The customer who does not know pays for the technician's time to find out. That is honest.

Form (b) — the question is removed. The fallback resolves to the same (price, time, branch, prep) as the confident answers. The question does not retire uncertainty, and no amount of wiring can make it, because the information it asks for is either already knowable from navigation or free text, or is going to be measured on-site regardless.

door_size is the canonical case. In prehung_interior_door_install, all three options — 30″ x 80″, 32″ x 80″, and I don't know – the tech can measure on site — carry complexity_override: "routine", carry no modifier_ref, change no branch, and change no prep. The question fires only in the client_supplying_door → "No, I need you to procure it" branch — the branch in which the technician is already going to measure the opening before ordering anything. The divergent case (a non-standard-height prehung interior door) exists, but the branch that fires the question is precisely the branch in which the technician's tape measure supersedes the customer's guess. The question adds friction in every case to serve the one case the technician handles anyway.

space_ready is another. All three options — Yes, everything is ready, No, there's some clutter or furniture, Not sure – I'll describe in notes — carry complexity_override: "routine", carry no modifier, change no branch, change no prep. The answer doesn't change anything. The module is dead.

There is no third form.

Interaction with R-INTAKE-ESCAPE. A constrained question — a list of brands, models, or symptoms — that has already earned its place under this rule MUST offer an escape under R-INTAKE-ESCAPE. That escape is not an exception to this rule: it must route to a different downstream state than the confident answers, or the question is removed. An escape that resolves to the same (price, time, branch, prep, tier, risk classification, batch-risk flag) tuple as a confident answer, without carrying a modifier, is a fallback this rule prohibits.

The rule

An intake module's fallback option — "not sure", "tech measures on site", "describe in notes", or any equivalent — must either carry a real modifier_ref reflecting the honest cost of on-site determination, or the module must be removed.

The mechanical test enumerates every module in intake_modules, resolves each option's downstream effect against the (price, time, branch, prep, tier, risk classification, batch-risk flag) tuple the option produces, and fails when a fallback option produces the same tuple as a confident option and carries no modifier. The test does not care which option is the fallback; it cares that the outcomes differ. A fallback that resolves to the same tuple as any other option, without a modifier, does not earn its place.

The axes are the test

An answer moves the job when it changes at least one of the following. If it changes none, the question does not earn its place.

AxisWhat it means for the answer to move it
Skill tierThe answer reveals a materially different sub-job shape — a commercial fixture rather than residential, a non-standard size, a hidden material, a scope-changing branch reaching a higher-baseline sub-job.
Risk tierThe answer reveals a hazard, a regulated condition, an active water/electrical/gas situation, or a permit requirement.
Batch riskThe answer reveals heterogeneity inside a batch — one piece needs a real repair rather than a swap; the batch is not uniform.
DurationThe answer adds minutes — a slower drain, a larger area, a second coat, a two-person lift.
BranchThe answer opens a sub-job with its own intake requirements — the scope-changing-branch case.
PrepThe answer tells the technician something they cannot infer from navigation, free text, or the service itself — a specific part on hand, a specific clearance, a specific existing condition.
PriceThe answer fires a modifier_ref that reaches the price through a genuinely-consulted mechanism.

If the answer moves none of these, it does not earn its place — for any option, including the fallback. The axis test and the Wire-or-Remove test are the same test stated at two levels of precision. Wire-or-Remove's four words — price, time, branch, prep — are the coarse form; the axes are the precise form. An answer that moves an axis passes Wire-or-Remove. An answer that moves none fails both.

The frequency clause

Rarity may be relevant to whether a question is worth its customer effort, but the Charter does not establish a universal numerical frequency threshold. A question belongs only when its answer can materially change the resolved path and the customer-facing evidence cost is justified by the value of obtaining that information.

  • Rare divergence handled correctly elsewhere → do not impose the question on the common case solely to protect the rare case.
  • Material divergence is common but expressed in technical units → translate the question into customer-observable terms.
  • Material divergence is common and customer-answerable → retain the question when its answer changes the resolved outcome.

If a service specification needs a numeric frequency threshold, that threshold belongs in the owning specification, where it can be changed without redefining the Charter's general law.

The confidence side-effect

An answer contributes scope-confidence weight only when it retires an uncertainty class that affects a downstream outcome. An answer that resolves to the same downstream state as another answer retires nothing, and contributes nothing.

door_size currently carries legacy confidence-gain metadata: 20 — a nominal twenty-point gain toward clearing prehung_interior_door_install's threshold, earned by an answer that changed no price, no time, no branch, and no prep. That is the P-CONF-NOQUESTIONMATH failure in miniature. The rule already prohibits deriving confidence from question counts; what this section adds is the mechanical consequence: if an answer retires nothing, it grants nothing, and a module whose fallback retires nothing is a defect.

Removing the module is the fix. If the service then cannot reach its threshold, that is honest signal that the threshold or the surrounding modules need attention — not a reason to reinstate a dummy question. A threshold that cannot be cleared by the real questions the service asks is a threshold that is wrong. Adding a question that manufactures the appearance of retirement hides the misalignment without fixing it.

Accomplishment: Each fallback either carries a real modifier that changes the resolved downstream outcome or is removed; a counterfactual check fails for a fallback that contributes confidence while leaving price, time, branch, preparation, tier, risk, and batch-risk state unchanged.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — A fallback answer must change the resolved path or offer an honest next step; it cannot pretend uncertainty was resolved.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: a fallback answer must change the resolved path or offer an honest next step; it cannot pretend uncertainty was resolved.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a fallback answer must change the resolved path or offer an honest next step; it cannot pretend uncertainty was resolved fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

R-INTAKE-ESCAPERuleEvery Constrained Question Has an Escape, and Every Question Declares Its Purpose

In plain English: Every constrained question needs a usable escape route and a declared purpose.

Vision outcome protected: Every constrained question needs a usable escape route and a declared purpose.

Accomplishment: Every question that constrains an answer (a list of brands, a list of models, a list of symptoms) offers an explicit escape answer† — “Other”, “Don’t know”, or “None of these” — that routes to a genuinely different downstream state: a conditional quote, a diagnostic, or a route_to pointing at a named service. It must never be a dead end or merely repeat the confident-answer path. Every question declares its purpose (pricing, diagnostic, or routing; the vocabulary follows R-INTAKE-WIREREMOVE’s categories) so the UI can present it honestly; a question without a purpose fails validation. informational is not a purpose: a question whose answer changes nothing is prohibited by P-INTAKE-QTEST.

Serves:P-WHITEFLAG-PRICED · P-INTAKE-QTEST · P-INVARIANT-CHIPSNOTFEES

Historical evidence: Battle 19 — No way out

A question with no escape is a dead end; a question with no purpose is opaque. The brand question offered three brands and no “Other / don’t know”; a customer with a brand not listed had no answer. Eight modules had no purpose field, so the UI could not decide whether to show an “affects price” badge. Both are the same failure: a question that does not tell the customer what it is for and does not let them say “I don’t know” is a question that does not belong. The escape answer is the mini-form of the priced diagnostic path; the purpose field is the mini-form of the transparency principle.
An escape is not a get-out-of-jail card. The escape exists for questions that have already earned their place under R-INTAKE-NONRETIRING. The escape is compliant only when it routes to a different downstream state than the confident answers — a conditional quote, a diagnostic, or a route_to pointing at a named service. A neutral “Other / don’t know” option on a question whose answers do not differ is not a compliant escape; it is a fallback this rule’s companion prohibits, and R-INTAKE-ESCAPE’s “every constrained question has an escape” does not save it. The question is removed, not given a decorative option.

R-INTAKE-RECOVERYRuleRecovery Composes from Current Open Uncertainty

In plain English: a recovery request is evaluated against current open uncertainty; every offered question can retire at least one currently open class or reveal a justified dependent class; already-retired questions are not repeated without a newly opened dependency; and no.

Vision outcome protected: Recovery asks only about uncertainty that remains open and can change the resolved job or next step.

When the flow offers a recovery affordance such as “Answer More Questions,” the offered path must be composed from the current resolved BookingContext, the currently open uncertainty classes, the questions and evidence that can retire them, and their declared dependencies. It must not simply replay an entity's default question list or reopen questions whose relevant uncertainty is already retired.

The recovery decision is a distinct Logic responsibility. The confidence engine measures what remains open; the composer composes valid question chains; the resolver determines the resulting state; the recovery responsibility selects the useful next evidence path; and the Glue coordinates these calls. Recovery is not implemented by hiding this decision inside the composer, confidence engine, UI handler, or a static service list.

If a useful remote question or evidence path exists, recovery must offer it. If no available remote path can retire the remaining material uncertainty, the system must say so and route to the appropriate priced diagnostic rather than presenting a decorative or repetitive second door.

Accomplishment: a recovery request is evaluated against current open uncertainty; every offered question can retire at least one currently open class or reveal a justified dependent class; already-retired questions are not repeated without a newly opened dependency; and no remaining remote path yields an explicit diagnostic/conditional route rather than a dead end.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Recovery asks only about uncertainty that remains open and can change the resolved job or next step.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: recovery asks only about uncertainty that remains open and can change the resolved job or next step.

P-INTAKE-INFERPrincipleThe Inference Rules

Vision outcome protected: using facts already supplied by the customer or navigation context, avoiding redundant questions and inconsistent interpretations.

  1. Free-text inferability. If NLP-detected tags or parsed entities already carry the answer (e.g. the client typed "brick wall"), do not re-ask it as a structured question.
  2. Navigation-path inferability. If reaching a service required tapping through a sub-group that already declares the answer (e.g. a "Brick" sub-group before a repair service), the system already knows that fact structurally.
  3. Redundancy. If another question in the same chain already resolves it, do not ask it again, even if worded differently.
  4. Structural availability. If the fact is a structural property of the job — parking access, urgency for an emergency service, whether a dishwasher is in a kitchen — it must not be asked. It is captured from the service itself, the navigation path, or free text, and applies as a modifier when known. The question does not exist.
The specific, most-frustrating failure mode these rules exist to prevent: when the client has already told us the answer through their navigation, and the intake asks them anyway.

A client taps through a specific path — brick wall, hourly-billed, frames. The system now knows, with certainty, four facts about the job. Then the intake asks "what wall are we drilling into?" (brick — already told), "what are you hanging?" (a frame — already told), "do you need a 50-ft ladder?" (this is a picture frame at eye level — obviously not).

This is the intake chain being asinine. The narrow, correct behavior is: the system asks only what it cannot infer.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — using facts already supplied by the customer or navigation context, avoiding redundant questions and inconsistent interpretations.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: using facts already supplied by the customer or navigation context, avoiding redundant questions and inconsistent interpretations.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that using facts already supplied by the customer or navigation context, avoiding redundant questions and inconsistent interpretations fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Question-Count Ranges

Every service should have an authored minimum and an allowed range rather than a single fixed intake length. The system chooses where the actual request falls within that range from the information already known and the way the job evolves.

  • Base scope: the minimum information verified as sufficient for a standard request.
  • Escalation: additional questions become available only when the client's facts diverge from the expected job shape or expose a material pricing, labor, schedule, or preparation risk.
  • Authoring obligation: the authored chain must be capable of reaching the required resolution state for the jobs it claims to cover.
  • Scope-changing branch: when an answer reveals a genuinely different job shape, a new branch may have its own intake requirements; those questions are not simply "extra" questions on the original job.

The Charter does not prescribe a universal numeric question ceiling. Friction protection is a design invariant; the exact ranges and limits belong in the owning service/routing specification.

The scope-changing-branch exception

One class of question sits outside the tier cap. A question whose answer opens an entirely new sub-scope of work is not a longer version of the same job — it is the questions of a different job the client's own answer has revealed. The canonical case: a prehung interior door install normally asks two questions. If the client answers that they need a custom door size — a size no prehung unit is manufactured in — the branch that opens is a second job's worth of scoping. Its questions render even though the base tier cap is two.

The distinction is strict. A question is a scope-changing branch if and only if its answer changes the shape of the work — a different material, a different action, a different entity. A question that merely clarifies a parameter of the same job is subject to the tier cap normally.

The Confidence Baseline

Every service may have a nominal baseline describing a common starting shape. The baseline is a starting hypothesis, not a confidence calculation. Evidence can confirm the baseline, refine it, contradict it, or reveal facts the baseline did not represent.

Confidence changes according to uncertainty retired or introduced, not according to agreement with a baseline. Evidence that diverges from the baseline may increase confidence if it retires material uncertainty. Evidence that confirms the baseline may add little or no confidence if the relevant uncertainty was already retired.

Two clients requesting the nominally "same" service can receive different, correct question sets — one shorter, one longer — both reaching 100% confidence for their respective actual jobs.

The canonical example — a prehung interior door install:

If the client says "upstairs bathroom" — location context infers solid-core construction. Standard job. Confidence rises. We skip the door-type question entirely.

If the client says "bedroom closet, 14 inches, white carpet" — the baseline has changed. Confidence drops. We now need more questions.

Same nominal service. Same nominal tier. Radically different actual jobs.

Questions Should Be Customer-Answerable

A technically useful question is not automatically a good customer question. Prefer decisions the client can reasonably make over internal measurements or terminology they should not have to calculate. When the system needs a technical distinction, translate it into a familiar choice or use another available source of evidence when the business rules support doing so.

Quantity Questions

Routine services: the only question asked should be total quantity — plus, where a real, named exception applies, one additional question that changes the cost of the work itself.

Skilled / Specialized services: quantity should generally not be asked at all. Each unit of a genuinely complex job carries too much of its own nuance to be meaningfully batched by a count.

The "no, but not never" rule. Quantity may legitimately be asked of a complex job only when the client themselves explicitly states it:

  • Never in a catalog grid tap. Tapping a tile is the client saying "this one"; there is no quantity signal.
  • Possibly if the client explicitly selects a quantity in the Guided Builder.
  • Possibly if the client writes a quantity in the free-text input and the parser extracts it.
R-INTAKE-QTYFIELD Rule Quantity Is One Authoritative Fact, Presented Once

In plain English: Quantity has one authoritative value across entry, display, pricing, and booking; every path reads and edits that same value.

Vision outcome protected: The customer sees one clear quantity value that remains consistent across the request, quote, and booking views.

Regardless of which entry path supplies the quantity, it belongs to the canonical resolved-request contract, is written through one authoritative path for that entry, and is presented in one unmistakable, editable place. It must never appear twice or contradict itself across views. The field name, storage shape, and UI control may evolve; the one-value contract may not.

Accomplishment: Every entry path supplies one canonical quantity fact; every quantity control reads and edits it; and the displayed, priced, and booked quantities remain consistent.

All entry paths write quantity to the same canonical quantity fact in the resolved-request contract, and every quantity control reads and edits that fact. The field name and storage shape belong to the owning schema; the invariant is one authoritative quantity value across entry, display, pricing, and booking.

Rule health & evolvability

The field name and storage shape belong to the owning schema; the invariant is one authoritative quantity value across entry, display, pricing, and booking.

Harm prevented — Duplicate or independently edited quantity values can make the preview, quote, and booking disagree, causing underquotes or incorrect work scope.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

R-INTAKE-QTYONCEQuantity must not double-count. A service that already carries its own, dedicated quantity question captures the count through that question. The outer quantity multiplier must not then apply on top of it. When a service's chain genuinely does not address quantity, the outer multiplier is the correct mechanism — and only then. The decision is made once, at the resolution boundary, and applied consistently by every caller.

Vision outcome protected: ensuring quantity changes the price exactly once, preventing duplicate multiplication and misleading totals.

Accomplishment: Quantity is applied exactly once for each request across the supported quantity range; tests include quantity greater than one and fail on double multiplication.

In plain English: Quantity is applied exactly once for each request across the supported quantity range; tests include quantity greater than one and fail on double multiplication.

Part VII
No White Flag
Never a dead end. Every uncertainty resolves to a priced path forward.

P-WHITEFLAG-PRICEDPrincipleNo White Flag — Priced Paths Forward

Vision outcome protected: ensuring uncertainty never becomes a dead end: the customer receives a defensible price or a useful, honestly priced next step.

Vision link: It protects the Vision by ensuring uncertainty never becomes a dead end: the customer receives a defensible price or a useful, honestly priced next step.

There is always a priced path within the supported precision methods. If a registered diagnostic path is unavailable, the service must resolve to an honest range or conditional price; otherwise it is not publication-ready.

Precision levels. For this Charter, precision means the specificity of the priced outcome presented to the client: exact quote → bounded range → conditional quote → registered diagnostic path. Confidence determines which level is justified; it does not determine whether the client gets a priced next step.

The important distinction is that confidence gates the level of precision, not the existence of a price. The orchestrator does not need to force low-confidence work through the exact-quote formula. It resolves the appropriate pricing path: exact quote, bounded/conditional quote, or diagnostic pricing. Each path is reproducible and bookable on its own terms.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — ensuring uncertainty never becomes a dead end: the customer receives a defensible price or a useful, honestly priced next step.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring uncertainty never becomes a dead end: the customer receives a defensible price or a useful, honestly priced next step.

P-WHITEFLAG-PRECISEPrincipleNo Invented Precision

Vision outcome protected: ensuring the displayed price never claims greater certainty than the resolved scope and evidence can support.

Uncertainty does not authorize invented precision. If a range is shown, its bounds must come from explicit, reproducible scenarios or modifiers supported by the pricing model. The Charter does not require a probability distribution or a weighted expected value.

When the remaining uncertainty is too material for an exact quote, the system may instead resolve to a registered diagnostic service with its own explicit price. That price is not a fallback number manufactured by the confidence engine; it is the price of a real, bookable diagnostic path.

The Fork — Two Paths to Conversion

Path A — Remote Guided Intake (Deep Dive)

Trigger: use this path when the system has identified the work sufficiently to know which additional remote evidence can materially reduce remaining scope uncertainty.

Expose deeper, more technical questions. Value prop: "Save money by providing more detail." Outcome: If successful, the seesaw balances; exact price appears.

Path B — On-Site Pro (Diagnostic Escape Hatch)

Trigger: use this path when remaining uncertainty is material enough that further remote intake is not justified or cannot establish the confidence required by the applicable exact-quote path, and a registered diagnostic service is available.

When remote scope cannot be established to the level required for an exact quote, the system may offer a priced diagnostic visit as the next legitimate service. The customer receives a concrete, bookable outcome rather than an unpriced handoff. If the business offers a diagnostic-fee credit toward subsequent work, that credit is an explicit pricing/business rule, not an implicit consequence of low confidence.

R-WHITEFLAG-FORKBRuleThe Diagnostic Path Preserves the Customer’s Price

In plain English: an end-to-end test selects the diagnostic path and asserts agreement among displayed fee, BookingContext fee, checkout fee, and booking payload; a deliberately mismatched fee fails the test.

Vision outcome protected: The diagnostic fee remains consistent from the customer-facing offer through checkout and the booking record.

When a customer chooses a priced diagnostic/on-site path, the fee displayed at the decision point must be the fee carried into the booking, subject only to an explicitly disclosed and accepted change before commitment. A diagnostic path is not a valid conversion if the price that earned consent is silently lost or replaced.

Accomplishment: an end-to-end test selects the diagnostic path and asserts agreement among displayed fee, BookingContext fee, checkout fee, and booking payload; a deliberately mismatched fee fails the test.
