Part VIII Runtime & Architecture
The authoritative data, entry classifiers, shared runtime, and layer boundaries.
The pipeline that runs. How the catalog descends. How text becomes structure. Where the truth lives. The dimensions and layers the code composes.

The Single Source of Truth

The SSOT file is the single source of business knowledge. Authoritative business knowledge lives there. Executable behavior belongs in code; durable project governance belongs in this Charter; owning field-level contracts belong in their specifications.

R-SYSTEM-NODATARuleThe Core Rule

In plain English: A change to authored service, pricing, fee, or routing knowledge can be expressed in the SSOT without adding a duplicate business rule in application code.

Vision outcome protected: A change to authored service, pricing, fee, or routing knowledge can be expressed in the SSOT without adding a duplicate business rule in application code.

Accomplishment: A change to authored service, pricing, fee, or routing knowledge can be expressed in the SSOT without adding a duplicate business rule in application code.

Serves:P-SYSTEM-CLASSIFY-ONE

No business rule in code that should be in data. If a change to pricing, services, or fees requires touching application code, that is a signal the SSOT is incomplete — not that the code needs the change.
Data Authority. Every real business number lives in the SSOT. When a new number is needed — a fee, rate, threshold, buffer, modifier value, credit policy, or any other coefficient — the agent decides it using the hierarchy in Decision Authority and Agent Autonomy, anchors it to existing catalog data or NYC market rate where possible, and documents the reasoning in the field's own _note. Flagging for operator review is the last resort in that hierarchy, not the first response. The original intent of this rule — that ungrounded numbers must not silently enter the catalog — remains fully in force; the change is that "ungrounded" is defined by the hierarchy, not by the absence of operator sign-off.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — A change to authored service, pricing, fee, or routing knowledge can be expressed in the SSOT without adding a duplicate business rule in application code.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: a change to authored service, pricing, fee, or routing knowledge can be expressed in the ssot without adding a duplicate business rule in application code.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a change to authored service, pricing, fee, or routing knowledge can be expressed in the ssot without adding a duplicate business rule in application code fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-DECLARATIVERuleDeclarative Only

In plain English: The SSOT contains no executable JavaScript or hidden application runtime; schema validation rejects executable content where declarative data is required.

Vision outcome protected: The SSOT contains no executable JavaScript or hidden application runtime; schema validation rejects executable content where declarative data is required.

Accomplishment: The SSOT contains no executable JavaScript or hidden application runtime; schema validation rejects executable content where declarative data is required.

Serves:P-SYSTEM-NOEDIT · P-SYSTEM-CLASSIFY-ONE

The SSOT declares knowledge. It does not contain JavaScript and it does not become a hidden application runtime.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — The SSOT contains no executable JavaScript or hidden application runtime; schema validation rejects executable content where declarative data is required.

Harm prevented — A component can take responsibility it does not own, duplicate another component’s decision, or couple an unrelated concern to a detail that later changes. The specific commitment at risk is: the ssot contains no executable javascript or hidden application runtime; schema validation rejects executable content where declarative data is required.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that the ssot contains no executable javascript or hidden application runtime; schema validation rejects executable content where declarative data is required fails under a moved responsibility, changed deployment boundary, or workaround that duplicates a concern, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-SHAPERuleContent Is Protected; Shape Is a Design Decision

In plain English: A workaround caused by source shape triggers a shape review; declarative-content requirements and single-file deployment requirements are documented as separate decisions.

Vision outcome protected: A workaround caused by source shape triggers a shape review; declarative-content requirements and single-file deployment requirements are documented as separate decisions.

Accomplishment: A workaround caused by source shape triggers a shape review; declarative-content requirements and single-file deployment requirements are documented as separate decisions.

Shape is a design decision, but a shape that forces an artifact to serve two roles is a defect. The single-file deployment constraint is handled separately by R-SYSTEM-SINGLEFILECOST; declarative content is not justified by deployment packaging.

Authored and derived content must not share an authoritative artifact. If a structure contains both curated business knowledge and generated indexes, split the roles: move every curated value into the authored source, then derive the complete machine representation. A hybrid artifact is defective even when its generated portion is reproducible, because hand-curated values hidden in the output have no authoritative source and cannot be safely regenerated.

Serves:P-SYSTEM-NOEDIT · P-INVARIANT-PROPAGATE

The SSOT has two things the Charter has previously conflated: content and shape. They are governed differently.

The SSOT's content — the services, fees, chains, rules, tags, materials, formulas — is hand-curated, authoritative, and protected by every rule in this chapter. No compiler overwrites it. No session silently edits it. It is the source of business truth.

The SSOT's shape — the namespace structure, the key layout, whether a fact lives on a service or on the group that owns it, whether derived data sits on the node it describes or in a separate compiled namespace — is a design decision. It is not content. It is not hand-curated business knowledge. It is the container the content sits in.

When the shape forces a consumer to work around it — by copying a fact into a second location, by maintaining a parallel representation, by skipping a lookup it would naturally perform, by merging at the call site what could have been merged at the source — the shape is the defect. The correct response is to change the shape, not to add the workaround. This is R-GOVERN-TRANSITION applied to the SSOT specifically.

The failure this rule exists to prevent: a project in which the SSOT's shape is treated as a fixed given, and every other part of the architecture is built around it. The architecture's quality becomes bounded by the shape's quality, and the shape is never improved because improving it looks like a violation. That is a stable, self-reinforcing failure mode, and it is what this rule names and forbids.

Architecture must evolve with the authoritative model. Regression protection must prevent recurrence of incorrect behavior, not prevent legitimate structural change. When the domain model or SSOT shape is corrected, dependent architecture, code, tests, and derived representations must be allowed — and required where applicable — to evolve with it.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — A workaround caused by source shape triggers a shape review; declarative-content requirements and single-file deployment requirements are documented as separate decisions.

Harm prevented — A component can take responsibility it does not own, duplicate another component’s decision, or couple an unrelated concern to a detail that later changes. The specific commitment at risk is: a workaround caused by source shape triggers a shape review; declarative-content requirements and single-file deployment requirements are documented as separate decisions.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a workaround caused by source shape triggers a shape review; declarative-content requirements and single-file deployment requirements are documented as separate decisions fails under a moved responsibility, changed deployment boundary, or workaround that duplicates a concern, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

Derived Data Has a Different Lifecycle

Compiler output is a derived artifact, not a second SSOT. It may provide indexes, routing structures, or other executable forms of the declarative knowledge, but it does not become an independent source of business truth. Do not hand-edit it. Before applying regenerated output, confirm that the compiler preserves all authoritative curated content it is required to preserve. A compiler failure to reproduce curated knowledge is a compiler defect, not permission to delete or replace that knowledge. If it cannot, the compiler is wrong or incomplete; the missing knowledge does not become disposable merely because it appeared in derived data.

P-SYSTEM-NOEDITPrincipleThe compiler may derive structure; it may not overwrite curated content. When existing, hand-curated content is present for a group, the compiler preserves it outright — not merge, not average, not re-derive. Fresh derivation is used only as a genuine fallback for entities that have nothing to protect. This is the rule that prevents a routine compiler re-run from silently reclassifying a group the catalog deliberately classified. Clarification (placement vs. lifecycle). The rule governs lifecycle — derived data is not hand-edited, is not a second SSOT, and is fully regenerable — not location. Derived data may live either on the node it describes (as a compiler-owned, underscore-prefixed key) or in a dedicated compiled namespace; both placements are compliant, and the choice is a design decision, not a governance one. What the rule prohibits is treating a derived artifact as authoritative — regardless of where it sits.

Vision outcome protected: preventing derivation from overwriting hand-curated business knowledge it cannot reproduce.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — preventing derivation from overwriting hand-curated business knowledge it cannot reproduce.

Harm prevented — The system can fail to deliver the specific outcome this commitment protects, and the failure may remain undetected or be patched inconsistently. The specific commitment at risk is: preventing derivation from overwriting hand-curated business knowledge it cannot reproduce.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that preventing derivation from overwriting hand-curated business knowledge it cannot reproduce fails under a concrete counterexample to the protected outcome, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

P-SYSTEM-VERSIONSPrincipleSource Schema, Derivation, and Runtime Contracts

Vision outcome protected: preserving a traceable, compatible path from authored knowledge through derivation to runtime behavior.

Keep three meanings distinct: the source-schema version governs authored source shape; the derivation version governs the generated artifact shape; and the runtime/interface version governs compatibility with consuming code. These values may differ because they answer different questions. They must not be collapsed into one counter or negotiated implicitly.

  • Content version — bumps on any meaningful content edit: new services, retagged smart tags, new modules, adjusted thresholds.
  • Schema version — bumps only when the shape of the file changes in a way that would make old code misread it: renamed keys, removed keys, new required fields, changed nesting.

An additive change — a new optional field, a new namespace — does not bump the schema version, because old code that ignores an unrecognized field still reads the file correctly. A structural change does. The two counters answer different questions, and conflating them hides the difference between "the content moved" and "the file's contract changed."

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — preserving a traceable, compatible path from authored knowledge through derivation to runtime behavior.

Harm prevented — A component can take responsibility it does not own, duplicate another component’s decision, or couple an unrelated concern to a detail that later changes. The specific commitment at risk is: preserving a traceable, compatible path from authored knowledge through derivation to runtime behavior.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that preserving a traceable, compatible path from authored knowledge through derivation to runtime behavior fails under a moved responsibility, changed deployment boundary, or workaround that duplicates a concern, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The Flat Graph and the Three-Artifact Contract

The schema direction follows from the one-purpose rule. It is a target architecture, not a claim that the migration has already happened. The authored source, generated runtime representation, and deployed runtime/code have different owners and lifecycles; their contracts must be explicit.

  1. Stable identity: every independently referenceable entity, action, modifier, question, service, tag, and option has an ID; labels are presentation.
  2. References by ID: one canonical reference grammar and target type; legacy spellings are normalized at a migration boundary, not supported as parallel runtime contracts.
  3. Authored source only: SSOT contains authored business knowledge and typed relationships, not compiled indexes or runtime projections. Its primary organizing axis is the entity; shared actions and modifiers are defined once and referenced by ID. Curated content currently living only in a derived structure must first be moved to its proper authored home; it must never be silently discarded.
  4. One authoritative schema: one versioned schema validates source shape. The source-schema version describes authored data; the derivation version describes generated output shape; runtime/interface version describes compatibility with consuming code. These versions may differ and have distinct meanings.
  5. Separate tools: the governed authoring surface authors and initiates publication; derivation generates output; validation returns a verdict. Validation is callable without running derivation, and tests never invoke the compiler merely to validate.
  6. Explicit publication: the derived artifact is generated, not hand-edited. A generated field must trace to authored source or an explicitly documented derivation rule, and every generated index must name a real consumer.
  7. Compatible deployment: code and published output ship atomically or are guarded by version checks that refuse incompatible combinations before customer work begins.
  8. One-file decision: the runtime may remain one HTML file if the deployment benefit justifies it; otherwise a build may produce that file from modular source. The project must not simultaneously treat one-file deployment as mandatory and modular test extraction as if it were native runtime architecture.

Content and structure changes are different decisions. A content-only change edits authored business knowledge and must leave code untouched unless validation reveals that the existing contract cannot represent the intended content. A structure-only change edits schemas, compiler/validator, or runtime contracts and must not silently alter curated business meaning. If a change genuinely requires both, state both intended changes and verify each independently; do not force an artificial boundary that would preserve a broken shape.

Accomplishment: a source-schema validator rejects invalid identity/references; derivation is reproducible; each output field has authored provenance; tests can validate without invoking derivation; version mismatch is refused; and no consumer reads a second authoritative source.

Entity-First Authoring: Make the Domain Explicit

Illustrative example, not a live data snapshot. Names, IDs, tags, numeric weights, and example field values below explain the authoring shape only. Current business values and policy thresholds belong in the authoritative SSOT and owning policy specification.

The target SSOT is entity-first: its primary organizing axis is the customer-named thing or component, not a workflow chain, module, or list of customer-facing services. The entity is the domain substrate. The composer, resolver, confidence engine, route builder, pricing engine, and interface consume different views of the same authored relationships.

The customer describes a noun, a verb, and conditions: fridge door + repair + seal/align; toilet seat + replace + no extra modifiers; wall-mounted object + mount + brick, oversized, and ladder conditions. Every curated service is a point in (entity, action, modifiers). A dynamic request composes from that same space. Behavioral classes remain descriptive metadata, never the primary identity axis.

The logical ownership is entity-first without duplicating shared vocabulary. The authored root organizes entities. Each entity owns its applicable questions, reusable uncertainty-class definitions, and curated service combinations. Shared actions and modifiers have one stable ID and one definition; entities and services reference them. A service carries the pricing and intake overrides that are genuinely specific to that combination. A question is authored once for its declared scope and can be reused only where that scope is valid.

The seven explicit authoring obligations

  1. Uncertainty definitions and policy. Entity-owned uncertainty classes identify material unknowns and the questions or evidence that can retire them. Each service with a confidence bar declares its applicable uncertainty classes, service-specific weights or relevance where needed, and a confidence policy with exact, range, and diagnostic thresholds. Definitions are reusable; applicability and policy are explicit per service.
  2. Stable identity. Every independently referenceable entity, action, modifier, question, service, tag, and option has a stable id. Labels are presentation. Every option carries a stable identifier; nothing is keyed by label, spelling, or punctuation.
  3. Question scope. Every question declares entity_scope (or an explicit global/context scope when it genuinely applies beyond one entity). The compiler rejects a scope that includes an unrelated entity merely to make a chain validate. For example, a general location question must not claim washer-specific scope unless the question is truly about the washer.
  4. Option effects. Every option declares its actual downstream affects set, drawn from the supported outcomes such as price, minutes, branch, preparation, checkout state, and complexity. A module cannot claim affects_price: true when the option declares no price effect. The declared effect must match the resolved behavior; it is not a free-standing promise.
  5. Dependencies. Every question declares prerequisite relationships in first-class depends_on data, including the prerequisite question and required answer where applicable. Dependency is not inferred by scraping a then map. A then map may describe consequences; it is not the canonical dependency graph.
  6. Recovery affordances. Each service with a confidence gate declares whether remote recovery is available and the policy for composing it from current open uncertainty. It must not merely replay the entity's default question list. If no applicable question or evidence path can retire a remaining class, the route must say so and select the appropriate priced diagnostic path rather than pretending recovery is possible.
  7. Component/action identity. Each curated service declares its primary entity_id and action_id, plus referenced modifiers and any explicit service-specific overrides. These are authored identity, not metadata inferred later from labels, categories, or a compound map key.

These obligations define the authoring surface: one uncertainty-class shape with explicit retirement paths, explicit entity scope and dependencies, explicit option effects, and service declarations for confidence policy, recovery, and the entity/action combination. They are typed relationships, not a demand to rewrite every business fact or create one bespoke service for every noun phrase.

Example of the target contract

{
  "entities": [
    {
      "id": "ent.washer",
      "label": "Washer",
      "kind": "appliance",
      "family": "major_appliance",
      "parent_id": null,
      "aliases": ["washing machine", "washer", "clothes washer"],
      "questions": [
        {"id": "q.washer_type", "entity_scope": ["ent.washer"], "depends_on": [], "options": [
          {"id": "washer.front_load", "label": "Front-load", "affects": ["branch", "prep"]},
          {"id": "washer.top_load", "label": "Top-load", "affects": ["branch", "prep"]}
        ]},
        {"id": "q.washer_symptom", "entity_scope": ["ent.washer"], "depends_on": [], "options": [
          {"id": "sym.leaking", "label": "Leaking", "affects": ["branch", "prep"]}
        ]},
        {"id": "q.washer_leak_source", "entity_scope": ["ent.washer"], "depends_on": [
          {"question_id": "q.washer_symptom", "option_id": "sym.leaking"}
        ], "options": [
          {"id": "leak.hose", "label": "Hose or connection", "affects": ["price", "minutes", "prep"]},
          {"id": "leak.door_seal", "label": "Door seal", "affects": ["price", "minutes", "prep"]},
          {"id": "leak.unknown", "label": "Not sure", "affects": ["checkout_state"]}
        ]}
      ],
      "uncertainty_classes": [
        {"id": "u.washer_type", "retired_by": [{"question_id": "q.washer_type", "option_ids": ["washer.front_load", "washer.top_load"]}]},
        {"id": "u.symptom", "retired_by": [{"question_id": "q.washer_symptom", "option_ids": ["sym.leaking"]}]},
        {"id": "u.leak_source", "retired_by": [{"question_id": "q.washer_leak_source", "option_ids": ["leak.hose", "leak.door_seal"]}]}
      ],
      "services": [
        {
          "id": "svc.washer_repair",
          "entity_id": "ent.washer",
          "action_id": "act.repair",
          "modifier_ids": [],
          "uncertainty_profile": [
            {"class_id": "u.washer_type", "weight": 20},
            {"class_id": "u.symptom", "weight": 40},
            {"class_id": "u.leak_source", "weight": 30}
          ],
          "confidence_policy": {
            "exact_at": 0.80,
            "range_at": 0.50,
            "diagnostic_below": 0.50
          },
          "recovery_affordances": {"source": "current_open_uncertainty"}
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "actions": [{"id": "act.repair", "label": "Repair"}],
  "modifiers": [{"id": "mod.brick", "label": "Brick or concrete", "affects": ["complexity", "minutes"]}]
}

Scale is explicit: the Charter's scope-confidence value is normalized to 0–1, so thresholds use fractions such as 0.80 and 0.50, not mixed 0–100 percentages. The example is illustrative, not a schema substitute; the versioned schema defines exact field shapes and allowed values. Shared actions and modifiers may live in normalized top-level registries while the entity remains the primary authoring and navigation axis.

Dynamic-service composition. A dynamic service composes from the entity/action/modifier space. Every business fact it carries has an authored home in the entity-first source. A representation of a dynamic service that does not resolve to the entity/action/modifier space is not part of the domain model. Composition is the default; a service-specific override exists only when the composition alone cannot express the intended behavior, and that override is authored in the same shape as every other service declaration.

Workflow is a view, not the source of domain truth. The composer reads the entity graph, evidence, dependencies, and current open uncertainty to choose the next question. The resolver determines the current resolved state. The confidence engine measures uncertainty retirement. The recovery responsibility identifies the most useful next evidence path. The route builder maps the resolved state and confidence to an honest quote route. The Glue sequences those calls. The target module names and current responsibility assignments belong in the maintained component-responsibility map, which may evolve as implementation is migrated; this Charter states the durable ownership rules, not a frozen file list. No workflow table, module-specific copy, or caller fallback may become a second authority for relationships already declared in the SSOT.

Validation

Every change to the SSOT must validate against its schema. The schema is not self-enforcing — a comment claiming validation was run is not the same as running the validator. A validator that prints an error and exits zero is not validating; it is narrating.

The specific failure mode: a schema validator whose exit code never reflects its own findings. Every call must fail loudly and with a non-zero exit code when the SSOT is invalid, so that an automated check that only looks at the exit code actually catches the problem. Reference-check advisories — orphan modules, unused fields — may remain non-blocking; genuine schema violations may not.

P-SYSTEM-TAGSPrincipleTwo Tag Systems, Not One

Vision outcome protected: keeping recognition vocabulary separate from authored business meaning, so a matching label cannot silently become a pricing decision.

The NLP input path uses smart_tags from the SSOT. The catalog search path uses intent_mappings.objects. These are two distinct systems with overlapping but non-identical tag/keyword sets. Both live in the SSOT as data. They are not the same tag set, and the charter does not claim they are. Any future unification must be done as one deliberate change, with a migration path and a ledger entry.

Compiler boundary. The compiler may derive structure; it may not silently invent business knowledge. Derived artifacts have a different lifecycle from their source: they are not a second SSOT and are not freely regenerable. If compiler output contains hand-curated knowledge that the compiler cannot reproduce, the compiler is incomplete and the source knowledge must not be discarded.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — keeping recognition vocabulary separate from authored business meaning, so a matching label cannot silently become a pricing decision.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: keeping recognition vocabulary separate from authored business meaning, so a matching label cannot silently become a pricing decision.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that keeping recognition vocabulary separate from authored business meaning, so a matching label cannot silently become a pricing decision fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The Navigation Architecture

Canonical routing taxonomy. A routing mode describes how the request is understood: component_first, symptom_first, parent, or undetermined. Dynamic-chain classifications such as surface_repair and quantity_driven describe the shape of a chain, not additional routing modes. Use the canonical underscore spelling in machine data; hyphenated prose is explanatory only.

The catalog grid is not a flat list of every service. It is a structured descent through category, group, and — for a group whose services split into recognizable shapes — a behavioral-class-driven picker that asks the customer for the fact they already know, not the fact the backend wants. This section states how that descent is built, why it is structured this way, and how it stays inside the component-layer discipline every other part of the system follows. The customer-facing treatment of the picker and its companion tile is in Gateway A and Gateway B; this chapter is the rule's home for the classification and the layer discipline, and the customer chapters reference it rather than restating its content.

Every Group Carries a Routing Mode

Every group in the catalog is classified by the shape of work it contains. The classification is derived from the group's own aggregate module usage; it is not hand-authored per query, and it is the same classification the intake composer and the semantic route validator already consult. There is exactly one classification per group, and every subsystem that needs it reads from that one source.

Routing ModeWhat the customer arrives knowingHow the picker leads
component-firstThe part. (A lock, a lever, a fixture.)"Which one?" — components first; action refined second, only where the group has genuine action multiplicity for the selected component.
symptom-firstThe problem. (Won't spin, leaking, making noise.)"What's happening?" — symptoms first; each symptom resolves through its own real mapping.
parentNothing yet — this group delegates to its children.No picker. Child groups render as they always have.
undeterminedNothing structured — the group has no real signal either way.No picker. The group's structured builder opens directly.

Component and Symptom Are Two Shapes, Not Two Modes

It is tempting to think of component-led and symptom-led as two competing interface modes, and to look for a third for "action." That reading misses what the classification is actually telling us: for a given family of work, one of the two is what the customer arrives with, and the other isn't. A customer thinking about a deadbolt is not going to describe its symptoms; a customer whose refrigerator is making a strange noise is not going to name its compressor. The picker honours the shape the customer is actually holding.

Action is a real, orthogonal axis, but it belongs under the component or symptom choice, not beside them. For most groups, the component already names the action — "deadbolt" is already a noun for "install or replace a deadbolt." Where a group genuinely has more than one real action for a single component, the picker asks the action as a second, real question. Where it doesn't, it doesn't.

P-SYSTEM-PICKER-RENDERPrincipleWhat the Picker Is, and What It Is Not

Vision outcome protected: keeping the picker responsible for presenting choices rather than becoming a second resolver or business-logic layer.

The picker is a front door onto the canonical resolution. It reads the routing-mode classification and the group's own component and symptom vocabulary, and it renders whatever the customer's own next real choice should be.

The picker is not a resolution pipeline. It does not compose chains, compute confidence, or calculate price. Whatever the customer selects — a specific component, a specific symptom, or the group's "Other" tile — hands off to the same canonical collector and the same orchestrator the direct-tile tap uses. The picker is a Renderer; the resolution is Logic; the two are separate by layer, and the boundary is enforced.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — keeping the picker responsible for presenting choices rather than becoming a second resolver or business-logic layer.

Harm prevented — A component can take responsibility it does not own, duplicate another component’s decision, or couple an unrelated concern to a detail that later changes. The specific commitment at risk is: keeping the picker responsible for presenting choices rather than becoming a second resolver or business-logic layer.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that keeping the picker responsible for presenting choices rather than becoming a second resolver or business-logic layer fails under a moved responsibility, changed deployment boundary, or workaround that duplicates a concern, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

P-SYSTEM-PICKER-MODELPrincipleThe Picker Renders the Customer's Model, Not the Backend's

Vision outcome protected: presenting the customer’s conceptual model rather than exposing backend organization as a burden the customer must understand.

When several distinct component choices resolve to the same underlying service, the picker still renders each as a genuine, separate option. The customer's mental model is what the picker honours; the fact that the backend converges is an implementation detail the customer never sees and never needs to. Collapsing the picker's own tiles because the backend collapsed the resolution would trade a customer-recognizable choice for an internal convenience — exactly backwards.

This is a durable rule, not a styling preference. A picker that collapses choices to match a backend that has already unified them removes a real, meaningful decision the customer was making. The customer's own reasoning is what the picker exists to capture.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — presenting the customer’s conceptual model rather than exposing backend organization as a burden the customer must understand.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: presenting the customer’s conceptual model rather than exposing backend organization as a burden the customer must understand.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that presenting the customer’s conceptual model rather than exposing backend organization as a burden the customer must understand fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The Picker and Its Companion Tile

Every picker has a companion "Other" tile at the same level. When the picker's own vocabulary does not cover the customer's case — a component the group has not authored, a symptom outside the group's known set, a job that is genuinely a different shape from this family of work — the "Other" tile opens the group-scoped structured builder. The customer-facing treatment of the tile, and why it is a current and permanent part of the product rather than a transitional escape hatch, is in Gateway B — The "Other" Tile. This chapter states the pairing as an engineering fact; the doctrine belongs in Part I, and the two chapters reference each other rather than restating the same argument.

P-SYSTEM-CLASSIFY-ONEPrincipleThe Classification Is a Single Source, Not a Second Copy

Vision outcome protected: ensuring every layer uses the same routing classification, so the first question, intake chain, and resolved request do not contradict one another.

The routing-mode classification lives in derived data — a compiler output — and is consumed by every subsystem that needs it: the picker's own shape, the intake composer's branch selection, the semantic check that notices when a resolved service's own chain disagrees with what its group's routing mode would predict. There is exactly one classification, and every consumer reads from that one. A second copy, wherever it appeared, would drift — and that drift is a defect, not a variation.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — ensuring every layer uses the same routing classification, so the first question, intake chain, and resolved request do not contradict one another.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring every layer uses the same routing classification, so the first question, intake chain, and resolved request do not contradict one another.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that ensuring every layer uses the same routing classification, so the first question, intake chain, and resolved request do not contradict one another fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

R-SYSTEM-PICKER-AGREEA picker rule and its routing mode classification are never in disagreement. If the picker would lead with symptoms for a component-first group, either the picker is wrong or the classification is — and the disagreement is a defect to resolve at the source, not a variation to accept downstream.

Vision outcome protected: For every group, picker order and routing-mode classification agree; a deliberately contradictory classification is rejected or corrected before rendering.

Accomplishment: For every group, picker order and routing-mode classification agree; a deliberately contradictory classification is rejected or corrected before rendering.

In plain English: For every group, picker order and routing-mode classification agree; a deliberately contradictory classification is rejected or corrected before rendering.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — For every group, picker order and routing-mode classification agree; a deliberately contradictory classification is rejected or corrected before rendering.

Harm prevented — A valid request can fall outside the modeled domain or be forced through a symptom-only path or caller-side exception that loses facts needed to resolve the work. The specific commitment at risk is: for every group, picker order and routing-mode classification agree; a deliberately contradictory classification is rejected or corrected before rendering.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that for every group, picker order and routing-mode classification agree; a deliberately contradictory classification is rejected or corrected before rendering fails under a new entity, action, modifier, service combination, or routing mode, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

The Picker Respects the Layer Boundary

The picker is a renderer. It does not own the classification, it does not own the group's vocabulary, and it does not own the resolution. It reads state that the logic layer has already resolved and renders whatever the customer's next real choice is. The classification itself, the component and symptom mappings, and the resolution path all live in the layers their own charter rules assign them to; the picker's only job is to make the customer's next step legible and to hand off the customer's own selection.

When the picker needs to know what to show, it asks the logic layer. When it needs to know what the customer chose, it hands the choice back. It never computes a price, never composes a chain, and never inspects any global UI state to decide what a customer should see.

The NLP Layer

The NLP layer is what turns a client's sentence into a structured request the orchestrator can act on. It is the recognition half of the recognition-and-scoping process that every client walks through; the intake layer is the other half, and the two share more machinery than is obvious from the outside.

The same machinery produces the live "We Understood" preview in Gateway C. As the client types, the composer runs the recognition pass against the current text on every keystroke (or a tight debounce), fills what it can, and renders the result as an editable sentence. That sentence is not a separate summary of what the client typed — it is the system's actual current understanding of the request, expressed in natural English.

R-SYSTEM-ONEPARSERuleOne Parse, One Composer

In plain English: Every entry path uses the canonical parser and composer; equivalent request facts produce the same canonical intent and displayed sentence.

Vision outcome protected: Every entry path uses the canonical parser and composer; equivalent request facts produce the same canonical intent and displayed sentence.

Accomplishment: Every entry path uses the canonical parser and composer; equivalent request facts produce the same canonical intent and displayed sentence.

Serves:P-CLIENT-THREE · P-INVARIANT-NOPATCH

The input pipeline has exactly one canonical implementation. One function parses the client's words into structured intent. One function composes the sentence back out. Every real entry point — the live preview, the confirm button, the guided builder's close-sync, the builder finish — calls these. There is no second parser, no parallel sentence builder, no exception.

This is not an aesthetic preference. When two parsers exist, they eventually disagree, and the customer is the one who finds out — they watch one understanding compose on screen and get a different one priced at the end. That failure has a name in this project: the preview-vs-charge mismatch. It is the reason the single-parse rule is a structural test, not a policy.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Every entry path uses the canonical parser and composer; equivalent request facts produce the same canonical intent and displayed sentence.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: every entry path uses the canonical parser and composer; equivalent request facts produce the same canonical intent and displayed sentence.

Implementation freedom — Parsers and composers may be reorganized or replaced, provided equivalent request facts converge on one canonical interpretation and the displayed explanation faithfully expresses that interpretation.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every entry path uses the canonical parser and composer; equivalent request facts produce the same canonical intent and displayed sentence fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

What the Layer Sees

Recognition must be robust to how clients actually write. Real clients type in fragments; they include incidental words; they complain about the app itself mid-request; they use vocabulary the catalog has never seen. The layer must distinguish a stated need from an incidental mention, extract slots from sentences whose word order does not match the template, and route an unfamiliar phrase into the same behavioural machinery as an explicitly named service.

Four concrete defenses exist, each for a real, observed class of error:

  • Quoted and parenthetical content is stripped before scoring. A customer writing "I need a bed assembled (the app keeps suggesting 'drywall or plaster' which is nonsense)" must not be routed on the word plaster. Quoted text is a customer talking about the system, not to it.
  • Matches found beyond the first ~15 words score at half weight. A customer's real intent is overwhelmingly in the head of the sentence; trailing clauses are far more likely to be incidental. The weighting compounds with the synonym half-weight rule below rather than replacing it.
  • A winning candidate whose entire evidence came from the tail region has its named recommendation suppressed. General routing may still proceed; a confident named-service recommendation may not. The system never overstates certainty it does not have.
  • Every promoted synonym is checked against every other entry in the catalog. A synonym earns full confidence only when it is genuinely specific and unambiguous. Refrigerator earns full weight; range, burner, and nest do not, because each is a common English word with real, unrelated meanings.

Two Tag Systems, Not One

The NLP path consults smart_tags. The catalog path consults intent_mappings.objects. These are two distinct systems with overlapping but non-identical tag sets; they are not interchangeable, and they are not the same tag set. Both live in the SSOT as data. The full treatment — what each system answers, why they are distinct, and what any future unification would require — is in The Single Source of Truth.

The Cross-Entry Specificity Rule

Every entry in intent_mappings.objects is scored independently of every other. This is fine until an author writes a natural, customer-friendly multi-word synonym that happens to contain a different entry's bare keyword — e.g. "dish washer" contains "washer", and both are real synonyms for different appliances.

The resolution is not to lower the specificity of either entry. It is to enforce a single, precise rule: when the current winner's own bare keyword appears, as a whole word, inside a different entry's authored multi-word synonym or keyword, and that phrase genuinely appears in the customer's text, the more specific entry wins outright — provided it clears its own confidence bar on its own merits, and provided it is not itself a resolver-bearing entry whose whole job is to re-derive from a shared verb.

This rule is auditable at the data level. The project maintains a permanent detector that recomputes the full catalog-wide risk list on every run and fails loudly the instant a new pair appears. The count of reviewed-and-accepted risk pairs is a real, current number in the record, not a snapshot of a good day.

Grounding in the Catalog's Own Vocabulary

Object extraction is grounded in the SSOT's own vocabulary first, not in a hardcoded word list. When a customer types a noun the catalog already knows, the extractor finds it. When the noun is genuinely new, the extractor falls back to a constrained pattern, and the resulting phrase is evaluated by the same resolver that handles every other real request — no special case, no invented entity.

Two extractions of the same object exist, and they are deliberately distinct:

  • The strict noun — client-visible, held to the catalog's own vocabulary, quoted in the "We Understood" sentence.
  • The lenient noun — internal matching only, wider vocabulary, never quoted to the customer.

Keeping these apart is what allows the system to recognize a wider range of phrasings without promising the customer it understood a word it did not.

The Group-Scoped Inference

Some concepts are inferable from context when the customer's own words would not trigger them directly — a mantel implies brick, a vaulted ceiling implies height. These inferences are real, authored, and group-scoped: a keyword that fires only within a specific group, so that "fireplace" can imply the brick-wall tag in a wall-mounting context without bleeding the same inference into unrelated work. The three-level fact scoping described in the Domain chapter is the same discipline applied to the tag data itself.

An inference that does not carry its group context is dead on arrival. A group-scoped tag whose inference function is called without the group id will silently fail every time, and the failure looks like "the feature was never built" rather than "the feature lost its input." The group id must be threaded through the inference call the same way it is threaded through every other piece of the resolution pipeline.

R-SYSTEM-COMPOSERRuleThe Sentence Renderer Is Not a Parser

In plain English: The sentence renderer expresses resolved facts as customer-facing text; it does not parse input or make business decisions.

Vision outcome protected: The sentence renderer expresses resolved facts as customer-facing text; it does not parse input or make business decisions.

Accomplishment: The composer only renders structured intent returned by the engine; parsing rules exist in the NLP layer and no renderer independently interprets customer text.

Serves:P-SYSTEM-PICKER-RENDER · P-INVARIANT-RENDERERS

All parsing of a client's typed words lives in the NLP layer and nowhere else. The renderer contains zero parsing functions. It renders what the engine returns. This boundary is enforced structurally: the test suite fails if any module-level function is declared twice (the later copy silently shadows the earlier), if the renderer gains a parsing function, or if a module-level initializer reads SSOT state before the SSOT exists.

The structural test is not just a guard against a future mistake. It is the running record of two specific classes of mistake that have already happened: duplicate parsers (the preview pipeline carrying its own object extractor separate from the canonical one) and early-load readers (the builder's room and condition choices computed at script parse time, before the SSOT was available).

Constraint-specific rule:R-SYSTEM-SCRIPT-SCOPE-IS-REAL. Its existence depends on R-SYSTEM-SINGLEFILECOST; if the one-file deployment constraint is retired, reassess this obligation.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — The sentence renderer expresses resolved facts as customer-facing text; it does not parse input or make business decisions.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: the sentence renderer expresses resolved facts as customer-facing text; it does not parse input or make business decisions.

Implementation freedom — The rendering technology and sentence format may change. The sentence renderer must express resolved facts rather than independently interpreting customer input or making business decisions.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that the sentence renderer expresses resolved facts as customer-facing text; it does not parse input or make business decisions fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

What the Layer Guarantees

  • The same request reaches the same understanding. One parser, one composer, one set of grounded vocabulary.
  • The live preview is the price. The sentence the customer watches compose is the sentence the price is built from — not a paraphrase, not a re-derivation.
  • An unfamiliar phrase falls through to the same machinery as a familiar one. Dynamic services, group resolution, and the fallback chain are the same paths for a "smart mirror" as for a dishwasher.
  • Weak evidence never masquerades as strong evidence. A match from the tail, a match from a quoted aside, a match from a common-word synonym — each is scored honestly, and none can be promoted into a named-service recommendation on its own.
  • A genuinely unfinished parse is not silently priced. When the parse does not reach a real entity, the composer opens the guided builder rather than inventing a service, and the customer is asked the questions that will complete the understanding.
The layer's whole job, in one sentence: turn whatever the client typed into the same structured intent the rest of the pipeline would have produced if the client had tapped the right service card — and do it without inventing anything the client did not say.

Independent Decision Dimensions and One Derived Checkout Projection

The orchestrator composes three independent decision dimensions and one derived checkout projection. This is the single most important architectural decision in the project: it allows the system to scale without coupling business decisions to presentation.

Dimensional contract. Entity, Routing Mode, and Pricing Mode are independent resolved decisions. Checkout State is not a fourth peer input: it is a downstream presentation projection derived from the resolved quote precision and authored duration policy. It must not be independently guessed by a renderer or selected from an entity label.
ConcernDefinesDoes not define
Routing ModeHow confidence is builtThe UI or pricing
Pricing ModeHow cost is calculatedThe service itself
EntityWhat exists in the worldHow it's routed or priced
Checkout StateHow the price is presented to the clientWhat the price is

Authoring hierarchy. Entity is the primary authored identity. Routing Mode and Pricing Mode are operational decisions; Checkout State is a downstream presentation projection. Decision dimensions are orthogonal in execution, not co-equal in authoring. Checkout State is derived from resolved quote precision and authored duration policy; it is not independently inferred from an entity, routing label, or pricing label. Routing Mode and Pricing Mode are attached to the resolved composition, not authored identity layers through which every service must be defined. A new entity inherits useful behavior without any subsystem needing to know the others exist; its authored identity remains the entity/action/modifier composition.

Two Clarifications

Entity is the conceptual primitive: the thing in the world being discussed (for example, a Door Lever, Window, or Toilet). It is the same conceptual entity used by the Domain Model; the decision dimensions and derived checkout projection do not introduce a second "Item Primitive" concept.

Checkout State is a presentation/output concept, not a pricing method. Pricing determines the monetary result; checkout state determines how that result is expressed to the client (for example, exact, ranged, conditional, or diagnostic). It therefore belongs to the output side of the pricing pipeline while remaining separate from the pricing calculation itself.

Layered Architecture

Every function has one cohesive responsibility and belongs to exactly one architectural layer: Knowledge, Logic, Rendering, or Glue. A component may depend only on the contracts permitted for its layer; no function may straddle layers to “save a call” or duplicate another layer’s work. Module names can change; these responsibility boundaries cannot. Current assignments are tracked in the maintained component-responsibility map, while this Charter states the enduring law.

RoleOwnsMust Not Own
KnowledgeBusiness truth, declared as data.Executable logic. Branching. Calculations.
LogicPure computation, state transitions, resolution, pricing.DOM access. Global window UI state. Inline event handlers.
RenderingDOM construction from resolved state.Business logic, pricing, or resolution.
GlueWiring events to logic calls. Bootstrap.Business rules, pricing, resolution.
R-SYSTEM-LAYERSThe hard rule: a function has one responsibility. If it appears to span roles, it must be split — not documented as an exception.

Vision outcome protected: Static boundary analysis identifies forbidden cross-layer imports/calls, and every exception is eliminated or represented by a separately justified architecture change rather than a silent bypass.

Accomplishment: Static boundary analysis identifies forbidden cross-layer imports/calls, and every exception is eliminated or represented by a separately justified architecture change rather than a silent bypass.

In plain English: Static boundary analysis identifies forbidden cross-layer imports/calls, and every exception is eliminated or represented by a separately justified architecture change rather than a silent bypass.

R-INVARIANT-BOUNDARYLayer assignment is mechanically enforced, not reviewed. A Rendering module must not read session state (S.*), the pricing SSOT (authoritative pricing data, authoritative pricing formulas, registered checkout policy), or call engine functions (the canonical quote calculation, the canonical confidence calculation, the service-resolution mechanism, the category-tag validation mechanism, the confidence-policy selection mechanism, the pricing-formula application mechanism). Enforcement is a structural test — a source-level analysis of each function body — not a code review and not a naming convention. This operationalizes the general "no business logic in renderers" Principle (P-INVARIANT-RENDERERS) to a specific, checkable subset. The general rule remains a Principle; the specific rule is a Rule.

Vision outcome protected: The layer analyzer fails when rendering reads session/pricing state or invokes business engines, and passes when those dependencies are removed.

The binding rule applies to every dependency across presentation-to-state, presentation-to-pricing, and presentation-to-engine boundaries, independent of identifier or implementation language. Enforcement must discover these dependencies from ownership, import, or call relationships rather than rely on a manually maintained list of names.

Accomplishment: the selected enforcement mechanism detects prohibited dependencies across presentation, state, pricing, and decision-engine boundaries; the rule remains enforceable if component names or implementation language change.

Historical evidence: Battle 13 — A debounce is not a fix

Presentation consumes resolved facts and does not own or invoke business decisions.

Harm prevented — Rendering can independently reinterpret a request or invoke business decisions, allowing the preview and committed quote to diverge and making layer ownership impossible to enforce consistently.

Separation of Concerns

  • UI / Rendering is dumb: it renders supplied state, emits user intent through the canonical dispatch boundary, and owns no resolution, confidence, pricing, business branching, or session-state interpretation.
  • Knowledge is declarative only. No JavaScript inside the SSOT file.
  • Extracted standalone modules must stay in sync with their source. Any change to the source must be followed by re-extraction and a parity check.
  • No duplicated implementations. If two functions solve the same problem, one must be canonical and the others delegate.
  • Legacy code must be explicitly marked. Bug fixes are permitted; new features are not.
  • Any list, constant, or registry used by more than one function must be defined once. A duplicated definition is a confirmed recurring bug class.

Rendering Rules

  • All renderers are pure functions of resolved state.
  • State mutations go through the canonical dispatch path.

  • R-SYSTEM-HOSTSEvery renderer owns a render host, and no two renderers share one. A renderer may clear only the host it owns. A host is never nested inside another concern’s host; two renderers never write to the same host; no renderer may clear an ancestor of another concern’s host.

  • Event handlers must not contain business logic.
  • Renderers must clear containers before re-rendering — stale content is a real, confirmed bug class.
