Derived Data Lifecycle — Canonical Rule in the SSOT

The compiler may derive executable structure from the SSOT, but derived data is not a second source of business truth. The complete lifecycle rule is defined canonically in The Single Source of Truth.

ResolvedRoute vs. BookingContext:BookingContext is the canonical convergence contract shared by all gateways. ResolvedRoute is the orchestrator's resolved execution result, which may carry route metadata alongside the resulting BookingContext. They are related but not aliases; their exact object fields belong to the owning implementation specification.

Runtime Resolution and Recompute Contract

The Charter's Complexity → Uncertainty → Evidence → Confidence → Pricing relationship is a state-transition contract, not merely a conceptual sequence. The trigger is the acceptance of new canonical evidence.

CUSTOMER INPUT
  navigation / NLP / catalog / chips / intake evidence
        ↓
BOOKING CONTEXT
  intent facts + scope facts + evidence
        ↓
RESOLVED JOB SHAPE
  entity → capability → action → service / dynamic service
        ↓
COMPLEXITY PROFILE
  skill / risk / batch risk; duration as derived consequence
        ↓
RELEVANT UNCERTAINTY
  price / labor / routing / scheduling / preparation
        ↓
EVIDENCE STATE
        ↓
Cₓ + τₓ
        ↓
PERMITTED PRICING PRECISION
  exact / bounded / conditional / diagnostic
        ↓
NEXT INTAKE ACTION OR PRICING / CHECKOUT PATH

The arrows are dependency boundaries. They are not independent pipelines. An upstream change invalidates every downstream value that depends on it.

Trigger. Every accepted customer fact or evidence event that mutates canonical BookingContext triggers resolution from the resulting context before the system selects the next intake action or presents a new pricing outcome. Presentation-only state — opening a drawer, focusing a field, animating a chip, or changing layout without changing canonical facts — does not trigger business resolution.

Recompute. The resulting state is recomputed from the post-mutation BookingContext. The implementation may optimize internally, but an optimized result must be observationally equivalent to a fresh resolution from the resulting context. No stale downstream value may survive merely because the code path that produced it did not revisit that layer.

Ordering. The system does not ask the next question from a stale chain. It first applies the evidence, resolves the job shape and applicable uncertainty, recomputes confidence and threshold policy, determines the permitted precision, and only then chooses the next evidence action or pricing path.

Feedback is permitted, direct arithmetic coupling is not. New evidence may reveal a different job shape. A changed job shape may change the relevant uncertainty set. A changed uncertainty set may lower or raise Cₓ. A changed Cₓ may change the precision that is currently justified. This is not circularity; it is state evolution. What is prohibited is a direct formula such as Cₓ = f(complexity_score) that bypasses the uncertainty ledger.

Input mechanisms do not own the model. Navigation, NLP, catalog selection, chips, guided builders, and intake modules may contribute facts or evidence. They do not independently calculate pricing, complexity, confidence, or checkout state. The orchestrator remains the authority for resolution and pricing.

R-RESOLUTION-RECOMPUTEEvery canonical evidence mutation must be followed by downstream re-resolution before the next decision. The mechanical test mutates one BookingContext fact at a time, records the incremental result, then resolves a fresh context containing the same resulting facts. The two results must agree for resolved scope, complexity profile, active uncertainty classes, retired uncertainty, Cᵢ, Cₓ, τₓ, precision policy, pricing result, and checkout state. The same test verifies that the next intake action is selected from the new state rather than from the pre-answer chain.

Vision outcome protected: After each accepted BookingContext evidence mutation, incremental resolution equals fresh resolution from the resulting context before the next intake or pricing decision.

Accomplishment: After each accepted BookingContext evidence mutation, incremental resolution equals fresh resolution from the resulting context before the next intake or pricing decision.

In plain English: After each accepted BookingContext evidence mutation, incremental resolution equals fresh resolution from the resulting context before the next intake or pricing decision.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — After each accepted BookingContext evidence mutation, incremental resolution equals fresh resolution from the resulting context before the next intake or pricing decision.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: after each accepted bookingcontext evidence mutation, incremental resolution equals fresh resolution from the resulting context before the next intake or pricing decision.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that after each accepted bookingcontext evidence mutation, incremental resolution equals fresh resolution from the resulting context before the next intake or pricing decision fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

The Canonical Execution Path

Input
  ↓
Detect / collect context
  ↓
Resolve entity + action + modifiers
  ↓
Apply routing/strategy policy where an independently defined execution purpose exists
  ↓
Build confidence and scope
  ↓
Resolve service (curated, or dynamically composed from the same entity/action/modifier space)
  ↓
Calculate price
  ↓
Return ResolvedRoute / BookingContext
  ↓
Render

Strategy and Capability are execution concepts, not mandatory authored identity layers. They may appear in the operational path only where they have an independently defined execution purpose (see Independent Decision Dimensions and One Derived Checkout Projection and the Component Layer Map). They do not replace the entity/action/modifier composition model, and they are not intermediate identity stages that a service must be authored through.

The Constitutional Diagnosis — One Artifact, One Purpose

Scope and consolidation decisions.
  • P-INVARIANT-RENDERERS remains a Principle for the broad architectural direction; its mechanically checkable layer boundary is stated as R-INVARIANT-BOUNDARY. This is a deliberate scope distinction, not an omitted promotion.
  • P-INVARIANT-VERIFY remains a Principle for verifying the complete relevant set. Its binary, testable obligations are stated in R-TEST-CANFAIL and R-INVARIANT-BEHAVIORAL. A general verification philosophy is not itself one indivisible mechanical check.
  • R-INVARIANT-RERUN is retired as a single combined trigger. Its two separate obligations are retained explicitly by R-INVARIANT-DERIVATION: an SSOT change triggers derivation, and an SSOT change triggers validation. They remain separate tools, reports, exit statuses, and lifecycles; the retirement removes the misleading single-trigger identity, not either obligation.
  • P-SYSTEM-TAGS describes the distinct authored tag/keyword systems only. Pricing-factor semantics belong to P-INVARIANT-CHIPSNOTFEES, avoiding duplicate ownership.
R-INVARIANT-DERIVATION is a one-direction dependency, not a mutual prerequisite. Derivation requires valid input and therefore runs after validation succeeds; validation does not require derivation to have run. That is what “neither is a hidden prerequisite of the other” means: validation is callable without derivation, and derivation cannot silently produce output from invalid source. A caller that treats validation and derivation as interchangeable, or that skips one by invoking the other, violates the retirement of R-INVARIANT-RERUN.

The recurring failures are not a random collection of bugs. They share one structural cause: an artifact was asked to serve two purposes at once, and no explicit boundary said which purpose it owned. The SSOT was expected to be both human-authored knowledge and machine-optimized runtime structure. The compiler was asked to derive output and validate correctness. Tests were asked to verify behavior while pinning source shape. A single HTML file was asked to be both deployment unit and code-organization model. The Charter itself was asked to be durable law and a changing status ledger. Each dual role made one responsibility harder to change without damaging the other.

The remedy is not abstraction for its own sake. It is to name each artifact’s role, give each role an authoritative home, and make transitions explicit. The domain substrate must also be entity-first: author the customer-named noun, action, modifiers, uncertainty, question scope, dependencies, and effects so downstream modules consume declared knowledge rather than reconstructing it. The authored source contains business knowledge; the schema states valid shape; derivation produces machine-oriented output; validation returns a verdict; the runtime consumes a published version; tests verify behavior and contracts; evidence records what was observed; and this Charter states durable law. The governed authoring surface, derivation, validation, runtime, verification, historical record, and Charter are complementary responsibilities, not interchangeable sources of truth.

This is a governing direction, not a claim that every migration is complete. The single-file runtime constraint is named because it has real costs; it is not a timeless product principle. The project may retain it if its deployment benefit justifies the cost, or retire it when evidence supports doing so. What is forbidden is treating a contingent constraint as invisible and allowing its workarounds to harden into permanent architecture.

G-TEAM-ROLE-SEPARATIONGovernance ProcessSeparate Product, Engineering, Governance, and Verification Decisions

When a change contains a product decision, architectural decision, governance amendment, and verification, treat these as distinct decisions even if one agent performs them. Product determines the intended customer outcome; engineering determines the implementation; governance resolves authority and amendments; verification independently tests the resulting claim. Evidence for one does not automatically validate the others.

Accomplishment: the change record identifies the intended outcome, implementation choice, any governance amendment, and evidence used to verify behavior as distinct items.

G-SYSTEM-ROLE-DECLARATIONGovernance ProcessDeclare Artifact Roles

When creating or materially repurposing a maintained artifact, record its role in a header, manifest, or owning specification. Do not create a heavyweight registry merely to restate obvious file names; use the smallest authoritative place that makes the role discoverable.

Accomplishment: a maintainer can identify the artifact’s purpose, authority, consumers, and lifecycle from its owning record without inferring them from convention alone.

G-SYSTEM-TRANSITION-CLOSUREGovernance ProcessOpen and Close Migrations Explicitly

When a structural change creates two live sources, open a transition record before or as part of the change. Record start date, owner, target end date, completion detector, expected migration cost, and rollback/closure condition. Close the record only when the detector proves the old source is no longer authoritative.

Accomplishment: no live dual-source migration lacks a record, and a completed record links to evidence that the old source was retired.

G-CONTENT-STRUCTUREGovernance ProcessDeclare Whether a Change Is Content, Structure, or Both

Before a material change, identify whether it changes authored business meaning, the shape or execution of the system, or both. A content-only change preserves code and contracts unless the current contract cannot express the intended content. A structure-only change preserves curated business meaning. A change that genuinely spans both states the two outcomes separately and verifies each; the labels must not be used to block a necessary foundational correction.

Accomplishment: the change description names content and structural effects separately, and the diff/verification shows that no unrelated business meaning or architecture was silently changed.

R-SYSTEM-ONEPURPOSERuleOne Artifact, One Authoritative Purpose

In plain English: Each artifact has one authoritative job; do not make it both source, compiler, validator, runtime index, or status ledger.

Vision outcome protected: Each artifact has one authoritative job; do not make it both source, compiler, validator, runtime index, or status ledger.

An artifact must have one declared authoritative role. The SSOT owns authored business content; the schema defines valid source shape; derivation produces the runtime representation; validation produces correctness verdicts; the governed authoring surface authors and publishes; the runtime consumes; tests verify behavior or a separately named contract; the timeline records history; the Charter states durable law. An artifact that must be changed in one role to satisfy an unrelated role is a design defect, not a preference.

Accomplishment: every production artifact has a named role and owner; no consumer relies on content that exists only in a derived artifact; tests can validate without invoking derivation; and changes cross role boundaries only through explicit, versioned interfaces.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Each artifact has one authoritative job; do not make it both source, compiler, validator, runtime index, or status ledger.

Harm prevented — An ungoverned edit path can publish invalid or conflicting business knowledge, leaving derived output inconsistent with what a human intended to author.

Implementation freedom — Artifact boundaries may be reorganized, split, or combined when justified, but each resulting artifact must have one clear authoritative purpose; a file must not become competing sources of truth merely to avoid a structural decision.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a new authoring workflow or publishing boundary cannot preserve validation, provenance, and a single authoritative source without creating an ungoverned alternate path. Amend the mechanism through governance; do not bypass the commitment silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-SINGLEFILECOSTRuleThe One-File Constraint Must Be Explicit and Revisitable

In plain English: each constraint-dependent rule references this Rule; a retain/retire decision names deployment benefit, engineering cost, and testability impact; if retired, solely dependent workarounds are reviewed for deletion.

Vision outcome protected: each constraint-dependent rule references this Rule; a retain/retire decision names deployment benefit, engineering cost, and testability impact; if retired, solely dependent workarounds are reviewed for deletion.

The single-HTML-file runtime is a deployment constraint, not a requirement that all logic live in one conceptual module. Every workaround caused by it—script-tag scope handling, sandbox extraction, source-shape checks, CSP accommodations, and line-sensitive splicing—must identify that dependency. No contingent constraint may remain invisible while downstream workarounds harden into permanent architecture.

Accomplishment: each constraint-dependent rule references this Rule; a retain/retire decision names deployment benefit, engineering cost, and testability impact; if retired, solely dependent workarounds are reviewed for deletion.

Implementation freedom — The single-file constraint is contingent, not constitutional. Deployment conditions may justify retaining or retiring it; if it changes, revisit workarounds that existed only because of it rather than preserving them by inertia.

R-TEST-CANFAILRuleA Test Must Be Capable of Failing for the Defect It Claims to Detect

In plain English: the verification record names the behavior and counterfactual; missing prerequisites fail loudly or are reported as non-passing skips; and the check fails for the target defect and passes for corrected behavior.

Vision outcome protected: the verification record names the behavior and counterfactual; missing prerequisites fail loudly or are reported as non-passing skips; and the check fails for the target defect and passes for corrected behavior.

A green result is meaningful only if the check reaches the behavior under test and distinguishes failure from success. A renderer test that silently returns, a parity test comparing two broken implementations, a test whose sides call the same function, or a skipped check that exits successfully is not evidence for the claimed behavior. Demonstrate sensitivity with a targeted mutation, deliberately broken fixture, known failing baseline, or equivalent counterfactual.

Accomplishment: the verification record names the behavior and counterfactual; missing prerequisites fail loudly or are reported as non-passing skips; and the check fails for the target defect and passes for corrected behavior.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — the verification record names the behavior and counterfactual; missing prerequisites fail loudly or are reported as non-passing skips; and the check fails for the target defect and passes for corrected behavior.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: the verification record names the behavior and counterfactual; missing prerequisites fail loudly or are reported as non-passing skips; and the check fails for the target defect and passes for corrected behavior.

Implementation freedom — Test frameworks and fixtures may change. Evidence must be capable of detecting the failure it claims to detect and must not manufacture expected results through the same logic under test.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that the verification record names the behavior and counterfactual; missing prerequisites fail loudly or are reported as non-passing skips; and the check fails for the target defect and passes for corrected behavior fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-TEST-SEMANTICDIFFRuleA Change Gate Must Distinguish Semantics from Formatting

In plain English: A change-based gate either normalizes formatting before comparison or requires a formatting-only change to be isolated in its own commit; a gate that goes red on a wholesale reformat for functions the reformat did not touch fails verification.

Vision outcome protected: Review effort follows changes in meaning and behavior rather than formatting noise.

Accomplishment: A change-based gate either normalizes formatting before comparison or requires a formatting-only change to be isolated in its own commit; a gate that goes red on a wholesale reformat for functions the reformat did not touch fails verification.

Serves:P-GOVERN-GOODHART · R-TEST-CANFAIL

Historical evidence: Battle 17 — A gate that cannot tell formatting from behavior

A change-based gate compares base and head and fails any function that does not comply with its layer. That is the right rule. But a wholesale reformat makes almost every function “changed” and the gate goes red on functions the reformat did not touch semantically. The gate is correct in its rule but wrong in its measurement: it cannot tell a semantic change from formatting noise. When the diff is dominated by formatting, the gate either normalizes formatting before comparison or the formatting change is isolated into its own commit so semantic changes are gated on their merits.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Review effort follows changes in meaning and behavior rather than formatting noise.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: review effort follows changes in meaning and behavior rather than formatting noise.

Implementation freedom — The comparison tool and representation may change. A gate must distinguish meaning-changing edits from formatting-only changes to the degree required by the artifact’s purpose.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that review effort follows changes in meaning and behavior rather than formatting noise fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-INVARIANT-FIXEDPOINTRuleArtifacts Must Have a Stable Reproducible Form

In plain English: identical inputs yield byte-identical canonical output; integrity values have a defined canonicalization rule; and validity does not depend on editing a self-referential value.

Vision outcome protected: identical inputs yield byte-identical canonical output; integrity values have a defined canonicalization rule; and validity does not depend on editing a self-referential value.

An artifact that refers to or reproduces itself must have a defined stable fixed point, or the self-reference must be removed. Hashes cannot depend on a hash embedded in the same content; generation cannot vary with process hash seed or incidental iteration order; output cannot drift on a second run; and validity cannot depend on the artifact's own mutable line shape. Prefer sidecars, canonical serialization, or separate history artifacts over local patches.

Accomplishment: identical inputs yield byte-identical canonical output; integrity values have a defined canonicalization rule; and validity does not depend on editing a self-referential value.

Rule health & evolvability

Implementation freedom — Serialization and canonicalization methods may evolve. Equivalent input must settle into a stable, reproducible representation without unbounded self-reference or oscillation.

R-SYSTEM-DUALSOURCERuleTwo Live Sources Are a Bounded Migration, Never a Steady State

In plain English: Two editable sources may coexist only during an explicit bounded migration, never as a steady state.

Vision outcome protected: Two editable sources may coexist only during an explicit bounded migration, never as a steady state.

A migration that leaves two live sources must name its start, owner, completion condition, detector, expected cost, and target end date. 'Both are authoritative' is not a steady state. The detector must prove consumers use the target source and the old source can be removed without changing intended behavior. If the end date passes, close, explicitly reauthorize with a new reason and end date, or roll back; silent extension is prohibited.

Accomplishment: every active dual-source transition has all six fields in the transition ledger; its detector returns complete/not-complete; completion disconnects or deletes the retired source.

Rule health & evolvability

The specific commitment at risk is: two editable sources may coexist only during an explicit bounded migration, never as a steady state.

R-SYSTEM-IDENTITYRuleIdentity Is an ID; a Name Is Human-Facing Text

In plain English: schema validation rejects missing/duplicate IDs and invalid references; label renaming preserves relationships; and reference checks find no runtime lookup keyed by human-facing text.

Vision outcome protected: schema validation rejects missing/duplicate IDs and invalid references; label renaming preserves relationships; and reference checks find no runtime lookup keyed by human-facing text.

Every independently referenceable entity—services, entities, groups, options, modules, tags, formulas, materials, states, and routing concepts—has a stable identifier. Machine references use IDs, not labels, titles, spelling, or punctuation. Renaming display text must not break relationships. References use one canonical spelling, grammar, and target type; aliases are normalized at a deliberate import boundary, not scattered through runtime logic.

R-SYSTEM-DEPLOYORDERRuleDeployment Compatibility Must Be Explicit

In plain English: published artifacts carry relevant versions; the runtime checks them before customer work; and a deliberately mismatched fixture is refused with an actionable error.

Target contractThis states the intended steady-state architecture, not a claim that migration is complete. Any transitional implementation must be explicit, bounded, evidence-backed, and closed through the existing transition governance; this label is not a live status field.

Vision outcome protected: published artifacts carry relevant versions; the runtime checks them before customer work; and a deliberately mismatched fixture is refused with an actionable error.

Artifacts sharing a contract declare compatible source-schema, derivation, and runtime-interface versions. A release states deployment order or ships atomically. At startup, the runtime refuses a known incompatible combination with a clear diagnostic instead of continuing with a mixed shape that can throw, misroute, or misprice.

R-SYSTEM-CMSRuleOne Governed Human-Authoring Surface

In plain English: People author business knowledge through one governed workflow; validation and publication remain distinct from derivation.

Target contract. This defines the intended steady state; it is not a migration-status claim. Any transition must remain explicit, bounded, evidence-backed, and governed.

Vision outcome protected: Trusted business knowledge has one governed origin, with validation and publication boundaries that do not depend on the chosen authoring interface.

The intended steady state has one governed human-authoring surface for domain knowledge. That surface owns the authoring workflow, stable identity, schema-aware editing, reference integrity, validation feedback, and governed publication. The surface may be implemented or replaced differently; derivation remains a distinct, independently testable responsibility and must not create a competing authoring authority. Direct edits to derived output are not an alternate source of truth.

Accomplishment: routine content changes can be authored and validated through the one governed authoring surface; the supported publication path invokes required checks and derivation; and no alternate human-editing workflow creates a competing authority for the same source.

Rule health & evolvability

In the intended steady state, human-authored business knowledge has one governed authoring surface with explicit validation, provenance, and publication boundaries. The surface may be implemented or replaced differently; any transitional or alternate authoring path must be explicit, bounded, governed, and retired when it no longer serves a necessary transition. This Rule states the target contract and does not assert migration status.

Harm prevented — An ungoverned edit path can publish invalid or conflicting business knowledge, leaving derived output inconsistent with what a human intended to author.

Implementation freedom — The authoring surface may be implemented or replaced differently if it preserves one governed human-authoring authority, validation, provenance, and safe publication. The authoring-surface term describes a responsibility, not a vendor, UI, or current migration status.

R-SYSTEM-DEPLOY-CONTRACTRuleThe Runtime Consumes Only the Purely Derived Artifact

In plain English: The runtime consumes validated derived data, not the authoring source or a hybrid fallback.

Target contractThis states the intended steady-state architecture, not a claim that migration is complete. Any transitional implementation must be explicit, bounded, evidence-backed, and closed through the existing transition governance; this label is not a live status field.

Vision outcome protected: The runtime consumes validated derived data, not the authoring source or a hybrid fallback.

The authored source is for authoring and governance; the derived artifact is the runtime contract. The engine fetches or loads only the purely derived artifact, never the authored source as a fallback and never a hybrid file containing both curated source content and generated indexes. The runtime reads business data from the artifact; business knowledge is read through the artifact by following the provenance reference the artifact carries — source identity, source version and hash, and derivation version — so every value the artifact contains is traceable to the business knowledge that authored it. The publish flow validates the source, derives the artifact, verifies freshness and reproducibility, and refuses publication if the artifact is missing, invalid, stale, incompatible, or not reproducible from the source. Runtime and artifact versions must satisfy R-SYSTEM-DEPLOYORDER.

Accomplishment: runtime-loading checks show only the derived artifact is consumed; a stale or deliberately mismatched artifact causes the publish gate to fail; and the runtime refuses an incompatible published contract rather than falling back to source or legacy data.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — The runtime consumes validated derived data, not the authoring source or a hybrid fallback.

Harm prevented — A valid request can fall outside the modeled domain or be forced through a symptom-only path or caller-side exception that loses facts needed to resolve the work. The specific commitment at risk is: the runtime consumes validated derived data, not the authoring source or a hybrid fallback.

Implementation freedom — The artifact format, compiler, and deployment mechanism may evolve. The invariant is a validated, reproducible boundary between human-authored source and runtime-consumed representation, with no silent competing authority.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that the runtime consumes validated derived data, not the authoring source or a hybrid fallback fails under a new entity, action, modifier, service combination, or routing mode, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-COMPILER-NOT-TESTRuleBehavioral Tests Do Not Use the Compiler to Manufacture Their Expected Results

In plain English: behavioral and contract tests can run without invoking derivation to manufacture expected values; compiler tests separately demonstrate both correct output and failure on deliberately invalid or changed input.

Vision outcome protected: preventing tests from using the implementation under test to manufacture expected answers and thereby certify its own mistakes.

Tests of runtime behavior or source/output contracts must not invoke the production compiler to create the expected result they then compare against; that makes the same derivation defect capable of contaminating both actual and expected values. Those tests use independently authored expectations, known fixtures, or a separately defined contract. The compiler itself must still have direct tests of derivation, determinism, preservation of curated knowledge, and invalid-input rejection. Validation tests invoke validation independently of derivation, as required by R-INVARIANT-DERIVATION.

Accomplishment: behavioral and contract tests can run without invoking derivation to manufacture expected values; compiler tests separately demonstrate both correct output and failure on deliberately invalid or changed input.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — preventing tests from using the implementation under test to manufacture expected answers and thereby certify its own mistakes.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: preventing tests from using the implementation under test to manufacture expected answers and thereby certify its own mistakes.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that preventing tests from using the implementation under test to manufacture expected answers and thereby certify its own mistakes fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-ONECONCERNRuleCross-Cutting Mechanics Have One Canonical Implementation

In plain English: call-site analysis identifies competing implementations of the same mechanics; each shared behavior has one canonical implementation, and any intentionally distinct policy has a named contract and separate evidence.

Vision outcome protected: call-site analysis identifies competing implementations of the same mechanics; each shared behavior has one canonical implementation, and any intentionally distinct policy has a named contract and separate evidence.

Once a concern has an owner, its shared mechanics must be implemented once. Callers supply inputs and consume the result; they do not maintain competing copies of quantity handling, tag parsing, defaults merging, confidence arithmetic, or other shared mechanics. Distinct policies may differ only when the business distinction is declared and the difference is intentional.

Accomplishment: call-site analysis identifies competing implementations of the same mechanics; each shared behavior has one canonical implementation, and any intentionally distinct policy has a named contract and separate evidence.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — call-site analysis identifies competing implementations of the same mechanics; each shared behavior has one canonical implementation, and any intentionally distinct policy has a named contract and separate evidence.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: call-site analysis identifies competing implementations of the same mechanics; each shared behavior has one canonical implementation, and any intentionally distinct policy has a named contract and separate evidence.

Implementation freedom — Modules and ownership boundaries may be refactored. The enduring requirement is one canonical implementation for each cross-cutting behavior, with explicit interfaces between concerns.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that call-site analysis identifies competing implementations of the same mechanics; each shared behavior has one canonical implementation, and any intentionally distinct policy has a named contract and separate evidence fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-CONCERN-HOMERuleEvery Cross-Cutting Concern Has One Proper Module Home

In plain English: each cross-cutting concern has one documented owner; static or targeted analysis finds no concern computed inside a module whose declared primary responsibility differs; and moving a call site does not move ownership or create a shadow implementation.

Vision outcome protected: each cross-cutting concern has one documented owner; static or targeted analysis finds no concern computed inside a module whose declared primary responsibility differs; and moving a call site does not move ownership or create a shadow implementation.

Every cross-cutting concern has one named module that owns its meaning, decision, and canonical computation. A module may call another concern's owner, but it may not absorb that concern merely because it is already touching the call path. A concern computed inside a module whose primary responsibility is different is a defect even if the computation is currently correct and even if only one copy exists. This is concern absorption, not merely duplication.

For example, pricing may consume confidence to select a pricing route; it does not own confidence arithmetic. The confidence engine owns measurement, the composer owns question-chain composition, the resolver owns current resolved interpretation, the recovery responsibility selects evidence paths, and the route builder maps resolved state and confidence to a route. The Glue sequences these responsibilities without absorbing their decisions.

Accomplishment: each cross-cutting concern has one documented owner; static or targeted analysis finds no concern computed inside a module whose declared primary responsibility differs; and moving a call site does not move ownership or create a shadow implementation.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — each cross-cutting concern has one documented owner; static or targeted analysis finds no concern computed inside a module whose declared primary responsibility differs; and moving a call site does not move ownership or create a shadow implementation.

Harm prevented — A component can take responsibility it does not own, duplicate another component’s decision, or couple an unrelated concern to a detail that later changes. The specific commitment at risk is: each cross-cutting concern has one documented owner; static or targeted analysis finds no concern computed inside a module whose declared primary responsibility differs; and moving a call site does not move ownership or create a shadow implementation.

Implementation freedom — The module map may evolve. Every cross-cutting concern must have one identifiable owner rather than drifting into parallel implementations across callers.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that each cross-cutting concern has one documented owner; static or targeted analysis finds no concern computed inside a module whose declared primary responsibility differs; and moving a call site does not move ownership or create a shadow implementation fails under a moved responsibility, changed deployment boundary, or workaround that duplicates a concern, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-INVARIANT-DERIVATIONRuleDerivation and Validation Are Separate Acts

In plain English: validation runs without generating output; derivation refuses invalid input; each has its own exit status and report; tests validate source/output contracts without invoking the compiler.

Vision outcome protected: keeping validation and derivation independently meaningful, so invalid source cannot produce trusted runtime output and validation remains usable on its own.

Derivation produces an artifact from authored source. Validation evaluates whether source or output satisfies a contract and returns a verdict. They have separate entry points, responsibilities, and independently callable lifecycles. Tests import the validator, not the derivation pipeline. A source change triggers validation and, after source validity is established, derivation; neither is a hidden prerequisite of the other.

Accomplishment: validation runs without generating output; derivation refuses invalid input; each has its own exit status and report; tests validate source/output contracts without invoking the compiler.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — keeping validation and derivation independently meaningful, so invalid source cannot produce trusted runtime output and validation remains usable on its own.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: keeping validation and derivation independently meaningful, so invalid source cannot produce trusted runtime output and validation remains usable on its own.

Implementation freedom — The tools and pipeline may change, but producing an artifact and determining whether it is valid remain distinct responsibilities. One process may orchestrate both without making derivation itself the proof of validity.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that keeping validation and derivation independently meaningful, so invalid source cannot produce trusted runtime output and validation remains usable on its own fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-SYSTEM-OPAQUERuleAuthoring Readability and Runtime Readability Are Separate Goals

In plain English: source is edited through the authoring workflow; runtime consumes generated representation; every runtime field derives from authored source or is rejected as unsupported.

Vision outcome protected: source is edited through the authoring workflow; runtime consumes generated representation; every runtime field derives from authored source or is rejected as unsupported.

The authored SSOT is optimized for people curating business knowledge; the derived artifact is optimized for unambiguous machine consumption. Do not force one representation to optimize both. Human-facing labels and editorial notes may exist in source, but machine references use IDs and schema-defined structures. Runtime efficiency comes from derivation, not from turning source into a second runtime index.

Accomplishment: source is edited through the authoring workflow; runtime consumes generated representation; every runtime field derives from authored source or is rejected as unsupported.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — source is edited through the authoring workflow; runtime consumes generated representation; every runtime field derives from authored source or is rejected as unsupported.

The specific commitment at risk is: source is edited through the authoring workflow; runtime consumes generated representation; every runtime field derives from authored source or is rejected as unsupported.

Implementation freedom — Authoring and runtime representations may differ when each serves its audience, provided derivation is traceable, validation is independent, and neither representation becomes a competing authority.

Vision outcome protected: each generated index names its consumer in the derivation manifest; consumer removal identifies unused fields; no index remains solely through historical inertia.

Every generated index or projection identifies a real runtime or authoring consumer. Do not compute, ship, and maintain a structure merely because it existed before. When its last consumer is removed, remove the generated structure and generation path unless an approved, time-bounded migration names a real consumer.

The Charter states durable direction and Rules; the SSOT contains authored business knowledge; the schema defines valid shape; technical specifications define contracts; the timeline records history; pending decisions list unresolved choices; tests and traces provide evidence. A field or shape used by a consumer becomes a contract whether or not its author intended it. Published interfaces have an owning schema/version and compatibility policy. In plain English: A check must be either a release gate or an observation, with its role and consequence made explicit.

Vision outcome protected: A check must be either a release gate or an observation, with its role and consequence made explicit.

Every recurring diagnostic declares whether it blocks a change or merely reports information. A non-gating diagnostic is not described as enforcement or compliance. A gate states its failure condition, owner, and resolution path; an observation must inform a named decision or be retired.

Accomplishment: CI distinguishes blocking failures from observations; gates fail with nonzero status for declared violations; recurring observations have an owner and decision they inform.

R-SYSTEM-UNFUNCTIONRuleFunctions Preserve One Cohesive Responsibility

Vision outcome protected: Each function has a cohesive responsibility so changing one behavior does not unintentionally alter unrelated customer behavior.

A function must not own unrelated stages merely because they are convenient to place together. Resolution, pricing-shape selection, modifier application, labor calculation, fee application, checkout-state creation, DOM rendering, and persistence are distinct responsibilities. Split functions that independently own several of these stages along stable input/output boundaries. Length is a warning signal, not a substitute for identifying responsibility.

Accomplishment: each core function has one named responsibility and input/output contract; pipeline stages can be tested at their own boundary; a change to one responsibility does not require edits to unrelated calculations or rendering.

R-SYSTEM-GATEBYPASSRuleA Gate Cannot Be Silently Bypassed by Its Caller

In plain English: each gate has one decision point; behavioral tests cover allowed, denied, and authorized exception paths; no caller-side bypass changes the result outside that authority.

Vision outcome protected: ensuring a caller cannot evade a safety or correctness gate and proceed with an unverified quote or booking.

A mechanism authoritative for whether a path, fee, module, or checkout state is allowed cannot be skipped through a parallel override or fallback. An exception is permitted only as a separately defined policy path resolved through the same authority. Caller-side switches must not silently nullify the decision the gate exists to enforce.

Accomplishment: each gate has one decision point; behavioral tests cover allowed, denied, and authorized exception paths; no caller-side bypass changes the result outside that authority.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — ensuring a caller cannot evade a safety or correctness gate and proceed with an unverified quote or booking.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring a caller cannot evade a safety or correctness gate and proceed with an unverified quote or booking.

Implementation freedom — Call structure may change, but callers must not silently bypass a gate whose result protects a consequential decision. An intentional alternative path must preserve the gate’s purpose and be governed explicitly.

R-SYSTEM-OBSERVABILITYRuleProduct Decisions Use Evidence Appropriate to Their Consequence

In plain English: Use evidence proportionate to the consequence of the decision.

Vision outcome protected: Use evidence proportionate to the consequence of the decision.

Decisions about question burden, confidence thresholds, conversion friction, and customer outcomes distinguish measured evidence from architectural reasoning. Use privacy-appropriate customer telemetry when available. When it is not, state the limitation and use bounded traces, representative fixtures, or usability observation as proxies; never present proxies as real-world measurements or collect personal data merely to make a metric available.

R-SYSTEM-UNVERIFIABLE-CLAIMRuleClaims of Verification Must Name Evidence

In plain English: verification claims have traceable evidence references; skipped checks are reported as skipped; readers can distinguish observed results from inference.

Vision outcome protected: verification claims have traceable evidence references; skipped checks are reported as skipped; readers can distinguish observed results from inference.

A comment, report, or change summary must not say 'tested', 'verified', or 'confirmed' unless it names the check, command, runtime observation, or evidence artifact. Reasoning may be stated as inference but not as direct observation. A failed or skipped check is not verification.

Reading the evidence is part of verifying. A claim that cites its check is verified only if the check’s actual output was read — not the check’s pass/fail line, not a partial summary. An archetype-label count reported from a partial experiment is the same defect as an uncited claim.

Accomplishment: verification claims have traceable evidence references; skipped checks are reported as skipped; readers can distinguish observed results from inference.

R-SYSTEM-UNBOUNDEDRuleSelf-Reference Must Not Make an Artifact Unbounded

Vision outcome protected: scans identify self-referential hash/splice patterns; each is removed or uses a documented canonical fixed-point algorithm; repeated generation remains stable.

No generated artifact may use its own mutable contents as an input to a value written back into those contents. Move integrity values to a sidecar, exclude them through documented canonicalization, or remove the self-reference. Do not solve each occurrence with a bespoke line-shape or hash patch.

R-SYSTEM-SCRIPT-SCOPE-IS-REALRuleExecution Scope Reflects the Chosen Deployment Boundary

In plain English: the assembled page parses and boots as deployed; a sensitive scope/duplicate-declaration defect makes verification fail; tests do not mistake extraction semantics for browser semantics.

Execution and isolation assumptions must match the actual chosen deployment and packaging architecture. Verification must exercise the same relevant boundaries that govern the deployed artifact; it must not depend on a test-only reconstruction that changes those boundaries.

Execution and isolation rules must match the chosen deployment and packaging architecture. When those boundaries change, obligations that existed only for the former arrangement must be reassessed and retired where no longer needed. Verification must exercise the same relevant execution model that governs real use, not a test-only reconstruction with different semantics.

Accomplishment: verification exercises the chosen deployment model; an execution-scope or duplicate-declaration defect relevant to that model makes verification fail; and the verification harness does not introduce semantics absent from deployment.

Rule health & evolvability

Execution and isolation assumptions must match the actual chosen deployment and packaging architecture. Verification must exercise the same relevant boundaries that govern the deployed artifact; it must not depend on a test-only reconstruction that changes those boundaries.

Harm prevented — A verification harness can pass while the deployed artifact behaves differently because the harness changes script scope, isolation, loading order, or duplicate-declaration behavior.

Implementation freedom — Packaging and script boundaries may change with deployment architecture. Scope rules and verification must reflect the actual chosen delivery constraint rather than preserve assumptions from an earlier packaging model.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that the deployment or packaging boundary changes, or a counterexample shows that the verification harness and deployed artifact execute under materially different semantics. Engineering Invariants

These are the rules that should survive implementation changes. The section name is a naming convention for foundational engineering rules — it does not introduce a second status tier.

Invariants are not a second policy hierarchy. An invariant is a foundational constraint, mathematical property, physical limit, or architectural truth recognized by the system. It may be discovered rather than authored. When the project deliberately declares that constraint as a requirement — "must" or "must not" — it is a Rule. There is no separate invariant status, registry, promotion process, or enforcement tier.

R-INVARIANT-SOURCERuleOne Source of Truth

In plain English: Every runtime business fact traces to one authoritative authored source; removing or changing that source cannot leave an unowned competing copy.

Historical evidence: Battle 3 — Derived data has a different lifecycle than its source

Vision outcome protected: Every runtime business fact traces to one authoritative authored source; removing or changing that source cannot leave an unowned competing copy.

Accomplishment: Every runtime business fact traces to one authoritative authored source; removing or changing that source cannot leave an unowned competing copy.

Serves:P-SYSTEM-CLASSIFY-ONE · P-INVARIANT-VERIFY

Business knowledge belongs in the SSOT.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Every runtime business fact traces to one authoritative authored source; removing or changing that source cannot leave an unowned competing copy.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: every runtime business fact traces to one authoritative authored source; removing or changing that source cannot leave an unowned competing copy.

Implementation freedom — The physical source format and storage location may change. Each kind of fact must have one authoritative source and a traceable derivation path.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every runtime business fact traces to one authoritative authored source; removing or changing that source cannot leave an unowned competing copy fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-INVARIANT-CANONICALRuleOne Canonical Implementation per Behavior

In plain English: One canonical implementation decides each behavior; callers must not quietly re-decide it.

Historical evidence: Battle 2 — Two systems that answer the same real question will eventually disagree

Vision outcome protected: One canonical implementation decides each behavior; callers must not quietly re-decide it.

Accomplishment: Equivalent inputs routed through different callers produce the same canonical resolution; caller-side fallback or re-derivation cannot override the resolver silently.

Serves:P-INVARIANT-NOPATCH · P-INVARIANT-RENDERERS

If two functions answer the same real question differently, one is eventually going to drift. A canonical resolver is only canonical if it is the last step. A caller-side OR, whitelist, or re-derivation on top of a canonical resolver is a second implementation, regardless of whether the resolver itself was duplicated. Extract shared logic; make other callers delegate.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — One canonical implementation decides each behavior; callers must not quietly re-decide it.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: one canonical implementation decides each behavior; callers must not quietly re-decide it.

Implementation freedom — Implementation boundaries may be redesigned. Equivalent behavior must not drift among independent implementations; shared behavior needs one canonical owner or an explicitly governed transition.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that one canonical implementation decides each behavior; callers must not quietly re-decide it fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-INVARIANT-PROVENANCERuleResolution Provenance Travels on the Return Value

In plain English: Every resolver result carries its selected source/branch; downstream consumers use that provenance rather than reconstructing the decision from field presence.

Historical evidence: Battle 2 — Two systems that answer the same real question will eventually disagree

Vision outcome protected: Every resolver result carries its selected source/branch; downstream consumers use that provenance rather than reconstructing the decision from field presence.

Accomplishment: Every resolver result carries its selected source/branch; downstream consumers use that provenance rather than reconstructing the decision from field presence.

Serves:P-INVARIANT-VERIFY · P-INVARIANT-PROPAGATE

A resolution's source travels on the return value. A function that resolves a concept returns a record carrying both the value and which branch supplied it (source: 'service_override' | 'archetype_default' | 'dynamic_engine' | 'fallback' | 'answer_override'; answer_override names the case where the customer's own answer superseded the resolved value, and is set where that answer is applied, so a resolver whose branches do not include one never returns it). Callers read the resolved value from the resolver's return value. A caller that composes a resolver's return with a second source — a || chain, an ?? chain, a whitelist that selects between pre-computed candidates, or an inline re-derivation of the same concept — has reintroduced arbitration outside the resolver, and that is the defect this rule prohibits. A "plausible number" that cannot be traced to a named source on its own return value is a defect, not a success.

Why one rule does the work of three. It forces resolvers to declare their choice, and it forbids the caller-side composition layer that has been the actual source of divergence. Because provenance is carried in the return value rather than reconstructed by callers, there is exactly one place a resolution's origin can be read from — the resolver's own output — and no caller has the information needed to re-derive it. See Named Defect Classes — Arbitration.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Every resolver result carries its selected source/branch; downstream consumers use that provenance rather than reconstructing the decision from field presence.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: every resolver result carries its selected source/branch; downstream consumers use that provenance rather than reconstructing the decision from field presence.

Implementation freedom — The data structure carrying provenance may change. Decisions must retain enough origin and resolution information for downstream consumers to explain and verify the result.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every resolver result carries its selected source/branch; downstream consumers use that provenance rather than reconstructing the decision from field presence fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

R-INVARIANT-DUPLICATION-TICKETRuleTemporary Duplicate Implementations Are Controlled Migrations

In plain English: Every temporary duplicate implementation has a named canonical replacement, reason for coexistence, completion detector, and deletion condition; no duplicate becomes independently authoritative.

Historical evidence: Battle 2 — Two systems that answer the same real question will eventually disagree

Vision outcome protected: Every temporary duplicate implementation has a named canonical replacement, reason for coexistence, completion detector, and deletion condition; no duplicate becomes independently authoritative.

Accomplishment: Every temporary duplicate implementation has a named canonical replacement, reason for coexistence, completion detector, and deletion condition; no duplicate becomes independently authoritative.

Serves:P-INVARIANT-NOPATCH · R-INVARIANT-DONE

A second implementation of the same behavior is prohibited except as a declared migration artifact. The migration record identifies the canonical replacement, why temporary coexistence is necessary, and the condition for deletion. The duplicate may not become an independent source of business behavior. If a later change touches the duplicate, the change must either remove the duplicate or explicitly preserve the migration plan; it may not silently make both implementations authoritative.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every temporary duplicate implementation has a named canonical replacement, reason for coexistence, completion detector, and deletion condition; no duplicate becomes independently authoritative fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. R-INVARIANT-DELETIONRuleA Retirement Is Deleted, Not Deprecated

In plain English: A declared retirement removes the retired implementation and references, or explicitly records the remaining dependency as an open migration rather than claiming completion.

Vision outcome protected: A declared retirement removes the retired implementation and references, or explicitly records the remaining dependency as an open migration rather than claiming completion.

Accomplishment: A declared retirement removes the retired implementation and references, or explicitly records the remaining dependency as an open migration rather than claiming completion.

Serves:P-INVARIANT-NOLEGACY

A retirement is complete when the retired artifact is deleted from source control. Deprecated-but-present is not a state. Deprecated-but-still-called is a defect. Any declaration of completion for an architectural migration must cite the specific removal, or it does not use the word "complete."

Compliance before deletion. When existing code is non-compliant but still load-bearing, the first response is to attempt to adopt or adapt that code to the Charter's compliant architecture. If no compliant substitute or comparable implementation yet exists, the load-bearing code should be rewritten toward compliance rather than reflexively deleted. Deletion becomes the next step when the code has been successfully superseded, when a compliant replacement exists, or when a deliberate assessment establishes that retaining it cannot be justified. The point is to retire non-compliant architecture deliberately, not to make the metric look clean by deleting the mechanism before its consequences are understood.

This rule exists because prior sessions declared an architectural migration complete while the code remained present. It also prevents the opposite failure: treating deletion itself as progress when the deleted code still carries necessary behavior. A completion claim must account for both sides — compliance or a verified replacement, and actual removal of the retired artifact.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — A declared retirement removes the retired implementation and references, or explicitly records the remaining dependency as an open migration rather than claiming completion.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: a declared retirement removes the retired implementation and references, or explicitly records the remaining dependency as an open migration rather than claiming completion.

Implementation freedom — The retirement mechanism may vary by artifact type. Once a behavior or representation is deliberately retired, active paths and documentation must not continue to preserve it as an undocumented fallback.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that a declared retirement removes the retired implementation and references, or explicitly records the remaining dependency as an open migration rather than claiming completion fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

P-INVARIANT-NOPATCHPrincipleNo Path-Specific Patching

Vision outcome protected: correcting the shared cause of a defect instead of making one customer path appear correct while sibling paths remain wrong.

Historical evidence: Battle 7 — Wiring an existing mechanism is not the same as building new logic

If a behavior is correct, it belongs in the canonical resolution architecture. A fix that exists only on one entry path is a temporary symptom fix, not the finished behavior.

Governance implication. No fix may be applied as a path-specific patch to parallel entry modes. Any correct behavior discovered in a transitional path must be incorporated into the canonical pipeline and verified as equivalent across all supported entry modes before that transitional path is retired.

R-INVARIANT-DONERuleDefinition of Done

In plain English: A change is declared complete only after intended behavior, relevant verification, dependent derived output, and obsolete-behavior retirement are each dispositioned in the change record.

Vision outcome protected: A change is declared complete only after intended behavior, relevant verification, dependent derived output, and obsolete-behavior retirement are each dispositioned in the change record.

Accomplishment: A change is declared complete only after intended behavior, relevant verification, dependent derived output, and obsolete-behavior retirement are each dispositioned in the change record.

A change is complete only when: the intended behavior is present; the complete relevant set has been verified to the extent relevant to the change; every dependent derived representation is synchronized; obsolete behavior or representations replaced by the change are retired or superseded; and verification is proportionate to the defect class. Regression protection may be a class detector, an existing authoritative contract test, or documented one-off evidence when no permanent test is justified. Passing a test suite alone never establishes completion.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — A change is declared complete only after intended behavior, relevant verification, dependent derived output, and obsolete-behavior retirement are each dispositioned in the change record.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: a change is declared complete only after intended behavior, relevant verification, dependent derived output, and obsolete-behavior retirement are each dispositioned in the change record.
