Regression Prevention Discipline

Regression protection protects intent, not history. A verification artifact has no authority over the behavior it was created to observe. A regression artifact exists to prevent recurrence of incorrect behavior or to protect a current authoritative contract. It does not grant permanent authority to the implementation, architecture, data shape, or historical behavior that originally produced it. When the authoritative model or intended behavior legitimately changes, affected tests are re-evaluated and may be rewritten, migrated, subsumed, or retired.

The Charter previously required a permanent regression test for every fix. That produced hundreds of instance tests and made the codebase fragile. The replacement is to protect recurring defect classes and authoritative intent without freezing historical implementation details.

Verification must never become self-reinforcing authority. A test that protects deprecated behavior, obsolete architecture, historical implementation shape, or a superseded decision must be rewritten or retired even if it is green. The number of passing tests is not a quality target.
R-INVARIANT-DISEASERuleEvery fix receives regression protection appropriate to its defect class. A recurring defect extends an existing class detector or creates a new one; a genuine one-off is documented without a permanent test. A detector protects the defect class, not the implementation that happened to fix one instance.

Vision outcome protected: Recurring failures become detectable defect classes so the same customer-facing failure does not return under a new implementation.

Accomplishment: Each fix is dispositioned as an extension to an existing class detector, a newly named recurring class with a detector target, or a bounded one-off with a stated reason; no fix automatically creates a permanent one-test-per-bug obligation.

In plain English: Each fix is dispositioned as an extension to an existing class detector, a newly named recurring class with a detector target, or a bounded one-off with a stated reason; no fix automatically creates a permanent one-test-per-bug obligation.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Recurring failures become detectable defect classes so the same customer-facing failure does not return under a new implementation.

Harm prevented — A customer can receive different interpretations, explanations, or next steps for the same request depending on how they expressed it. The specific commitment at risk is: recurring failures become detectable defect classes so the same customer-facing failure does not return under a new implementation.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that recurring failures become detectable defect classes so the same customer-facing failure does not return under a new implementation fails under a changed or newly supported entry mode, evidence format, or customer behavior, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

There are two kinds of regression artifacts, and they have different lifecycles:

KindWhat it guardsHow many existWhen it changes
Class detectorA general shape — "no per-visit fee is ever multiplied by quantity," "no second parser exists," "no resolver return is composed with a second source at the call site"One per named defect class; the live count belongs in the regression ledgerWhen the class's signature sharpens, or when the underlying mechanism changes enough that the detector's own premise changes
One-off evidenceA specific instance used to diagnose or document a defectTemporary or ledger-onlyRetained only when it has independent value; it is not automatically promoted to a permanent regression test

The class detector is the disease cure. The one-off assertion is the symptom treatment. The prior rule mandated the second for every instance of the first, without distinguishing the two.

How a fix is dispositioned

A defect becomes a named class only when the recurrence pattern is broader than the individual instance and a detector can distinguish that pattern from legitimate behavior. Similarity alone is not sufficient reason to create a permanent class.
  1. Recognize the class. When a bug is diagnosed, the first question is which named defect class it is an instance of. The named classes are in Part XI — Rules, Classes & Battles.
  2. If it is an instance of a named class, the detector for that class is extended — never a new one-off test. The class's own detector file grows.
  3. If it is an instance of no named class, and the shape is likely to recur, a new class is named and its detector is created. This is the mechanism by which the class list grows.
  4. If it is a genuine one-off — a data error, a typo, a specific config mistake — it is documented in the owning ledger entry and produces no permanent test. Its absence from the test suite is correct, not a gap.
The number of tests grows with the number of real classes, not with the number of bugs. A codebase with eight well-chosen class detectors is more robust than one with eight hundred instance assertions, and it is orders of magnitude less fragile.

Test retirement

G-INVARIANT-RETIRE-TESTGovernance ProcessRetire a test when it is subsumed by a broader class detector, guards retired behavior, or is over-fit to an instance the current contract no longer requires.

Test retirement is an affirmative act, not a violation. The discipline parallels R-INVARIANT-DELETION: a retirement is deleted, not deprecated. The suite's growth is bounded by the number of real classes, not by the number of changes the project has ever made.

What remains true

G-INVARIANT-PREFIXGovernance ProcessWhen a new class detector is created, run it against the pre-fix code first. A class detector that passes both before and after the fix it was written for is not yet detecting the class — it may be detecting a symptom of the class. This is a discipline, not a mechanically enforceable rule.

R-INVARIANT-REDTESTRuleA red test is either fixed within one working session or formally retired with a written reason in the test's own header.In plain English: The meta-test asserts no test file is marked as a persistent failure across more than one session's ledger entries. The meta-test asserts no test file is marked as a persistent failure across more than one session's ledger entries. "It's a pre-existing failure" and "it doesn't block anything customer-facing" are not valid dispositions.
Accomplishment: A failing test is fixed within one working session or carries a written retirement reason in its own header; a check detects unclassified red tests.

Vision outcome protected: Known failing checks are resolved or deliberately retired so ignored failures cannot mask new regressions.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Known failing checks are resolved or deliberately retired so ignored failures cannot mask new regressions.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: known failing checks are resolved or deliberately retired so ignored failures cannot mask new regressions.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that known failing checks are resolved or deliberately retired so ignored failures cannot mask new regressions fails under a new question type, answer state, uncertainty class, or evidence source, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

G-INVARIANT-SWEEPGovernance ProcessWhen a class is discovered, sweep every sibling mechanism the class could reach before declaring the defect contained. The class detector is a consequence of the sweep; the sweep is the act. Detectors are what make future sweeps unnecessary.

R-INVARIANT-SINGLEDEFRuleAny list, constant, or registry used by more than one function is defined once.In plain English: A shared constant used by more than one function is a single, module-level definition, with a test asserting no second copy exists. A shared constant used by more than one function is a single, module-level definition, with a test asserting no second copy exists.
Accomplishment: Shared lists, constants, and registries have one authoritative definition; static duplicate detection fails when a second definition is introduced.

Vision outcome protected: Shared facts have one authoritative definition so related calculations and decisions cannot drift apart.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Shared facts have one authoritative definition so related calculations and decisions cannot drift apart.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: shared facts have one authoritative definition so related calculations and decisions cannot drift apart.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that shared facts have one authoritative definition so related calculations and decisions cannot drift apart fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

The former combined rerun obligation is retired. SSOT changes trigger validation under R-INVARIANT-DERIVATION, and valid source changes trigger derivation as a separate act. No single compiler entry point may be treated as both tools.

R-INVARIANT-BEHAVIORALRuleBehavioral Rules Require Behavioral Protection

In plain English: Every Rule claiming cross-component/runtime behavior has a test that exercises that behavior through the relevant boundary and demonstrably fails for a counterexample.

Vision outcome protected: ensuring behavioral commitments are defended by evidence that would fail when the real behavior is wrong, not by a convenient proxy.

Accomplishment: Every Rule claiming cross-component/runtime behavior has a test that exercises that behavior through the relevant boundary and demonstrably fails for a counterexample.

Serves:R-INVARIANT-DONE · P-INVARIANT-VERIFY

A Rule that governs observable behavior must have at least one behavioral or end-to-end mechanism capable of exercising that behavior through its relevant boundary. Unit coverage may still be useful and may be sufficient for a purely structural or local Rule, but unit-only coverage does not establish behavioral protection for a Rule whose claim is about system behavior across components, gateways, or runtime state.

Coverage records describe mechanisms only. State which behavior is exercised, at which boundary, and what counterfactual makes the check fail. Do not use a coverage label as a normative status or as a substitute for the Rule’s accomplishment condition.

The Pre-Change Checklist

Before modifying a significant part of the system, in order:

  1. Run the full test suite. Know the current pass state before touching anything.
  2. Search the ledger. Check the historical record and the decision ledger — has it been fixed before? Deliberately deferred? Superseded?
  3. Check the component map. Before touching anything that looks like legacy code, read the maintained component-responsibility map.
  4. Run a real validator if a comment claims one was run. The schema is not self-enforcing.
  5. Sweep the full catalog for any change affecting routing or pricing.
  6. After the change: re-run the suite, re-sweep the catalog, and update the ledger.

Vision outcome — ensuring behavioral commitments are defended by evidence that would fail when the real behavior is wrong, not by a convenient proxy.

Harm prevented — The customer may be asked for information that does not change the decision, prevented from expressing an unknown or unlisted case, or given a confident result while material uncertainty remains. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring behavioral commitments are defended by evidence that would fail when the real behavior is wrong, not by a convenient proxy.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Inspect the relevant responsibility boundary for structural claims; use a counterexample capable of exposing the claimed failure for behavioral claims; use end-to-end evidence when multiple responsibilities must agree. Tests inform judgment but do not define the Vision.

Part IX Pricing & Booking Outcome
The pricing pipeline and the honest expression of its result.
A finite set of pricing methods. One reproducible pipeline. Coefficients belong in the SSOT.

The Pricing Pipeline

Regardless of the particular pricing shape, the durable pipeline is: resolve the applicable method, apply the scoped inputs, calculate the price, apply explicit per-visit fees and materials according to their declared scope, derive the client-facing checkout state, and expose the same result to the client and booking flow.

R-PRICE-PRECISIONRulePricing May Not Claim More Precision Than Resolution Permits

In plain English: Never show a more exact price than the resolved scope and evidence support.

Vision outcome protected: Never show a more exact price than the resolved scope and evidence support.

Accomplishment: For every quote, the presented exact amount, range, condition, or diagnostic is no more precise than the active resolved evidence supports; an unsupported exact amount is rejected.

Serves:P-CONF-PRECISION · P-WHITEFLAG-PRECISE

The pricing engine may calculate a candidate amount, range, or diagnostic fee, but the presented outcome must not claim a level of precision the active resolution state does not justify. Exact quote, bounded range, conditional quote, and diagnostic pricing are distinct reproducible outcomes.

Precision is selected after the current uncertainty/evidence state and policy threshold are known. A low Cₓ does not mean "no price"; it means the system must use the most precise permitted priced path. A high Cₓ does not override insufficient Cᵢ, routing safety, regulatory restrictions, or a diagnostic-only checkout state. Numeric precision bands belong to the owning policy/specification; this Charter does not invent universal gap values such as 20 or 40 merely to make the model appear complete.

The mechanical test constructs resolution fixtures at each supported precision boundary, runs the pricing engine, and asserts that the output representation does not exceed the precision allowed by the resolved state. The test also asserts that a diagnostic or project-based checkout state cannot be relabeled as an exact quote merely because Cₓ is high.

Resolved scope
↓
Pricing shape / method
↓
Scoped quantities + modifiers
↓
Labor / base calculation
↓
Per-visit fees + materials + surcharges
↓
Checkout state / explanation
↓
Bookable price

Checkout state is presentation/output. It communicates the justified precision of the pricing result; it does not calculate the price and is not itself a pricing shape.

  • Scope is explicit. Per-unit and per-visit effects must never be conflated.
  • Pricing is reproducible. The same resolved scope and inputs produce the same result.
  • The UI does not invent prices. Rendering consumes the resolved pricing result.
  • Preview and finalization converge. The number shown during scoping must come from the same pricing path used when the job is committed.

Exact formula stages, field names, and coefficients are implementation specification, not charter doctrine. They belong in the SSOT, schema, and owning engineering documentation.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Never show a more exact price than the resolved scope and evidence support.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: never show a more exact price than the resolved scope and evidence support.

Implementation freedom — Pricing formulas, numeric thresholds, and presentation formats may evolve in their owning policy/specification. The invariant is that the customer-facing precision never exceeds the evidence supporting the resolved scope.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that never show a more exact price than the resolved scope and evidence support fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

The Pricing Shape Registry

Pricing is not one universal equation with arbitrary switches. BTNYC uses a finite set of reproducible pricing methods, each suited to a different physical reality of work. The active registry and its exact names belong to the SSOT and its schema; this charter defines the rule that every price must resolve through an explicit, registered method.

R-PRICE-REGISTEREDA new pricing method is an engine change, not a new data value. A new coefficient, modifier, fee, quantity band, or service mapping should normally be data. A genuinely new formula shape requires an explicit implementation change, schema/validator coverage, and regression protection.

Vision outcome protected: Every pricing method invoked by authored SSOT data is registered in the engine and schema; an unknown method is rejected rather than silently approximated.

Accomplishment: Every pricing method invoked by authored SSOT data is registered in the engine and schema; an unknown method is rejected rather than silently approximated.

In plain English: Every pricing method invoked by authored SSOT data is registered in the engine and schema; an unknown method is rejected rather than silently approximated.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Every pricing method invoked by authored SSOT data is registered in the engine and schema; an unknown method is rejected rather than silently approximated.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: every pricing method invoked by authored ssot data is registered in the engine and schema; an unknown method is rejected rather than silently approximated.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every pricing method invoked by authored ssot data is registered in the engine and schema; an unknown method is rejected rather than silently approximated fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

The registered pricing-method set is intentionally small. The Charter names the shapes, not their coefficients:

ShapeMethod (conceptual)When it fires
flat_simpleA single, fixed base price with modifiers applied and quantity handled as an outer multiplierMost named services and flat-rate dynamic services
hourly_timedBase + extra fee + (minutes / 60) × tier rate, times quantity, plus per-visit feesServices whose real cost is time × rate
diagnostic_openThe diagnostic fee alone, credited toward repair if the customer proceedsServices where remote scope genuinely cannot be established
formulaDelegates to a named, registered entry in pricing_formulas for its complete calculationServices whose pricing shape is genuinely distinct (PAX wardrobe; hardware install; tile repair)
tiered_per_unitA real, two-tier model: a flat price covers the common range, then continuous per-unit math beyond itHigh-volume, low-per-unit-time work

The detailed live registry belongs with the authored source, the authoritative schema, and the pricing implementation. Keeping the complete list out of this charter prevents a routine pricing-data change from making the Charter stale.

Diagnostic pricing status: "diagnostic" is a recognized pricing shape in this architecture, but the live diagnostic service names, prices, eligibility rules, and any credit policy remain registered in the SSOT. The charter does not invent a particular diagnostic service or coefficient.

The Formula Shape

Pricing is a reproducible, data-driven method — not a rigid flat rate, and not a catalogue of numbers.

Price = max(base_labor, (base_minutes + Σ(quantity × per_unit_minutes) + Σ(modifier_minutes)) × hourly_rate) + flat_fees + materials + surcharges

The Three Components

  1. Base labor — irreducible effort: arrival, setup, safety, minimum billable work.
  2. Quantity-per-unit labor — a per-piece increment that scales continuously. Never stepped. Stepping creates artificial price cliffs.
  3. Complexity modifiers — conditional adjustments tied to intake answers reflecting real, physical differences in work.

Per-Visit vs. Per-Unit

Some costs genuinely scale per unit. Others are one-time, per-visit costs regardless of how many units are worked on in the same trip.

R-PRICE-SCOPEThe rule: every fee-bearing modifier or surcharge carries an explicit scope: "per_unit" | "per_visit". A condition modifier that changes only complexity, minutes, branching, preparation, or another non-fee outcome does not need fee scope; it must declare its actual effects and must not be treated as a fee. Per-visit fees are added once, outside the quantity multiplier. Per-unit fees are multiplied inside it.

Vision outcome protected: Every fee-bearing modifier declares per_unit or per_visit scope; non-fee condition modifiers declare their actual effects instead. A quantity sweep proves per-unit fees scale with quantity while per-visit fees are applied once.

Accomplishment: Every fee-bearing modifier declares per_unit or per_visit scope; non-fee condition modifiers declare their actual effects instead. A quantity sweep proves per-unit fees scale with quantity while per-visit fees are applied once.

In plain English: Every fee-bearing modifier declares per_unit or per_visit scope; non-fee condition modifiers declare their actual effects instead. A quantity sweep proves per-unit fees scale with quantity while per-visit fees are applied once.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Every fee-bearing modifier declares per_unit or per_visit scope; non-fee condition modifiers declare their actual effects instead. A quantity sweep proves per-unit fees scale with quantity while per-visit fees are applied once.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: every fee-bearing modifier declares per_unit or per_visit scope; non-fee condition modifiers declare their actual effects instead. a quantity sweep proves per-unit fees scale with quantity while per-visit fees are applied once.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that every fee-bearing modifier declares per_unit or per_visit scope; non-fee condition modifiers declare their actual effects instead. a quantity sweep proves per-unit fees scale with quantity while per-visit fees are applied once fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

The failure mode this rule prevents: a single, one-time surcharge — an urgency fee, a parking fee, an access fee — being charged once per door because the whole fee sum was multiplied by quantity. The rule is not cosmetic; it is the difference between a quote that reflects one visit and a quote that reflects ten. When a modifier's scope is wrong, the error scales linearly with quantity and is invisible at qty=1.

High-Volume Overflow

When piece count exceeds the quantity covered by the service's baseline flat rate, the service's declared overflow modifier applies. The phrase roughly one hour of labor describes an illustrative baseline only; the actual baseline quantity/minutes and coefficient are configurable SSOT data, not a charter constant.

  • Additional units beyond baseline use the service's declared overflow modifier.
  • The exact coefficient belongs in the SSOT, not in this charter.

Never Over, Never Under

  • Overquote — the client walks. Worse: they may never tell us why.
  • Underquote — we lose margin, and we damage the trust the Ad-Lib confirmation was designed to build.

When uncertainty remains material, the system must express that uncertainty through an explicit range, conditional price, diagnostic path, or other declared pricing mechanism rather than silently assuming favorable conditions.

R-PRICE-REACHESEvery modifier the customer's answer produced must actually reach the price. A formula that consumes one answer does not thereby exempt the other answers from their own effects. The pricing engine excludes only the keys the formula genuinely consumes; every other answer's modifier still applies. The alternative — silently skipping every modifier whenever any formula is active — is a real, confirmed undercharging class and is prohibited.

Vision outcome protected: Each answer-derived modifier is traceable into the final price or an explicit non-price consequence; a planted unused modifier is detected.

Accomplishment: Each answer-derived modifier is traceable into the final price or an explicit non-price consequence; a planted unused modifier is detected.

In plain English: Each answer-derived modifier is traceable into the final price or an explicit non-price consequence; a planted unused modifier is detected.

Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — Each answer-derived modifier is traceable into the final price or an explicit non-price consequence; a planted unused modifier is detected.

Harm prevented — The quote can omit a cost, apply it at the wrong scope, merge requests that should remain distinct, or promise precision the evidence does not support. The specific commitment at risk is: each answer-derived modifier is traceable into the final price or an explicit non-price consequence; a planted unused modifier is detected.

Implementation freedom — The implementation may change its representation, algorithms, component boundaries, wording, and interaction sequence. The enduring requirement is the outcome stated above—not today’s filename, function, framework, or arrangement.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that each answer-derived modifier is traceable into the final price or an explicit non-price consequence; a planted unused modifier is detected fails under a new pricing method, cost component, quantity behavior, or policy threshold, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use counterexamples at the relevant decision boundaries. Verify that scope, evidence, classification, price representation, and next step agree, including missing or contradictory evidence; a passing happy path is insufficient.

R-PRICE-LINEIDENTITYA cart line’s identity includes every fact its price depends on. Two requests that price differently are never merged into one line. A line’s total is its amount times its quantity, computed in one place.

Vision outcome protected: Each priced line preserves every fact that can change its price, preventing merged requests from losing priced distinctions.

Accomplishment: Every fact that can change a quote is part of the line’s identity; a repeated request is one line with a quantity read by every total; two requests at different prices are two lines; a planted identity that omits a priced fact makes the check fail.

Historical evidence: Battle 18 — The cart is not presentation; it is a ledger

Part X Verification & Measurement
How tests prove a rule without turning the test into the goal.
The durable lessons extracted from the historical record.

Goodhart's Law and the Measurement Rule

The Charter's rules are meant to protect outcomes and architecture, not to create a game in which passing the test becomes the objective. When a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure. A test can therefore become actively harmful when developers optimize the source to satisfy the test's observable shape instead of satisfying the rule the test was intended to protect.

P-GOVERN-GOODHARTPrincipleMeasure the Rule by Its Own Mechanism

Vision outcome protected: ensuring tests and metrics measure the intended outcome instead of rewarding superficial compliance that can preserve a broken design.

Test behavior where the rule is behavioral. Test source where the rule is about source. Never invent a source-shape test for a behavioral rule.

Every Charter Rule should be validated according to the kind of claim it makes. The Rule itself defines the accomplishment; the test is only evidence that the accomplishment occurred in the population and mechanism the test actually observes. The validation mechanism must follow from the rule itself, not from whatever check is easiest to automate. A green test suite is evidence of compliance with the tested claims; it is not authority to preserve an obsolete architecture or behavior.

Rule categoryWhat the rule claimsCorrect validation mechanism
Behavioral“X does Y.”Drive the real interface and observe the real output. Do not require a particular internal implementation.
Interface contract“X exists and has shape Y.”Assert the interface exists and behaves at its boundary. Do not prescribe the internal algorithm.
Source-shape“No X exists in source” or equivalent source structure is itself the rule.Inspect the source. The source check is the rule's own mechanism.
Retired artifact“The retired artifact is absent.”Assert absence. Presence is not evidence of successful retirement.

A compliant test validates the rule using the rule's own mechanism. A distorting test validates the rule using an invented mechanism that is merely convenient to check. The difference matters because a green test can otherwise certify the wrong architecture.

This is especially important for architectural migrations. A test that requires a particular helper, function name, regex, data structure, or source pattern can turn an implementation detail into a hidden specification. If the Charter's actual rule is behavioral, a compliant refactor should remain free to change that implementation while preserving the observed behavior.

Source inspection is not inherently wrong. It is correct when the rule itself is about source shape — for example, absence of unsafe evaluation, absence of retired artifacts, or a deliberate layer boundary. The error is using source shape as a proxy for a behavioral outcome merely because source shape is easier to test.

Three named lessons reinforce this without becoming new enforcement tiers:Chesterton's Fence — understand load-bearing behavior before removing it; Lehman's Laws of Software Evolution — useful software must continue to evolve rather than become frozen by preservation; Hyrum's Law — observable behavior can acquire dependencies whether or not those dependencies were intended. These names are explanatory labels for lessons already embodied here, not additional Charter rules.

Goodhart's practical question: “If someone optimized solely to make this test pass, could they satisfy the test while violating the Charter rule?” If yes, the test is measuring the wrong thing or is missing the behavioral assertion that makes the measurement meaningful.
Rule health & evolvability

Vision outcome — ensuring tests and metrics measure the intended outcome instead of rewarding superficial compliance that can preserve a broken design.

Harm prevented — A defect can survive because the check measures a convenient proxy, misses relevant cases, or shares the same faulty assumption as the implementation it is meant to verify. The specific commitment at risk is: ensuring tests and metrics measure the intended outcome instead of rewarding superficial compliance that can preserve a broken design.

Implementation freedom — Metrics and verification techniques may evolve. No convenient proxy, passing test count, or checklist completion may replace evidence that the underlying outcome is achieved.

Reconsider when — Reconsider when evidence shows that ensuring tests and metrics measure the intended outcome instead of rewarding superficial compliance that can preserve a broken design fails under a counterexample that passes the claimed check or a changed architecture that makes the check observe the wrong boundary, or when a simpler responsibility boundary preserves the same outcome with fewer exceptions. Change the mechanism through governance; do not bypass it silently.

Evidence mechanism — Use representative customer paths and counterexamples that could falsify the claim. Verify that interpretation, evidence, decision, and customer-facing explanation remain consistent; a checklist or single happy path is insufficient.

Rule Directory

Every citable statement carries a stable code. Rules use R-<DOMAIN>-<NAME>; Governance Processes use G-<DOMAIN>-<NAME>; Principles use P-<DOMAIN>-<NAME>. The prefix identifies the category. A small set of historical governance processes retain their original R-GOVERN-* identifiers as stable legacy anchors. The directory below indexes canonical G-* Governance Process codes; legacy anchors remain resolvable in their owning sections and are not claimed to be rows in that table.

The directory is navigation, not a status ledger. A Rule remains binding whether automation is present, absent, incomplete, or under development. Verification evidence records what was actually checked; it does not create or alter the Rule. Related links are navigation only; they do not imply a required Rule–Principle pairing or a causal dependency.
